# Decision Log

**Append-only. Never edit or delete an existing entry.** If a decision is later
reversed, add a new entry that says so and references the original — don't
rewrite history.

This file is for judgment calls that aren't obvious from code and that a
future session (human or Claude) would otherwise have to rediscover or
re-argue. Not every decision needs an entry — routine implementation choices
belong in `session_update.md`. This file is for things where reasonable
people (or reasonable Claudes) could have gone the other way.

## Entry format

```
### YYYY-MM-DD — Short title
**Decided by:** Tanveer / Claude (chat) / Claude Code / joint
**Context:** why this came up
**Options considered:** brief list
**Decision:** what was chosen
**Rationale:** why
**Status:** active / superseded by [link to later entry]
```

---

### 2026-09-09 — Agent/SDK auth schemes stay permanently separate

**Decided by:** joint (confirmed independently by two prior build sessions)
**Context:** Agent registration uses raw-secret HMAC; SDK/API requests use full canonical-signature verification with replay protection. Audit flagged this as a possible unintentional duplication.
**Options considered:** unify onto one scheme; keep both.
**Decision:** Keep both, permanently.
**Rationale:** Agent registration is a one-time trusted-process handshake over a persistent connection — no replay risk. SDK/API calls are per-request over untrusted networks and need timestamp/replay protection. Unifying would require agents to know the HMAC pepper, which was deliberately kept off the agent.
**Status:** active

---

### 2026-09-09 — SDK client strategy: client.ts becomes primary, TunnelClient narrows

**Decided by:** joint (resolved a genuine unresolved disagreement between two prior build sessions)
**Context:** Two build sessions gave contradictory answers on whether the new HTTP/subdomain `client.ts` replaces the WebSocket-based `TunnelClient`, or whether they coexist permanently. Neither had actually settled it during the original build.
**Options considered:** full replacement with deprecation timeline; permanent coexistence with no direction; primary/secondary split.
**Decision:** `client.ts` (HTTP) becomes the default/primary SDK export. `TunnelClient` (WS) is narrowed to a secondary `@vhyxvoid/sdk/ws` export for the one legitimate remaining case — browser contexts wanting a persistent connection. No hard deprecation yet; no new features land on TunnelClient going forward.
**Status:** active — not yet actioned in code (export reorg + docs still pending as of this entry)

### 2026-09-09 — VhyxUI stays a separate repo, consumed via pnpm link:

**Decided by:** Tanveer
**Context:** Migrating VhyxVoid's frontend to VhyxUI (in-house component library, 0.3.1-alpha) raised the question of how the two repos should relate long-term — same monorepo, separate repo + registry publish, or separate repo + local linking.
**Options considered:** (1) keep separate, link via pnpm link:/file: for local dev; (2) pull VhyxUI's packages into VhyxVoid's monorepo as workspace packages; (3) publish VhyxUI to a private/local registry and consume as a normal versioned dependency.
**Decision:** Option 1 — separate repo, linked via pnpm link: for local dev.
**Rationale:** Keeps VhyxUI independently versioned and releasable as its own product, while VhyxVoid can consume live source during the migration/bug-finding phase without publish overhead per iteration.
**Status:** active — implemented 2026-09-09, linked via relative `link:../../../VhyxUI/packages/*` paths so the setup is portable across any contributor's machine as long as both repos are checked out as siblings

### 2026-09-09 — apps/web kept out of root TS project-reference graph

**Decided by:** Claude Code (during the monorepo-move session), ratified by inclusion here
**Context:** VhyxVoid's backend packages use `tsc -b` composite TypeScript project references. Whether to fold the new apps/web into that graph was ambiguous.
**Options considered:** add apps/web to the composite graph for consistency; keep it independent.
**Decision:** Keep apps/web independent, with its own standalone `tsc --noEmit` script wired into `turbo run typecheck` instead.
**Rationale:** Next.js manages its own compilation (Turbopack/SWC) using `noEmit` + `moduleResolution: Bundler`, which is incompatible with the composite/declaration/CommonJS setup the rest of the graph uses. Nothing else in the monorepo imports types from apps/web, so there's no cross-package benefit to forcing the join.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-09 — apps/web kept on its own ESLint config, not merged into root's flat config

**Decided by:** Claude Code (during the monorepo-move session), ratified by inclusion here
**Context:** Root uses ESLint 9 flat config; apps/web (from the standalone repo) uses ESLint 8. Whether to merge was ambiguous, and the original audit's description of the root rule turned out to be wrong once actually checked.
**Options considered:** migrate apps/web to ESLint 9 flat config now; keep it separate, matching the existing apps/hub precedent.
**Decision:** Keep apps/web on its own ESLint 8 config for now.
**Rationale:** Merging would require an ESLint 8→9 flat-config migration, itself currently blocked on an `eslint-config-next` version mismatch (pinned 15.1.2 vs Next 16.1.1 actually in use). Mirrors existing precedent — apps/hub already keeps its own separate legacy config rather than joining root's.
**Correction to earlier record:** context.md (as of the 2026-09-09 audit) described the root rule as enforced via `eslint-plugin-boundaries`. Reading `eslint.config.ts` directly during this session showed it's actually a plain `no-restricted-imports` rule — context.md needs correcting, not just this decision recorded.
**Status:** active. **Known tradeoff, flagged for revisit:** apps/web isn't covered by any cross-package import-boundary rule yet — matters once it starts importing `packages/shared` or other workspace packages directly.

### 2026-09-09 — Frontend migration sequencing: incremental, not big-bang

**Decided by:** joint, based on Claude (chat)'s gap-analysis recommendation
**Context:** VhyxUI is pre-1.0 (0.1–0.3-alpha across packages), missing several components VhyxVoid uses constantly (Typography, layout primitives, nav/menu, Avatar, Skeleton), and has no Storybook safety net.
**Options considered:** full cutover in one pass; incremental screen-by-screen migration with MUI and VhyxUI coexisting temporarily.
**Decision:** Incremental. Order: (0) foundation/token work, (1) 4 public marketing pages, (2) 7 auth-flow pages, (3) dashboard shell + nav chrome (shared infra, blocks everything after), (4) simple dashboard content pages, (5) list+CRUD screens on GenericServerTable (tunnels → api-keys → members, increasing interaction complexity), (6) admin-only screens last.
**Rationale:** Lets individual component regressions be verified in isolation rather than shipping the whole 19-page app onto unstable pre-1.0 ground at once. First 1-2 migrated screens are treated as a joint pilot with the VhyxUI team to surface real gaps before scaling to the remaining ~15 screens.
**Status:** active — Step 0 (monorepo move + link setup) completed 2026-09-09; component migration not yet started

### 2026-09-10 — VhyxUI brand override scoped via data-brand, not a global default overwrite

**Decided by:** Claude Code (during Step 0 foundation-work session), ratified by inclusion here
**Context:** Step 0 required porting VhyxVoid's actual palette/spacing/typography values into `@vhyxui/tokens`. The values could either replace VhyxUI's own default primitive/semantic tokens directly, or be added as an opt-in override layer.
**Options considered:** (1) overwrite VhyxUI's default `--vhyx-primitive-*`/`--vhyx-color-*` values with VhyxVoid's brand colors directly; (2) add a new additive theme file scoped under a `[data-brand="vhyxvoid"]` attribute selector, following the exact same pattern VhyxUI already uses for `[data-theme="dark"]`.
**Decision:** Option 2. Added `packages/tokens/src/themes/vhyxvoid.css` in the VhyxUI repo, wired into `sync-bundle.js` and `package.json`'s `exports` (new `./vhyxvoid.css` entry), regenerated the shipped `index.css`. Inert unless `data-brand="vhyxvoid"` is set on an ancestor element — VhyxUI's own docs/playground and any other consumer are unaffected.
**Rationale:** VhyxUI is a separately-versioned, independently-releasable product (per the 2026-09-09 "VhyxUI stays a separate repo" entry) — unilaterally overwriting its shared defaults with one consumer's brand identity would undermine that independence and silently rebrand VhyxUI's own docs/playground. The `[data-brand]` pattern costs nothing extra (VhyxUI already has the identical mechanism for dark mode) and requires zero change to VhyxVoid's existing `@vhyxui/tokens/index.css` import.
**Not yet done:** VhyxVoid's app doesn't set `data-brand="vhyxvoid"` anywhere yet — that's real integration work for a later migration step, not this one. The shims (see next entry) work around this via CSS `var()` fallbacks in the meantime.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-10 — Temporary Typography/Skeleton shims

**Decided by:** Claude Code (during Step 0 foundation-work session), ratified by inclusion here
**Context:** VhyxUI has no Typography or Skeleton component (confirmed MISSING in the migration gap-analysis report). Step 0 needed something for pages to use once component migration starts, without waiting on VhyxUI to ship either.
**Options considered:** wait for VhyxUI to add these before migrating any page; hand-roll temporary local shims now.
**Decision:** Hand-rolled minimal shims at `apps/web/src/components/vhyxui-shims/` (`Typography`, `Skeleton`), built on the tokens ported in the entry above, with CSS `var()` fallbacks so they work even before `data-brand` is wired up anywhere. Explicitly marked temporary in code comments and a directory README, with TODOs pointing back to this entry.
**Rationale:** These are cheap to build correctly and let Step 1 (marketing-page migration) proceed without blocking on VhyxUI's roadmap. The risk with any "temporary" shim is it quietly becomes permanent — mitigated here by the README's explicit rule against extending scope, and by this entry existing as a thing future sessions can check before adding to it.
**Status:** active — not yet imported into any real page (that's Step 1's job, not this session's)

### 2026-09-10 — CSS-reset conflict resolved: reset.css scoped per-route, not global

**Decided by:** Claude Code, closing the open item from the 2026-09-10 Step 0 session
**Context:** The Step 0 spike found `@vhyxui/tokens/reset.css`'s `body { background-color }` rule silently wins over MUI CssBaseline's own `body` background rule (same specificity, later in cascade) whenever both are active on the same route.
**Options considered:** (1) import `index.css` (tokens only, no reset) globally, import `reset.css` only in route groups confirmed to have no MUI `CssBaseline` mounted; (2) fork/rewrite VhyxUI's reset.css to lower its specificity or namespace it; (3) remove MUI's CssBaseline entirely now, before component migration is otherwise ready.
**Decision:** Option 1. `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx` imports `@vhyxui/tokens/index.css` globally (safe — variables only, no element-selector resets). `@vhyxui/tokens/reset.css` is imported only in `(public-pages)/layout.tsx`, the one route group confirmed (by grep, zero `@mui`/`CssBaseline` hits) to have no MUI theme mounted.
**Rationale:** Doesn't touch VhyxUI's own reset.css (a shared file other consumers rely on) and doesn't force removing MUI's CssBaseline before the app is ready for that. Scales naturally as more route groups migrate off MUI — each just adds its own `reset.css` import once its own CssBaseline is gone.
**Verification:** Empirically re-checked the same way the conflict was originally found — `getComputedStyle(document.body).backgroundColor` on `/login` (still MUI-themed) now returns `rgb(9, 9, 11)`, matching VhyxVoid's real dark `background.default` (`#09090B`), where it previously returned VhyxUI's light default (`rgb(248, 250, 252)`) before this fix.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-10 — data-brand + data-theme wiring (and a correction to the brief's assumption)

**Decided by:** Claude Code, closing the open item from the 2026-09-10 Step 0 session
**Context:** Task asked to wire `data-brand="vhyxvoid"` "alongside the existing data-theme mode-toggle mechanism." Investigation found no such mechanism exists — MUI's mode attribute here is actually `data-mui-color-scheme` (via `InitColorSchemeScript attribute='data'`), a different name than VhyxUI's own dark-mode selector (`[data-theme="dark"]`). Wiring only `data-brand` as literally asked would leave VhyxUI's dark-mode CSS permanently inert (no code anywhere sets `data-theme`), silently breaking the branded dark values ported in the earlier "VhyxUI brand override" entry.
**Options considered:** (1) wire `data-brand` only, exactly as asked, and separately flag the `data-theme` gap for the user to decide on; (2) wire both `data-brand` and a real `data-theme` attribute (mirroring MUI's resolved mode) in the same pass, since leaving VhyxUI's dark mode dead was clearly not the intent.
**Decision:** Option 2. `data-brand='vhyxvoid'` and `data-theme={systemMode}` are both set on `<html>` in the root layout (SSR-correct initial value from the same `getSystemMode()` call `InitColorSchemeScript` already uses). `ModeChanger.tsx` (the one place mode changes at runtime) now also calls `document.documentElement.setAttribute('data-theme', resolvedMode)` alongside its existing `setMode()` call, so live mode toggles on routes where it's mounted (dashboard, blank-layout-pages) keep both attributes in sync.
**Rationale:** This exceeds the literal ask, done transparently rather than silently — leaving `data-theme` unset would make half of Step 0's token-porting work (the dark-mode override block in `vhyxvoid.css`) permanently unreachable, which seemed clearly against intent rather than a real open question.
**Verification:** Confirmed via direct DOM inspection (`document.documentElement.getAttribute(...)`) on `/login`: `data-brand="vhyxvoid"`, `data-theme="dark"` both present; page screenshot confirmed visually identical to before (no VhyxUI component renders on this route yet, so nothing should or does change).
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-10 — @vhyxui/react/style.css imported globally in root layout

**Decided by:** Claude Code (during Step 1 marketing-page migration), ratified by inclusion here
**Context:** The first real VhyxUI component (`Button`, on the migrated landing page) rendered with correct DOM structure/classes/`data-variant` attributes but zero visual styling — investigation found `@vhyxui/react/style.css` (VhyxUI's bundled component CSS) had never been imported anywhere in the real app, only in prior throwaway spike pages that were since deleted.
**Options considered:** (1) import it globally in the root layout, alongside `@vhyxui/tokens/index.css`; (2) import it per-route, only where a VhyxUI component is actually used, mirroring the reset.css scoping decision above.
**Decision:** Option 1 — global import in `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx`. Verified first that this is safe: read `packages/react/dist/style.css` directly and confirmed zero `body`/`html`/`*` selectors (grep, 0 matches) — it's ~47KB of purely class-scoped CSS Modules output plus `@keyframes`, so unlike `reset.css` there's no same-specificity collision risk with MUI, and every future VhyxUI component (not just Button) will need this same file regardless of which route it lands on.
**Rationale:** Re-importing this per-route the way reset.css is scoped would be pure duplication with no safety benefit, since the actual risk (global-selector collision) that justified scoping reset.css doesn't apply here.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-10 — ToastProvider mounted directly, not via VhyxUIProvider (correction: this entry was referenced in code comments during the prior session's closeout but never actually written — written now, backdated to when the decision was made)

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 0 closeout session)
**Context:** VhyxUI's `toast()` needs a mounted `ToastProvider` to render anything — confirmed empirically (no console warning, no DOM node) when none was mounted. VhyxUI's own doc comment says "Place inside VhyxUIProvider. Developers do not need to add this directly," implying the intended top-level wrapper is the full `VhyxUIProvider` (which also bundles an a11y skip-link and the SealProvider agent-contract registry).
**Options considered:** (1) mount the full `VhyxUIProvider`; (2) mount `ToastProvider` directly, bypassing `VhyxUIProvider`.
**Decision:** Option 2. Confirmed `ToastProvider`'s implementation has zero dependency on `VhyxUIProvider`'s other subsystems (self-contained store subscription, no context requirement).
**Rationale:** Only toast rendering was needed for this migration step; pulling in the skip-link/SealProvider system pulls in unverified behavior for no current benefit. Revisit if a later step needs VhyxUIProvider's other pieces — at that point mounting the full provider (which should absorb ToastProvider) is probably cleaner than keeping them separate.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-10 — VhyxUIToastRegion: 'use client' boundary needed even though ToastProvider is already marked 'use client'

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 0 closeout session)
**Context:** Mounting `<ToastProvider>` directly from `layout.tsx` (a genuine async Server Component fetching `getSystemMode()`) produced a real Turbopack build error — "You're importing a component that needs useEffect... mark the file with 'use client'" — even though `ToastProvider`'s own source file has `'use client'` at the top. Root cause: Turbopack doesn't reliably see VhyxUI's `'use client'` directive through the cross-repo `link:` symlink into its bundled dist output (same class of friction as Button needing an explicit client boundary in Step 0/1's smoke test).
**Options considered:** (1) make layout.tsx itself a client component (not viable — it does genuine async server-side data fetching); (2) add a thin dedicated 'use client' wrapper component that layout.tsx renders instead of ToastProvider directly.
**Decision:** Option 2 — `apps/web/src/libs/components/VhyxUIToastRegion.tsx`, mirroring the exact pattern this app already uses for its own MUI `CustomThemeProvider` (an async Server Component — `Providers.tsx` — rendering a 'use client' child).
**Rationale:** Matches an established, working pattern already in the codebase rather than inventing a new one. Generalizes: any VhyxUI component consumed from a genuine Server Component in this app will likely need the same thin-wrapper treatment — check here before assuming a direct import will work.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-10 — blank-layout-pages keeps MUI CssBaseline/ThemeProvider active; reset.css NOT extended to this route group

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 2 session)
**Context:** Step 2's brief asked to verify whether `(blank-layout-pages)` (login/register/forgot-password/reset-password/verify-email x2/accept-invitation) could get the same `reset.css` treatment as `(public-pages)` in Step 1. Checked two things: (1) does this route group mount `Providers`/`CssBaseline`? Confirmed yes — `(blank-layout-pages)/layout.tsx` wraps children in `<Providers>`, unlike `(public-pages)/layout.tsx`'s explicit "No Providers" comment. (2) Is there a deeper Vuexy theme dependency beyond component usage? Confirmed yes — `Login.tsx` alone has 12 direct uses of `useTheme()`/`theme.spacing()`/`theme.breakpoints()`/`theme.direction` in its illustration/responsive-layout code (`LoginIllustration`, `MaskImg`, the `hidden` breakpoint check), all requiring a live MUI theme object from `ThemeProvider`. Every page in this group follows the same illustration-panel pattern.
**Options considered:** (1) extend reset.css to this group like `(public-pages)`; (2) leave CssBaseline/ThemeProvider active, migrate only the interactive form/UI components.
**Decision:** Option 2. `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(blank-layout-pages)/layout.tsx` is unchanged — no `reset.css` import added here. Only Button/Alert/TextField/Checkbox/Separator/Typography/toast/Spinner were migrated to VhyxUI on the 7 pages in this group; the illustration `styled()` components and `useTheme()`/`useMediaQuery()` calls were left on MUI, out of scope.
**Rationale:** Removing CssBaseline/ThemeProvider here would break the illustration/responsive-layout system structurally, not just visually — that's a bigger rewrite (effectively the "dashboard shell" class of work) than a component-migration pass. This is the correct application of the reset.css scoping rule established in Step 0/1: it's per-route-group, gated on actually having no CssBaseline, not gated on "did this migration touch this route."
**Status:** active — this route group is a confirmed, ongoing hybrid (real VhyxUI components + MUI CssBaseline/ThemeProvider coexisting) until a later, larger pass removes the MUI theme dependency entirely.

### 2026-09-10 — VhyxUI Form/react-hook-form generic typing friction resolved with explicit `any` casts, not a properly-typed workaround

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 2 session)
**Context:** VhyxUI's `<Form form={...}>` types its `form` prop as `UseFormReturn<FieldValues>` from react-hook-form. A strongly-typed `useForm<LoginFormValues>()` result doesn't satisfy this — and the mismatch turned out deeper than ordinary TypeScript generic variance: `tsc`'s error showed **two distinct `react-hook-form` type-declaration paths** — one under this repo's `node_modules`, one under VhyxUI's own separately-installed `node_modules` (confirmed: VhyxUI has its own `react-hook-form@7.76.0` for its own dev/build, vs. this repo's `7.87.0`). The same cross-repo duplicate-dependency class of issue as Step 0's dual-React-instance finding (see "VhyxUI stays a separate repo" entry), but surfacing as a compile-time nominal-type mismatch rather than a runtime one. A `children as unknown as UseFormReturn<FieldValues>` cast (importing that type from _this_ repo's `react-hook-form`) still failed, because the target type itself resolves to the wrong physical module.
**Options considered:** (1) find/construct a "properly typed" cast target — not possible, since no single type declaration satisfies both copies' structural expectations simultaneously; (2) cast to `any` at the two friction points (`form` prop, `onSubmit` prop) rather than fighting phantom type identity; (3) avoid `<Form>`'s context-based error reading entirely, fall back to plain `<form onSubmit={form.handleSubmit(...)}>` and explicit `error={errors.x?.message}` per field (closer to the original Controller-based pattern, more boilerplate, but zero cross-repo typing friction).
**Decision:** Option 2. Pattern (established in `Login.tsx`, reused verbatim in every other form page this session):

```ts
const untypedForm = form as any;
const handleFormSubmit = (data: any) => onSubmit(data as ActualFormValues);
// ... <Form form={untypedForm} onSubmit={handleFormSubmit}>
```

Fields use `{...form.register('fieldName')}` (RHF's own, correctly-typed `form`, not `untypedForm`) spread onto VhyxUI's `TextField`, with the `name` prop omitted (register already supplies it — passing both triggers a real `tsc` "specified more than once" error). No explicit `error` prop is passed on any field — `Field`'s automatic `getFieldState(name)` context read handles that.
**Rationale:** Option 3 was tempting for being typing-friction-free, but it discards the real value of VhyxUI's `Field` (automatic error display), duplicates code across the ~4+ form pages already migrated and however many remain, and doesn't actually eliminate risk — it just avoids typing a workaround explicitly. `any` here is narrowly scoped (two call sites per form) and honestly labeled as a workaround for a cross-repo dependency-duplication issue, not a general typing shortcut.
**Verified working correctly despite the cast**: functional browser testing on Login and Register confirmed required-field validation, yup format validation (email), and cross-field validation (`confirmPassword` must match `password`) all display the exact yup-schema error messages through Field's automatic error reading — the `any` cast doesn't paper over an actual behavioral gap, only a type-checking one.
**Status:** active — this is now the standing template for every remaining form page in later migration steps. Do not rediscover; reuse verbatim.

### 2026-09-10 — Step 3 dashboard shell rebuilt on VhyxUI (Drawer + hand-built nav markup, per the brief — not a component-for-component migration)

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 3 session)
**Context:** Confirmed via direct investigation (not the original report, which the brief explicitly said not to trust blindly): the real nav config is one level of real nesting (Dashboard link, Organizations section with per-org submenus for Members/API Keys/Tunnels/Billing[owner-only]/Settings[admin+], Account section), active-route highlighting via `usePathname()` exact-match, and a genuinely-live desktop collapsed(71px)/expanded(260px) state with hover-to-expand, persisted via the existing `useSettings()`/cookie system — not just a responsive mobile/desktop CSS toggle. Breadcrumbs, despite being named in the task brief's chrome list, do not exist anywhere in the current shell (zero grep matches) — confirmed aspirational, not a regression to preserve.
**Options considered:** (1) reskin pieces of the existing 40+ file vendored `@menu`/`@layouts` Vuexy system in place; (2) hand-build fresh markup using VhyxUI's `Drawer` (mobile) + plain `<aside>` (desktop) + VhyxUI tokens, per the brief's explicit instruction, while reusing the EXISTING state/data layer (`useVerticalNav()` context, `useSettings()`, `useMyAccounts()`, role-check logic) rather than rebuilding those too.
**Decision:** Option 2. New files under `apps/web/src/libs/layout/vhyxui/`: `DashboardShell`, `DashboardSidebar` (+ `DashboardSidebarNav`), `DashboardTopbar`, `DashboardFooter`, plus new shims `ModeDropdown`/`UserDropdown` (VhyxUI `Popover`-based) and a new `Avatar` shim (confirmed MISSING from VhyxUI, same pattern as Typography/Skeleton). `NavToggle.tsx` (the mobile hamburger) was reused completely unchanged — it turned out to already be framework-agnostic (plain `useVerticalNav()` + a bare icon, zero MUI), so there was nothing to migrate there despite living in the old `vertical/` directory. `LayoutWrapper` was also kept unchanged after a near-miss: it looked like nav-chrome and was initially removed, but it's actually a thin, MUI-free wrapper whose real job is calling `useLayoutInit(systemMode)` — live OS-dark-mode-preference syncing distinct from `ModeChanger.tsx` (which only reacts to explicit settings changes, not live OS-preference changes) plus a `colorPref` SSR cookie. Restored before finalizing.
**Rationale:** The state/data layer (collapse/hover/breakpoint context, settings persistence, org data, role gating) was already correct and non-trivial — rebuilding it alongside the markup would have been pure risk with no benefit. Reusing it while replacing only the presentation layer matches the same "reuse hooks/data, replace only components" principle used in Steps 1-2.
**Explicitly deferred, not migrated**: `NotificationBell` and `FeedbackButton` are both real, substantial, data-driven features (12 notification-type mappings + mark-read mutations; a full RHF+yup feedback form with Dialog/Chip/Collapse) rendered inside the shell but are "content," not "nav chrome" — left fully MUI-internal, just continuing to render as-is inside the new shell. VhyxUI also has no `Collapse`/`Dropdown` primitive that would be needed to migrate `NotificationBell` properly anyway.
**CssBaseline/ThemeProvider must stay active for this route group** — same conclusion as blank-layout-pages (Step 2), but for a different reason now: the OLD shell's own theme dependency (34 `useTheme()`/style-generator uses) is gone in the new shell, but the still-unmigrated page CONTENT (dashboard/organizations pages), `AuthGuard`'s MUI `CircularProgress` loading state, and the deliberately-untouched `NotificationBell`/`FeedbackButton` all still need it.
**Status:** active. Gates (typecheck, build) pass. **Functional verification completed** in a follow-up session (browser extension reconnected after reinstall) via the temporary `dev-shell-test` harness: active-route highlighting confirmed by temporarily adding one extra `NavLink` probe directly into the real `DashboardSidebarNav` (real links all route into the `AuthGuard`-protected `(dashboard)` group, so they couldn't be clicked from the unauthenticated harness without losing it — the probe exercises the exact same production comparison logic and was removed immediately after); desktop collapse/expand and hover-to-expand confirmed; mobile Drawer open (hamburger)/close (backdrop click)/close (Escape) all confirmed; ModeDropdown and UserDropdown open/close, outside-click dismiss, Escape dismiss, and mode-switching all confirmed; Logout confirmed to fire the correct real request (`POST /api/v1/auth/logout`, 503 since no backend is running — same acceptable pattern as Steps 1-2). Org submenu (role badges, per-org Members/API Keys/Tunnels/Billing/Settings, expand/collapse) remains **untested** — not skipped, but no real or faked backend session with actual org data was available in this environment, so the org section only ever showed the loading skeleton or the empty "0 orgs" state. The temporary test harness (`apps/web/src/app/[locale]/dev-shell-test/page.tsx`) has been deleted now that functional testing is done.

### 2026-09-10 — Correction: RHF type mismatch — dependency classification was already correct; real gap is Form's prop typing

**Decided by:** VhyxUI team lead, relayed and logged by VhyxVoid
**Context:** The 2026-09-10 "VhyxUI Form/react-hook-form generic typing friction" entry attributed the type mismatch to VhyxUI having its own separately-installed react-hook-form as a non-peer dependency. VhyxUI's team lead reports this classification has been `peerDependencies` correctly since their commit `f25e1283` (predates this finding) — so the duplicate-install observation was real, but not caused by a dependency-classification bug on VhyxUI's side.
**Actual root cause (per VhyxUI):** `Form.tsx`'s `form` prop is typed directly against react-hook-form's `UseFormReturn<FieldValues>`, which is a nominal type tied to whichever physical react-hook-form module produced it — even with correct peerDependencies, pnpm's store layout under `link:` can still resolve two physical copies for a consumer, and nominal typing doesn't tolerate that. Fix (confirmed in progress, targeting 0.3.2-alpha): retype `form` against a structural `FormInstance` interface VhyxUI owns, which type-checks against any react-hook-form version/instance since TypeScript structural typing doesn't care which module produced the object.
**Status of the original `any`-cast workaround:** Still correct and still needed until 0.3.2-alpha ships and is verified. Do not remove yet.
**Action for next session, once 0.3.2-alpha is published:** verify the type mismatch is actually gone with the new VhyxUI version, then revisit the `any` cast in Login.tsx (and every page that reused the template) — this was flagged in the original entry as "the standing template for every remaining form page," so removing it cleanly will need checking all pages that copied it, not just Login.tsx.
**Status:** active — supersedes the causal explanation (not the workaround) in the original 2026-09-10 "VhyxUI Form/react-hook-form generic typing friction" entry.

### 2026-09-10 — VhyxUI brand override file relocated from VhyxUI's repo to VhyxVoid's (scoping mistake from Step 0, corrected)

**Decided by:** Claude Code (correction session)
**Context:** The 2026-09-09 "VhyxUI stays a separate repo" entry established that VhyxUI is consumed via direct git repo specifically so genuine upstream bug fixes can be contributed back with the team's permission later. Step 0's "VhyxUI brand override scoped via data-brand" entry got the scoping *mechanism* right (`[data-brand="vhyxvoid"]`, additive, opt-in) but put the *file* in the wrong place: `packages/tokens/src/themes/vhyxvoid.css` shipped inside VhyxUI's own distributable package (wired into `sync-bundle.js`'s `SRC_FILES`, `package.json`'s `exports` as `./vhyxvoid.css`, and `src/themes/index.css`'s `@import`), meaning VhyxVoid's brand-specific hex values were baked into every consumer's copy of `@vhyxui/tokens`, not just this app's. Consumer-specific branding is not a bug fix and should never have been added to VhyxUI's repo in the first place.
**Options considered:** (1) leave it in VhyxUI's repo but gate it behind a build flag or peerDep option; (2) delete it from VhyxUI entirely and recreate the identical override as a plain CSS file inside VhyxVoid's own repo (`apps/web`), imported directly after `@vhyxui/tokens/index.css` so load order still lets it win.
**Decision:** Option 2. Deleted `packages/tokens/src/themes/vhyxvoid.css` from VhyxUI and reverted the three wiring points (`sync-bundle.js`'s `SRC_FILES` entry, `package.json`'s `./vhyxvoid.css` export, `src/themes/index.css`'s `@import`), then regenerated VhyxUI's shipped `index.css` via `pnpm --filter @vhyxui/tokens sync` — confirmed via `git diff`/`git status` that `packages/tokens/` is now byte-identical to HEAD, with zero `vhyxvoid` references anywhere in VhyxUI's source (grep, 0 matches). Recreated the identical CSS (same custom-property values, same `[data-brand="vhyxvoid"]`/`[data-brand="vhyxvoid"][data-theme="dark"]` scoping) at `apps/web/src/app/vhyxui-brand-override.css`, imported directly in `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx` immediately after `@vhyxui/tokens/index.css` (order matters: same-specificity attribute selectors, later import wins).
**Rationale:** Restores the actual intended separation from the 2026-09-09 repo-topology decision — VhyxUI's repo now contains no VhyxVoid-specific artifacts, so it stays a clean base for genuine upstream contributions. The visual result is identical (verified empirically, see below); only the file's location and the mechanism for *shipping* it changed, not the override mechanism itself, which remains correct and unchanged.
**Standing project convention going forward:** changes made directly in the VhyxUI repo during this project are reserved for genuine upstream bug fixes intended for eventual contribution back to the VhyxUI team (e.g. the 'use client' dist-output fix tracked in the entry above). Anything consumer-specific — branding, app-side workarounds, VhyxVoid-only behavior — belongs in VhyxVoid's own repo, never in VhyxUI's. Check this entry before adding anything new directly to the VhyxUI checkout.
**Verification:** Recovered the exact original values from a stale Next.js build-cache CSS chunk (`.next/static/chunks/*.css`, still containing the old bundled output) since the source file had already been deleted from VhyxUI by the time full-content capture was needed — confirmed byte-for-byte match on every custom property before writing the new file. Empirically re-verified (same method as the original Step 0/1 check): on the migrated landing page, the primary Button's `getComputedStyle(...).backgroundColor` resolves to `rgb(124, 58, 237)` (`#7C3AED`, VhyxVoid's real brand purple, not VhyxUI's default indigo) with `data-theme="light"`; forcing `data-theme="dark"` on `<html>` confirmed the dark-mode override block also applies correctly (`--vhyx-color-bg: #09090b`, `--vhyx-color-surface: #0f0f14`, `--vhyx-color-text: #e1def5e6`, `--vhyx-color-accent` unchanged at `#7c3aed` since accent is mode-invariant in the source). `apps/web` typecheck and build both pass clean (19 routes, no count change) after clearing the stale `.next` cache used for value recovery.
**Status:** active — supersedes the file-location aspect only of the 2026-09-10 "VhyxUI brand override scoped via data-brand" entry above; that entry's scoping-mechanism decision remains correct and is not reversed.

### 2026-09-10 — Step 4: VhyxUI Form/Field error display requires reading a formState field that changes on submit (isSubmitting alone is not enough)

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 4 session)
**Context:** While building `ProfileView`'s edit form, submitting with an empty required field correctly blocked the mutation but rendered NO visible error message — `Field`'s automatic `getFieldState(name)` context read was not stale (it always fetches the current value), but nothing forced the tree to re-render after validation failed. Root cause, traced through VhyxUI's source: `Form` (`packages/react/src/components/Form/Form.tsx`) only re-renders when ITS OWN parent re-renders; it never itself reads a `formState` subfield during render, so react-hook-form's proxy-based subscription (which only re-renders a component when a field it actually read changes) never fires on `Form`'s parent unless that parent independently reads some `formState` field. Login.tsx/Register.tsx (Step 2) happened to work only because they already read `form.formState.isSubmitting` for the submit button's `loading` prop — an accidental, undocumented dependency, not a deliberate part of the template as written.
**A second, subtler layer:** reading `formState.isSubmitting` alone fixes the "error never appears" case (submit start/end always toggles `isSubmitting`), but NOT the "error never clears while typing" case — RHF's default `reValidateMode: 'onChange'` clears a field's error live as the user fixes it, but that alone doesn't change `isSubmitting`, so a component only subscribed to `isSubmitting` still won't re-render, leaving a stale error message on screen after the user has already fixed the field. Confirmed via direct testing: typing a valid value into a field showing "Required" left the message stuck until the next submit attempt, until also reading `formState.errors`.
**Options considered:** (1) pass an explicit `error` prop to every `TextField` sourced from `formState.errors` in the component body (defeats the point of Field's automatic context lookup, and reverses the Step 2 convention against passing explicit `error` props); (2) have each form-owning component also read `formState.errors` (in addition to whatever fields it already needs, e.g. `isDirty`/`isSubmitting`) purely to subscribe to the right re-render trigger, even when the value itself is otherwise unused.
**Decision:** Option 2. Every migrated form component now includes `void formState.errors` (or destructures `errors` and discards it) alongside its other formState reads, with a comment pointing back to this entry. Applied to `ProfileView`, `ChangePasswordView`, and `OrgSettingsView`'s rename form.
**Rationale:** Cheapest fix that doesn't reverse the established "no manual error prop" convention or require re-architecting VhyxUI's `Form`/`Field` internals (out of scope — this is VhyxVoid's repo, not VhyxUI's, per the repo-topology convention above). A one-line, well-commented `void formState.errors` per form is a small, clearly-flagged cost for correctness.
**Verified working correctly after the fix**: browser-tested on `ProfileView` — submitting with an empty required field shows "Required" immediately with no network call; typing a valid value clears the message live without resubmitting; a valid submit fires exactly one `PATCH /api/v1/account/me`. Same pattern verified on `ChangePasswordView`'s cross-field `confirmPassword` mismatch check and its real `POST /api/v1/account/me/password` submit.
**Standing template correction, going forward:** any new VhyxUI `<Form>` usage in this app MUST have some ancestor read `formState.errors` (not just `isSubmitting`/`isDirty`) or validation errors will not reliably display or clear. Login.tsx and Register.tsx (Step 2) have the same latent gap — not fixed in this session (out of Step 4's file scope) but should get the same one-line fix the next time either file is touched.
**Status:** active — standing template correction; supersedes nothing (Step 2's entry never claimed to have found this, it just didn't hit the bug by accident).

### 2026-09-10 — Local dev backend set up as standing test infrastructure (real API + real seeded org data, not a mock layer)

**Decided by:** Claude Code (prerequisite-work session, before Step 5), given latitude by the user to "pick whichever [direction] is actually viable, don't force one"
**Context:** Steps 3 and 4 both hit the same wall repeatedly: no real or faked backend session had actual multi-org/admin-level membership data, so org submenus, role badges, and the org-rename form were never functionally verified — reported honestly as environment limitations each time, but Step 5 (tunnels/api-keys/members) will need exactly this kind of data constantly, not occasionally. Investigated two directions: (a) a real local `apps/api`+`apps/hub`+Postgres/Redis stack seeded with test data, or (b) a lightweight mock/fixture layer (MSW or a dev-only API route stub).
**What was actually found, empirically, before choosing:** every prior session's `503` responses from `http://localhost:9000/...` were NOT from a real server — `curl`/`lsof` confirmed nothing was listening on that port at the start of this session. Those `503`s were the browser-automation network-tracking tool's own synthetic placeholder for a connection-level failure (confirmed by cross-referencing: real server-side request logs never contained a matching entry for those calls). So "the backend has never really been running" was accurate for every session up to and including Step 4.
**Also found, empirically:** this machine already has a local Postgres 16 server running (Homebrew) with a database named `Black-server`, already migrated to this project's current Prisma schema, and **already populated with real test data** (24 users, 25 accounts, 26 memberships, 23 API keys, tunnel/invitation records) — clearly from earlier local dev work, not created this session. `apps/api/.env` even has a commented-out `DATABASE_URL` line pointing at exactly this database, with the active line switched to a remote Neon Postgres instance instead (which also has real Stripe/Resend keys alongside it).
**Options considered:** (1) run `apps/api` against the already-active Neon `DATABASE_URL` and seed test data directly into it; (2) stand up local Postgres/Redis via Docker per the brief's literal suggestion; (3) run `apps/api` locally with `DATABASE_URL` overridden (via shell env, never editing `.env`) to the pre-existing local `Black-server` database, reusing its pre-existing seeded data rather than writing new seed data; (4) build a mock/fixture layer (MSW or dev-only route stubs) instead of a real backend.
**Decision:** Option 3. Never considered option 1 further once the Neon database's real-shared-resource nature and adjacent real Stripe/Resend credentials were understood — writing test data into a live remote database outside this sandbox's isolation, or risking a real Stripe/Resend call, is exactly the class of hard-to-reverse, shared-state-affecting action that warrants stopping and not just proceeding because a brief's phrasing loosely permitted it. Option 2 (Docker) turned out unnecessary — Docker's daemon isn't even running in this environment, but a suitable local Postgres already was. Option 4 (mocks) was not pursued once option 3 turned out fully viable — a mock layer would mean Step 5's functional checks never actually exercise the real auth/validation/persistence path, which is materially weaker than what's now available for free.
**What was actually done:** started `apps/api` with `DATABASE_URL` overridden via shell env var (dotenv does not overwrite an already-set var, so `apps/api/.env`'s Neon line is never read or touched) pointing at the local `Black-server` database. Confirmed it boots and serves real requests — Redis/Upstash env vars are present (even though this sandbox likely can't reach Upstash's actual cloud endpoint), and `initRedis()` only checks that the vars are *set*, not reachable, so boot never blocks on it. Found and fixed a real CORS gap: `apps/api/src/core/constant/hub.constant.ts`'s `allowedOrigins` only listed `http://localhost:4000`, not the `4177` port this project's sessions have been using as a fallback when port 4000 is occupied by an unrelated local project (confirmed occupied again this session, correctly left untouched) — added `http://localhost:4177` to the allowlist. Reset two existing test users' passwords directly via SQL (bcrypt cost 12, matching `BcryptPasswordHasher`) to a documented known value, since the originals were unknown and this is local-only test data with no external effect: `test@example.com` (Owner of `Test Corp`, which has 2 real API keys, 9 tunnel records, and a pending invitation) and `alicess@example.com` (Owner of `Acme Corp`, which has a SECOND real member, `alicesss@example.com`, at Admin level — the one org in the existing data with actual role-badge diversity to test against).
**Verified working, this session:** logged in as both test accounts via the real login flow; Members/API Keys/Tunnels/Settings pages all rendered real data (previously only ever seen as loading skeletons or empty states); `OrgSettingsView`'s rename form — untestable in every prior session — fired a real `PATCH` and the UI updated correctly (tested end-to-end, reverted the name back to `Test Corp` afterward); Acme Corp's Members table rendered two different role badges (Owner/Admin) and two different verified states (Pending/Verified) side by side.
**Explicitly NOT done:** no new seed script was written (the existing local data already covered every need); the real Neon `DATABASE_URL`, Stripe keys, and Resend keys in `apps/api/.env` were never touched or exercised; `apps/hub` was not started (not needed for `apps/web`'s functional checks — the Tunnels list reads Postgres directly via `apps/api`).
**Standing infra, not a one-off harness:** unlike `dev-shell-test`/`dev-step4-test` (deleted after each use), this local Postgres database and its seeded test accounts are meant to persist across sessions. Documented in `LOCAL_DEV_BACKEND.md` (repo root): exact restart commands, the port-4000-vs-4177 CORS note, the three test account credentials and what each is good for, and an explicit warning never to point `apps/api` at the real Neon `DATABASE_URL` for testing. Both processes were stopped at the end of this session (not left running); any future session restarts them per that doc.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-10 — Step 5a scope correction: GenericServerTable is not actually MUI-independent — only its `<table>` element is

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5a session)
**Context:** The Step 5a brief stated `GenericServerTable` was "confirmed already MUI-independent (plain HTML `<table>` + headless @tanstack/react-table, not MUI Table/DataGrid) — so this is restyling with VhyxUI tokens/CSS Modules, not a component swap." Reading the file directly showed this was only true for the literal `<table>`/`<thead>`/`<tbody>` markup. The surrounding chrome was all real `@mui/material` React components: `Card` (outer wrapper), `Typography` (title), `Chip` (search-result-count pill), `InputAdornment`+`CustomTextField` (search box and each filter dropdown, itself a MUI `TextField` wrapper), `Button`+`MenuItem` (Clear Filters, filter options). Its two helper files had the same gap: `TablePaginationComponent.tsx` used MUI `Pagination`/`FormControl`/`Select`/`MenuItem`/`Typography`, and `TableSkeleton.tsx` used MUI `Skeleton`. None of these are stylable via CSS alone without swapping the actual React component — restyling was not sufficient.
**Why this matters beyond Tunnels:** `GenericServerTable` is shared by every list screen in the app, including Step 4's deliberately-deferred `FeedbackHistoryTab`/`MembersTable`/`InvitationsTab`, the "My organizations" dashboard table (`MyAccountsTable`), Step 5b/5c's upcoming `ApiKeysView`/`MembersTable`, and the admin-only tables (Step 6, out of scope). Migrating it here fixes it once for all of them rather than requiring the same investigation again in Step 5b.
**Decision:** Migrated `GenericServerTable.tsx`, `TablePaginationComponent.tsx`, and `TableSkeleton.tsx` to VhyxUI (`Card`, `Badge`, `Input`, `Select`, `Button`; `Pagination`+`Select`; the Step 0 `Skeleton` shim respectively) rather than treating this as out-of-scope for a "Tunnels screen" brief — the brief's own bullet list already named Pagination/Typography/Button/TableSkeleton swaps explicitly, which are all properties of these shared files, not of `TunnelsView.tsx` itself. Also updated the shared `@core/styles/table.module.css` (`--mui-palette-text-primary/secondary`, `--border-color` → `--vhyx-color-text`, `--vhyx-color-text-subtle`, `--vhyx-color-border`) since the table's own visible cell/header text and borders are the one part of this file that genuinely was CSS-only.
**Verified working, this session:** confirmed via the real local backend (not just visual inspection) on two different consumers of the shared component in the same session — the "My organizations" dashboard table and the Tunnels history table — that search, the status filter dropdown, Clear Filters, the Total-count badge, and pagination controls all still work correctly against real data.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-10 — Step 5a: brand-override token gap found and fixed via Badge's default variant rendering illegibly

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5a session)
**Context:** After migrating `GenericServerTable`'s "Total: N" chip to VhyxUI's `Badge` (`variant='default'`), it rendered as a near-invisible light-gray-on-near-black pill. Root cause traced precisely: `data-theme` on `<html>` is `"light"` by default in this app (VhyxVoid's OS-preference-driven `systemMode`, not a user override), but VhyxVoid's own "light" mode is a near-black aesthetic by design (see the 2026-09-10 "VhyxUI brand override scoped via data-brand" entry). `--vhyx-color-bg-muted` (which `Badge`'s default variant reads) was never covered by the brand-override file for the default/light block — that entry's follow-up ("Temporary Typography/Skeleton shims" era) explicitly left `--vhyx-color-text-muted` uncovered too, reasoning it would "fall back to VhyxUI's own default." That fallback turned out visually broken here: VhyxUI's own un-overridden default for these tokens is an actual light gray/slate, correct for a real light theme but wrong against VhyxVoid's near-black "light" mode.
**Also found the same way:** `Select`'s trigger border and `Badge`'s `outline` variant both read `--vhyx-color-border-strong`, which had the identical gap. A quick audit of which CSS variables `Badge`/`Select`/`Pagination`/`Input` actually read (grepped their `.module.css` files directly, not guessed) surfaced the complete list of what those four components need beyond what Step 0 already covered: `bg-muted`, `bg-subtle`, `border-strong`, `border-focus`, `surface-overlay`, `text-muted`.
**Decision:** Added all six to `apps/web/src/app/vhyxui-brand-override.css`, in both the default/light block and the `[data-theme="dark"]` block, following the same alpha-overlay pattern already established for `--vhyx-color-border` (e.g. `--vhyx-color-bg-muted: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.06)`) rather than guessing arbitrary hex values. Did not add `bg-inverse`, `overlay`, or `text-inverse` — confirmed via the same grep that none of the components introduced in this step actually read them; adding values for tokens nothing yet uses would be speculative, not a confirmed fix.
**Rationale:** This corrects (not reverses) the earlier "falls back to VhyxUI's own default" reasoning — that reasoning assumed the fallback would look acceptable, which is now confirmed false for a near-black "light" mode. Scoped to exactly the tokens with a confirmed rendering defect, not a speculative full audit of every token VhyxUI ships.
**Verified working:** reloaded and re-inspected via `getComputedStyle` — the "Total: N" badge's background/text now match the rest of the near-black UI with correct contrast, in both the default and forced-dark states.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-10 — Step 5a: GenericServerTable's toolbar row needed `flex-shrink: 0` protection on migrated controls

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5a session)
**Context:** After the component swap above, "Clear Filters" rendered with its label visibly clipped (`overflow: hidden` cutting off the "C"). Root cause: VhyxUI's `Button` bakes `overflow: hidden` + `white-space: nowrap` into its base class (needed for its loading-spinner crossfade), unlike MUI's `Button`. In a `flex` toolbar row without explicit shrink protection, a flex item can compress below its content's natural width; MUI's button just let text overflow visibly when squeezed, but VhyxUI's clips it silently instead — a real, user-visible regression risk anywhere a VhyxUI `Button`/`Badge`/`Select`/`Input` sits in a tight flex row without protection.
**Decision:** Added `flex-wrap` to the toolbar rows (so controls wrap to a new line under real width pressure instead of compressing) and `shrink-0` (Tailwind's `flex-shrink: 0`) to the search `Input`, each filter `Select`, the Clear Filters `Button`, and the Total `Badge`.
**Standing note for future steps:** any VhyxUI `Button` placed in a `flex` row alongside other controls needs either `shrink-0` or a wrapping/width strategy that guarantees it never compresses below its label's width — Login/Register's simpler single-button-per-row layouts never hit this because there was nothing else in their flex row to compete for space with.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-10 — Step 5a: apps/api's tunnel-history endpoint never returns `id` (backend bug, worked around in the frontend, not fixed at the source)

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5a session)
**Context:** Functionally testing the Session history table against real backend data (via a temporarily-inserted DISCONNECTED test row, deleted after the check) surfaced a real React warning: "Encountered two children with the same key, `undefined`". Traced to `apps/api/src/modules/identity/presentation/http/user/tunnel.routes.ts`'s history handler: its `items.map((s: any) => ({...}))` DTO builds each row from `agentId, label, status, connectedAt, disconnectedAt, durationMs, apiKey` — never `id` — even though the frontend's `TunnelSession` type declares `id: string` as required and `GenericServerTable` requires a real, unique `id` for React row keys (`getRowId`, `<tr key={row.id}>`). Confirmed via direct DB query that `tunnel_sessions.id` rows are real, non-null UUIDs — the column exists and is populated, the backend's response mapper for this one endpoint simply omits it.
**Options considered:** (1) fix the backend mapper to include `id: s.id`; (2) work around it in the frontend by substituting a field that's already present and confirmed unique.
**Decision:** Option 2. `TunnelsView.tsx`'s `fetchHistory` now maps `id: item.agentId` onto every returned item before handing it to `GenericServerTable` — safe because `agentId` has a DB-level `UNIQUE` constraint (`tunnel_sessions_agentId_key`), confirmed by reading the schema directly, not assumed.
**Rationale:** This is a genuine backend response-shape bug, not a frontend styling concern — fixing `apps/api` business logic is outside a frontend restyling step's authorized scope, and a one-line frontend substitution using an already-guaranteed-unique field is lower-risk than editing the backend DTO mid-migration-step. Flagging here rather than silently leaving the console warning in place, per the brief's explicit instruction not to improvise past a found gap without flagging it.
**Verified working:** reloaded with the console-message tool active from page load — confirmed zero console errors on Session history with real multi-row data after the fix (the warning reproduced reliably before it, on the same data).
**Open item:** `apps/api`'s tunnel-history DTO should get `id: s.id` added whenever that file is next touched for a backend-side reason — the frontend workaround is safe and correct but the backend gap itself is still there for any other consumer of that endpoint.
**Status:** active

### 2026-09-11 — Step 5b: Dialog.Portal/Dialog.Overlay omission — every dialog in the app rendered permanently open until fixed

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5b session)
**Context:** After first migrating all four dialogs (`Confirmation`, `CreateApiKeyDialog`, `ApiKeySecretDialog`, `RotateApiKeyDialog`) to `<Dialog><Dialog.Content>...</Dialog.Content></Dialog>`, loading the API Keys screen showed the Create-key form, both Rotate dialogs, and both Revoke confirmations all rendered simultaneously and permanently `data-state="open"` — regardless of the `open` prop passed in. Traced to VhyxUI's own source: `Dialog.Content` has NO gating on `ctx.open` at all; only `Dialog.Portal` does (`if (!ctx.open) return null`), and `Dialog.Overlay` (the backdrop) is also normally rendered inside `Dialog.Portal`. VhyxUI's own `Dialog.tsx` docstring example omits both — `<Dialog><Dialog.Trigger>...</Dialog.Trigger><Dialog.Content>...</Dialog.Content></Dialog>` — but its own `Dialog.test.tsx` confirms the actual required structure always wraps `Dialog.Overlay` + `Dialog.Content` inside `Dialog.Portal`. The docstring example is a genuine (if minor) documentation bug in VhyxUI itself — not something to fix in VhyxUI's repo from this session (out of scope, and not a case where this project has upstream contribution access arranged), but worth flagging if this project's contact with the VhyxUI team ever includes doc feedback.
**Decision:** Added `<Dialog.Portal><Dialog.Overlay />...</Dialog.Portal>` wrapping `Dialog.Content` in all four dialog files. Verified via DOM inspection (`data-state`, and the overlay's actual computed `background-color`/`position: fixed`/`z-index`) that dialogs now correctly stay closed until triggered, and the backdrop renders correctly (a semi-transparent black overlay that's easy to miss in a screenshot against this app's already near-black theme, but confirmed present via `getComputedStyle`).
**Standing template correction, going forward:** any future VhyxUI `Dialog` usage in this app MUST wrap `Dialog.Overlay` + `Dialog.Content` in `Dialog.Portal` — copy the structure from any of the four files this session touched, not from VhyxUI's own docstring example.
**Status:** active — standing template correction

### 2026-09-11 — Step 5b: fixed two real pre-existing bugs in the Rotate API key flow, found only because this was the first session to exercise it against a real backend

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5b session)
**Context:** Two independent, real, pre-existing frontend bugs in the rotate flow — neither introduced by this migration, both invisible in every prior session because no session before this one had a real backend to test rotate against.
**Bug 1 — double mutation call:** `RotateApiKeyDialog` called `useRotateApiKey(accountId).mutate(row.id, {...})` itself AND `ApiKeysView`'s `onRotate` callback (triggered from the dialog's own `onSuccess`) called a SEPARATE `useRotateApiKey(accountId).mutate(keyId, {...})` — meaning every real rotate click fired the rotate endpoint twice, generating two secrets, silently discarding the first (its result was never read) and showing only the second. **Fixed** by making `ApiKeysView` the single owner of the mutation (`rotateKey.mutateAsync`); `RotateApiKeyDialog` now only triggers the parent's already-bound `onConfirm` and awaits it before closing, with its own local `isRotating` state driving the loading UI instead of reading the (now removed) local mutation's `isPending`.
**Bug 2 — wrong ID field sent to the backend:** the rotate action's `dialogProps: { onConfirm: () => args.onRotate(key.keyId) }` passed `key.keyId` (the public-facing key identifier string, e.g. `vhyxvoid_dev_...`), but `apps/api`'s route schema for this endpoint (`accountKeyParamSchema`, shared with revoke/update) expects the record's internal UUID — its own source has a comment confirming this: `keyId: z.string().uuid(), // internal UUID (id), not the public keyId string`. The adjacent revoke action already correctly used `key.id`; rotate did not. This produced a live Zod "Invalid UUID" 400-shaped rejection the instant rotate was tested against the real backend. **Fixed** by changing the call site to `args.onRotate(key.id)`.
**How both were found:** functional-testing rotate against the real local backend (per LOCAL_DEV_BACKEND.md) produced a request that failed before reaching any Redis-dependent code path, unlike create/revoke's failures (see the Redis entry below) — narrowed down via a temporary `window.__ROTATE_DEBUG__` trace (the browser console-message tool intermittently failed to surface `console.log` output during this investigation for reasons not fully understood — reading a plain `window` property directly was reliable and is a good fallback technique to remember) that captured the actual rejected error's message: a Zod validation error naming `keyId` with format `uuid`.
**Verified working after both fixes:** exactly one `POST .../rotate` request per click (confirmed via network log), targeting a real UUID path segment. The request still fails in this sandbox for the unrelated reason covered by the next entry (Redis unreachable) — that failure is a separate, already-understood environment limitation, not evidence either fix is incomplete.
**Status:** active — both fixed at the call sites named above; out of scope to also fix in `apps/api` even though bug 2 is nominally a backend schema-naming confusion, since the frontend-side fix fully resolves it from this app's perspective

### 2026-09-11 — Step 5b: Redis/Upstash unreachability causes create/rotate/revoke to return 500 even when the underlying database write succeeds (confirmed backend bug, flagged not fixed)

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5b session)
**Context:** `LOCAL_DEV_BACKEND.md` (written during the prerequisite session before Step 5) predicted that Redis-backed code paths "would be affected" by this sandbox's lack of real Upstash connectivity but noted no observed UI effect from Steps 1-5a's testing. Step 5b is the first session to exercise a mutation that actually calls into `RedisApiKeyCacheService` synchronously as part of its success path, and the effect is more severe than "silently degrades": `RedisApiKeyCacheService.set()` explicitly re-throws after logging (`catch (err) { console.error(...); throw err }`), and `invalidate()` has no try/catch at all — so a DNS failure reaching `*.upstash.io` (confirmed: `ENOTFOUND`, no network path to the real Upstash cloud endpoint from this sandbox) propagates up and makes the whole HTTP handler return 500, **even after the real, authoritative database write already succeeded**. Confirmed precisely via direct DB inspection across all three mutations: **create** — both test keys created via the UI (with a real 500 response) existed in Postgres with the exact name/environment/scopes submitted; **revoke** — a test key correctly flipped to `REVOKED` in Postgres despite a 500 response; **rotate** (after the two bugs above were fixed) — did NOT persist a rotation (no `previousSecretHash`/`rotationGraceEndsAt` set), meaning rotate's Redis interaction happens earlier in its flow than create/revoke's, blocking the DB write itself rather than happening after it.
**Practical consequence:** in this sandbox, every create/rotate/revoke call on the API Keys screen will appear to fail from the UI's perspective (no secret-reveal dialog, no visible confirmation) even when create/revoke actually mutate real data — meaning a naive retry-after-failure habit will silently create duplicate real rows (this session did exactly that once, then cleaned up both accidental test keys via direct SQL afterward).
**Options considered:** (1) fix `RedisApiKeyCacheService` to swallow cache-write failures the same way `incrementUsage` already does (it has a comment explicitly stating "Usage failure must never affect the gateway response") — the codebase's own author clearly considered this exact class of gap for one method but not the others; (2) flag and document, since this is `apps/api` business logic, out of scope for a frontend restyling task.
**Decision:** Option 2. Not fixed. `LOCAL_DEV_BACKEND.md` updated with this precise finding (which mutations are affected, what DOES vs. doesn't persist, and an explicit warning against retry-after-failure on this screen) so the next session doesn't have to rediscover it from scratch or accidentally accumulate more orphaned test data.
**Also discovered, unrelated but adjacent:** no `react-toastify` `<ToastContainer />` is mounted anywhere in this app, so the global `MutationCache`/`QueryCache` `onError` handlers in `queryClient.ts` (which call `toast.error(...)` on every failed query/mutation app-wide) silently no-op — the same class of "toast() needs a mounted provider" issue Step 0 found for VhyxUI's own toast, but for `react-toastify` instead, and pre-existing/unrelated to this migration. Flagged here for visibility, not fixed (out of this step's scope — it's a pre-existing app-wide gap, not specific to API Keys).
**Status:** active — confirmed backend limitation, documented in LOCAL_DEV_BACKEND.md; revisit if/when this sandbox gets real Upstash connectivity or `apps/api`'s cache-write error handling is revisited for its own reasons

### 2026-09-11 — Step 5b: component-mapping gaps where VhyxUI has no direct equivalent to what MUI offered

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5b session)
**Context:** Several MUI usages in the API Keys screen and its shared table/row-action infrastructure had no clean 1:1 VhyxUI equivalent. Recording the choices made so future steps don't re-litigate the same gaps.
1. **Scopes multi-select (confirmed accurate per the brief):** the original was a hand-built `Chip`-array toggle (local `useState`, not MUI Autocomplete) — confirmed still accurate by reading the file directly. VhyxUI has no multi-select component at all. Kept the same architecture, restyled onto `Badge`, but wrapped each `Badge` in a plain `<button type="button">` rather than putting `onClick` directly on the `Badge` (a `<span>`) — a non-interactive element with a bare `onClick` isn't keyboard-operable, which every other VhyxUI component in this app is deliberately built to be (focus traps, ARIA labels throughout). This is a real, deliberate a11y-driven deviation from "just make Badge clickable."
2. **Badge has no accent/primary variant** (only `default`/`success`/`warning`/`danger`/`info`/`outline`) — the selected-scope state needs the brand accent purple, which `Button`'s `primary` variant has but `Badge` doesn't expose at all. Applied as a targeted inline `style` override on the selected `Badge` rather than picking a semantically-wrong variant (e.g. `info`, which would render cyan, unrelated to "selected").
3. **Button has no per-instance `color` prop** (only a fixed `variant` set: primary/secondary/outline/ghost/destructive/link), unlike MUI's `IconButton color={...}`. For row actions (`RowAction.tsx`) that need to preserve a semantic tint (e.g. rotate = warning-orange, matching what MUI's `color` prop achieved), applied the color directly to the icon via inline style instead of trying to force it through `variant` — closer to MUI IconButton's actual visual effect anyway (it tints the icon, not the button background).
4. **Button has no `warning` variant** — `RotateApiKeyDialog`'s confirm button used MUI's `color='warning'` originally; VhyxUI's closest options are `primary` (undersells the warning framing already established by the Alert above it) or `destructive` (overstates severity — rotate isn't irreversible data loss the way revoke is, it has a grace period). Chose `primary`, accepting the minor loss of visual distinction, rather than picking `destructive` and misrepresenting how consequential the action actually is.
**Status:** active — these are UI-polish judgment calls, not blocking; revisit only if VhyxUI ships a multi-select, a Badge accent variant, or Button color/warning options later

### 2026-09-11 — Step 5c: `Member.userId` doesn't exist in the real backend response — a wrong-field bug that silently broke remove/change-role/transfer, fixed at the service layer

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5c session)
**Context:** Before migrating anything, cross-checked the Members screen's mutation payload fields against `apps/api`'s actual code (per this step's explicit brief, mirroring the exact check that caught Step 5b's rotate wrong-ID-field bug). `GetAccountMembersUseCase` (`apps/api/src/modules/identity/application/use-cases/user/GetAccountMembers.usecase.ts`) returns each member DTO with `id: m.userId` — there is no `userId` field in the response at all, only `id`. But `apps/web`'s `Member` type (`member.types.ts`) declares `userId: string` as a real field with a comment claiming "`id` is aliased to `userId`... set to userId in service layer" — that normalization was never actually implemented; `member.service.ts`'s `getMembers` just passed the raw response straight through. Every real consumer read `.userId`: `MembersTable.tsx`'s remove action (`onRemove(member.userId)`), `ChangeRoleDialog.tsx` (`row.userId`), `TransferOwnershipDialog.tsx` (`targetUserId: row.userId`), and `useRemoveMember`'s optimistic-update filter (`m.userId !== targetUserId`) — all silently operating on `undefined`.
**Confirmed live, not just by reading code:** before the fix, this would send `PATCH .../members/undefined/role` and `DELETE .../members/undefined`, and `POST .../transfer-ownership` with `targetUserId: undefined` in the body — every one of these three real mutations on this screen would have failed. This is the same bug *class* as Step 5b's rotate bug (frontend sends a malformed identifier the backend can't use), just server-shape-driven instead of a wrong param name at the call site.
**Decision:** Fixed at the single normalization point — `member.service.ts`'s `getMembers` now maps `items` to add `userId: item.id` onto every row before returning, making the `Member` type's own already-documented (but previously unimplemented) contract actually true. This fixes every consumer at once rather than patching each call site individually.
**Verified working after the fix:** functional-tested a real `PATCH .../members/<real-uuid>/role` against the local backend — confirmed via network inspection the URL now carries the correct UUID (`454ecec1-2147-4711-b8f8-01328c389283`), not `undefined`, and the request returned 200.
**Status:** active — fixed; TypeScript could not have caught this on its own since `httpClient` casts the raw fetch response to the declared type with no runtime validation, so the frontend/backend type contract silently drifted until exercised against a real backend

### 2026-09-11 — Step 5c: `GenericServerTable`'s own query key is disjoint from the semantic query-key factories mutations invalidate — mutations returned 200 but the visible table never refreshed

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5c session)
**Context:** After fixing the `userId` bug above, functional-testing change-role against the real backend showed the PATCH return a real 200 with the correct UUID, but the role Badge in the visible table never updated — even after a second identical mutation, even though the DB value had genuinely changed (confirmed by a hard page reload, which did show the new role). Root cause: `GenericServerTable.tsx` runs its own internal `useQuery` keyed by `[tableKey, JSON.stringify(cleanParams)]` (e.g. `['members-<accountId>', '{"page":1,...}']`), completely independent of the `memberKeys.list({accountId})` query-key factory (`createQueryKeys('members')`, producing a structurally different key shape) that `useChangeMemberRole`/`useRemoveMember`/`useTransferOwnership`/`useInviteMember`/`useCancelInvitation`'s `onSuccess`/`onSettled` handlers call `invalidateQueries` against. The two key namespaces never overlap, so `invalidateQueries` never matches the table's actual live query, and the visible screen only ever updates on a full navigation/reload that remounts the table with a cold cache.
**Scope of the gap:** this is a systemic pattern in `GenericServerTable`'s design, not specific to Members — any `GenericServerTable` consumer whose mutations invalidate via a semantic key factory instead of the table's own `tableKey` string will have the same silent-staleness bug. Not verified here whether Tunnels/ApiKeys (Steps 5a/5b) mutations already work around this some other way (e.g. optimistic `setQueriesData` writes that happen to hit the right cache entries) or share the same gap — worth checking explicitly in a future session rather than assuming either way.
**Decision:** Fixed narrowly for this screen's mutations only (not `GenericServerTable` itself, which is shared infra out of this step's scope) — added a second `invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [\`members-${accountId}\`] })` / `[\`invitations-${accountId}\`]` call (matching the literal `tableKey` strings `MembersTable.tsx`/`InvitationsTab.tsx` pass in) to `useChangeMemberRole`, `useRemoveMember`, `useTransferOwnership`, `useInviteMember` (`useMembers.ts`) and `useCancelInvitation` (`useOrg.ts`), alongside their existing semantic-key invalidation (kept, in case anything else depends on it).
**Verified working after the fix:** repeated the same change-role mutation — the Badge updated immediately in the visible table with no reload required.
**Status:** active — fixed for Members/Invitations; flagging the systemic `GenericServerTable` key-mismatch pattern here so it isn't rediscovered from scratch on a future list screen, and suggesting `GenericServerTable` itself eventually accept/expose a stable, importable key helper so mutation hooks can invalidate it directly instead of duplicating a hardcoded template-string convention

### 2026-09-11 — Step 5c: real, reproducible 500 on remove-member against the local dev backend — confirmed NOT the known Redis-500-on-success-write pattern, root cause not isolated

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Step 5c session)
**Context:** Functional-tested remove-member against the real local backend using a throwaway test member (inserted directly via SQL into `Black-server`'s `User`/`AccountMember` tables for Acme Corp, to avoid mutating the three real seeded members) — per this step's explicit instruction to check whether the Redis-500-on-success-write pattern from Step 5b ("check early whether any of this screen's mutations touch Redis-cached paths") recurs here. It does not touch a Redis-cached path (confirmed by reading `RemoveMemberUseCase`, `PrismaMembershipRepository.delete`, and `PrismaAuditLogRepository.create` directly — none reference Redis/`RedisApiKeyCacheService` or similar), but a real `DELETE .../members/<real-uuid>` request (confirmed correct UUID in the path, ruling out the bug in the entry above) still returned a 500.
**Confirmed distinct from the Step 5b Redis pattern:** direct DB query after the 500 showed the `AccountMember` row was NOT removed — unlike create/revoke's "DB write succeeds despite the 500" behavior, this failure is a genuine no-op, consistent with an exception thrown before or during the delete rather than an unrelated cache write failing after a successful core operation.
**Investigated and ruled out by reading code directly:** `RemoveMemberUseCase`'s permission checks (`actor.isAdmin()`, `actor.canManage(target)`, last-owner guard) — actor is Owner, target is Member, none of these should throw for this scenario; `PrismaMembershipRepository.delete`'s composite-key delete — matches schema; `PrismaAuditLogRepository.create`'s `targetUserId` field — confirmed it exists on the `AuditLog` Prisma model (`prisma/schema.prisma`), so this isn't a repeat of the "DTO missing a field" bug class. Could not capture the actual server-side stack trace this session — `apps/api`'s dev log (`/tmp/api-dev-step5c.log`) never surfaced the `errorHandler` middleware's `request.log.error(...)` output despite `NODE_ENV=development`, and a browser-side attempt to read the response body's dev-mode `error: String(error)` field was blocked by unrelated Chrome extension instability.
**Decision:** Not fixed — this is `apps/api` business logic, out of a frontend restyling step's scope even if it were isolated, and it wasn't fully isolated. Flagged here and in `LOCAL_DEV_BACKEND.md` as a confirmed, reproducible gap for whoever next has server-side log/debugger access to `apps/api`.
**Cleanup:** the throwaway test user/membership row was removed via direct SQL after testing (the UI's remove action itself failed to do this, per the bug above), restoring Acme Corp to its documented 2-member baseline.
**Status:** active — confirmed bug, not fixed, root cause not fully isolated; next session with server-side debugging access to `apps/api` should reproduce (real member, real org, real Admin-or-Owner actor removing a lower-role target) and capture the actual thrown error
