### 2026-09-15 — Phase 2: API Keys converted to props-based table pattern

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Phase 2, second-table session)
**Context:** Following the Members pilot (see the three "Phase 2 pilot" entries above), the task brief asked to convert API Keys next, using the same approach: real query-key-factory-backed list hook, convert off the `useSelfFetchingServerTable` compat shim, click-to-sort verified live against the real schema, remove the now-provably-unnecessary point-fix, verified two ways.
**Options considered:** Same shape of decision as the Members pilot already settled (single props-based contract + compat shim for unconverted tables, not a dual-mode component) — no new architectural option to weigh here, this session just applies the established pattern to a second table.
**Decision:** `apiKeyKeys` (`api/infrastructure/query-keys/api-key.keys.ts`) is a hand-written factory, not built on `@vhyx/api-kit`'s `createQueryKeys` the way `memberKeys` is — but its `list(accountId, params?)` already accepted full `ListApiKeysParams` before this change, so (unlike `memberKeys`) no generic-type widening was needed. New `useApiKeysTableList(accountId, params: FetchParams)` hook maps `FetchParams` → `ListApiKeysParams` (extracting `filters.status`/`filters.environment`) and queries via `apiKeyKeys.list(accountId, cleanedParams)`. `ApiKeysView.tsx` now owns `useServerTable('api-keys-${accountId}')` + this hook directly, passing results into `GenericServerTable` as props — same shape as `MembersTable.tsx`.
**The point-fix removal proof used a different mechanism than Members', verified separately, not assumed to transfer**: `memberKeys` (built on `createQueryKeys`) relies on React Query's *object-subset* partial-key matching (`{accountId}` matches `{accountId, page, limit, ...}`). `apiKeyKeys` is plain nested arrays (`['api-keys', accountId, 'list', params]`), so the relevant mechanism is *array-prefix* matching instead (a shorter filter array, e.g. `['api-keys', accountId, 'list']`, matches any actual key starting with those same elements, regardless of a trailing params object). Added `api/infrastructure/query-keys/api-key.keys.test.ts` (new, 3 tests) to prove this specific mechanism against a real `QueryClient` — both `apiKeyKeys.lists(accountId)` (used by create/update/revoke) and `apiKeyKeys.all(accountId)` (used by rotate) correctly invalidate the table's own parameterized query key, plus a negative case for a different `accountId`. Also verified live: created a throwaway API key on Test Corp, revoked it (`204`), and re-GET the exact default table query (`?sortBy=createdAt&sortOrder=desc`) immediately after — showed `REVOKED` with no separate cache-busting call. Throwaway key deleted via direct SQL afterward. With both proofs confirmed, removed `useApiKeys.ts`'s `invalidateApiKeysTable()` helper and its four call sites (`useCreateApiKey`, `useUpdateApiKey`, `useRevokeApiKey`'s `onSettled`, `useRotateApiKey`).
**Click-to-sort — verified live before shipping, per the Member/name lesson from the pilot session; no mismatch found this time.** Checked `ListApiKeys.usecase.ts`'s sort switch (`name`/`createdAt`/`lastUsedAt`/`status`/`environment`) against its route's actual Zod schema (`apiKey.routes.ts`'s `listApiKeysSchema`) *before* trusting the switch statement, learning from Members' `sortBy=name` mismatch — this time they match exactly, and a live request confirmed all 5 values return `200` (not a repeat of the Members bug). Name/Env/Status/Last-used columns made sortable; Key ID/Scopes/Expires/Actions are not (no matching backend option for any of them). Column ids already matched the backend field names by default (all were plain `col.accessor(fieldName, ...)` calls), so no id renaming was needed here either (another difference from Members' `'member'` → `'name'` rename).
**A real, unrelated bug found and fixed while functionally verifying this in the browser**: `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/.../api-keys/page.tsx` had a stray `console.log('Rendering ApiKeysPage with params:', params)` — logging the raw `params` Promise itself (already correctly awaited into `{accountId}` on the line above) rather than the resolved value. Next.js's dev-mode instrumentation flags synchronous property access on an un-awaited-looking Promise as an error, and this fired on every request to the route; the dev server log showed 8–38s render times as a direct result, and this was directly responsible for a Claude-in-Chrome screenshot action timing out mid-verification. Fixed by deleting the line (a one-line, zero-risk removal of dead debug output) — not something this session's actual task (the table conversion) touched otherwise, but it was directly blocking this session's own verification step, found via that verification, and trivially safe to fix in place.
**Browser visual verification not completed this session, unlike Part 0's toast check earlier the same day.** After fixing the `console.log` bug, the Claude in Chrome extension itself became unresponsive (`screenshot`/`wait` actions failing with "couldn't determine which page this action targets" across multiple fresh tabs, well past the skill's own 2-3-attempt retry guidance) — stopped retrying rather than keep hammering what looked like a tool-connectivity issue unrelated to the app. Relied on the curl-based verification instead, which is the same standard the Members pilot's own core mutate-then-refetch proof used.
**Rationale:** Verifying the point-fix removal via a mechanism-specific automated test (not just reusing Members' `memberKeys` test as a template without checking whether the same reasoning applies) matters because `apiKeyKeys` and `memberKeys` are genuinely different implementations with different invalidation-matching mechanics — assuming the Members proof "transfers" without checking would have been exactly the kind of unverified extrapolation this whole Phase 2 initiative is trying to avoid (see the pilot session's own "verified not just deleted-and-assumed-fine" framing).
**Status:** active. `pnpm --filter @vhyxvoid/web typecheck`/`build` both pass clean (19 routes, no count change); `pnpm --filter @vhyxvoid/web test` passes (4 files / 14 tests, up from 3/11); root suite unaffected (19 files / 99 tests). Tunnels/Invitations/My-Organizations/My-Feedback remain on the compat shim — see backlog.md.

### 2026-09-15 — Phase 2: Invitations converted to props-based table pattern

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Phase 2, third-table session)
**Context:** Following Members and API Keys, the task brief asked to convert Invitations next — but explicitly framed this one as an investigation first, not an assumed-safe repeat: check whether the disjoint-query-key staleness bug has ever actually been confirmed for Invitations (per backlog.md, it hadn't), and check which invalidation-matching mechanism its query-key factory uses before assuming either prior proof (Members' object-subset, API Keys' array-prefix) transfers.
**Investigation findings, all confirmed by reading the real code, not assumed:**
1. `InvitationsTab` is mounted inside `OrgSettingsView`'s `Tabs.Content value='invitations'`, not a standalone route — only renders once that tab is clicked (default tab is `'members'`).
2. `GET .../invitations` (`apps/api/.../account.routes.ts`, `listInvitationsQuerySchema`) accepts only an optional `status` filter — confirmed by reading the route handler directly: `orderBy: {createdAt: "desc"}, take: 100` is hardcoded server-side, no page/limit/search/sortBy param exists at all. This is a materially different backend shape than Members'/API Keys' real server-side pagination.
3. There is no query-key factory for invitations at all — `useInvitations`/`useCancelInvitation` (`useOrg.ts`) use a single plain array key, `['invitations', accountId]`, not `createQueryKeys`-based (`memberKeys`) or hand-written-but-parameterized (`apiKeyKeys`).
**The staleness-bug question, answered live/provably, not assumed either way:** a new baseline test (`useOrg.invitations.test.ts`, 2 of its 3 tests) proves that TODAY (pre-conversion), the semantic-only invalidate (`invalidateQueries({queryKey: ['invitations', accountId]})`) genuinely does NOT reach the `useSelfFetchingServerTable` compat shim's ad hoc `[tableKey, paramsString]` key — confirming the Step 5c-style diagnosis is structurally still accurate — but the existing point-fix (`invalidateQueries({queryKey: [\`invitations-\${accountId}\`]})`) DOES, via ordinary array-prefix matching against the shim's key. **Conclusion: the mutation does NOT currently leave the visible table stale, precisely because the point-fix has been doing real, necessary work this whole time** — it was never dead weight, it just hadn't been proven load-bearing with an actual test before now.
**Decision (conversion):** Added `useInvitationsTableList(accountId, params: FetchParams)` to `useOrg.ts`. Rather than introduce a new parameterized query (which would need its own factory and its own matching-mechanism proof, like Members/API Keys), it wraps the *same* `useInvitations(accountId)` query unchanged and does search/sort/pagination in a `useMemo` over its result. This means the table's real query key becomes **exactly** `['invitations', accountId]` — not a superset or a prefix, the literal same cache entry `useCancelInvitation`'s existing invalidate already targets. A third test in the same file proves this post-conversion state directly.
**Point-fix removed from both hooks that had it, not just the one the brief named:** `useCancelInvitation`'s `invalidateQueries({queryKey: [\`invitations-\${accountId}\`]})` was removed as expected — but grepping for the same ad hoc key (a discipline check, not trusting the brief's naming to be exhaustive) found `useInviteMember` (`useMembers.ts`, a different file) carried the identical call. Both removed; both are now covered by the exact-match invalidate.
**Click-to-sort — a genuinely different verification shape than Members'/API Keys'.** Those two sessions' lesson was "verify the backend schema accepts the sortBy value before shipping a column as sortable." Invitations has no backend sortBy at all, so there's no schema-mismatch risk to check — sorting is implemented entirely client-side in `useInvitationsTableList`'s comparator function. Email/Role/Expires/Status made sortable (each a directly comparable primitive or enum field); Invited-by (a nested object with two possible display fields — `fullName` or `email` — no single natural sort key) and Actions are not.
**Verified live against the real local dev backend**: invited a throwaway email on Test Corp, confirmed it appeared in `GET .../invitations` (the exact query the table now runs, default `status=PENDING`) immediately, canceled it (`204`), confirmed it disappeared from the same query immediately — matching the automated proof, no separate cache-busting call involved. Throwaway invitation's lifecycle left no residue (create → confirm → cancel → confirm gone, account back to its original 1 pending invitation).
**Browser visual verification not completed, third consecutive Phase 2 session this has happened in** (API Keys' session also couldn't complete it, for an unrelated reason that session fixed). This session: `tabs_context_mcp` returned an empty title/url immediately after navigating, and subsequent `wait`/`screenshot` calls failed with "couldn't determine which page this action targets" — stopped after 2-3 attempts per the skill's own guidance rather than keep retrying what looks like a recurring tool/extension-connectivity issue, not something in this app's code (the same pattern was already investigated and ruled unrelated to app performance in the API Keys session). Relied on the curl-based verification above, same standard as the other two Phase 2 conversions' core proofs.
**Rationale:** The brief's framing ("determine live... before assuming it needs the same fix") mattered here specifically because Invitations' architecture (no server pagination, no factory, a single unparameterized key) is different enough from Members/API Keys that assuming the same bug-and-fix shape would have been exactly the kind of unverified extrapolation this Phase 2 initiative has been deliberately avoiding at each step. Converting to an approach where the table's key becomes *identical* to (not merely matched by) the mutation's invalidate target is arguably the cleanest of the three conversions so far — there's no partial-matching mechanism to get wrong later.
**Status:** active. `pnpm --filter @vhyxvoid/web typecheck` passes clean; `pnpm --filter @vhyxvoid/web build` passes clean (19 routes, no count change, though this run took ~2.4min due to apparent file-lock contention with the concurrently-running dev server on the same `.next` directory — not a code issue, noted for whoever next runs build and dev simultaneously); `pnpm --filter @vhyxvoid/web test` passes (5 files / 17 tests, up from 4/14); root suite unaffected (19 files / 99 tests). Tunnels/My-Organizations/My-Feedback remain on the compat shim — see backlog.md.

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 2: Tunnels and My Feedback converted (both mechanical, as predicted)

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Phase 2, fourth-session, Part 1 + half of Part 2)
**Context:** Following Members/API Keys/Invitations, the task brief asked to convert Tunnels next (predicted trivial — zero mutations, confirmed by Step 5a and the closing-check session) and then investigate My Organizations/My Feedback before deciding scope for the rest.
**Tunnels — confirmed trivial, converted mechanically.** Grepped for `useMutation` across every tunnel-related hook/service file: zero results. No point-fix to investigate or remove, no invalidation-mechanism proof needed. New `useTunnelHistoryTableList(accountId, params)` (`useTunnels.ts`) keys through `tunnelKeys.history(accountId, params)` — the same hand-written, array-prefix-based factory `useTunnelHistory` already used elsewhere, just not wired into the table before. Preserved the existing `id`-from-`agentId` substitution (the backend's history response omits `id`, per decision.md 2026-09-10 "Step 5a"). All 4 backend `sortBy` options (`connectedAt`/`disconnectedAt`/`label`/`status`, `tunnelHistoryQuerySchema` in `apps/api/.../tunnel.routes.ts`) verified live against the real local dev backend — all return `200`, correct ordering confirmed for `connectedAt` asc/desc; no mismatch (Members'-style bug not repeated here). `apiKey` (nested object) and `durationMs` (client-derived, not a backend field) left non-sortable.
**My Feedback — also mechanical, and simpler than any prior conversion: a correct, already-existing hook just wasn't wired in.** `FeedbackHistoryTab.tsx` was on the compat shim, calling `feedbackService.list()` directly — but `useMyFeedback(params)` (`useFeedback.ts`), keyed via `feedbackKeys.list(params)` (the exact hand-written array-prefix factory `useSubmitFeedback` already invalidates via `feedbackKeys.lists()`), already existed, already correct, and was already used by nothing except itself. This meant submitting feedback never appeared in "My Feedback" without a manual reload — confirmed by reading `FeedbackButton.tsx`'s submit path, no reload anywhere. New `useMyFeedbackTableList(params)` is a thin `FetchParams`→`ListFeedbackParams` adapter delegating straight to `useMyFeedback` (no new query-key factory needed). All columns made non-sortable: the backend (`listMyFeedbackSchema`) has zero `sortBy` support, and — unlike Invitations — this endpoint has *real* server-side pagination (default 20/page via Prisma `skip`/`take`, not Invitations' fetch-up-to-100-then-slice-client-side shape), so client-side sorting would silently only ever sort the current page, not the full dataset.
**Verified live**, both via `curl` and (this session, working) the browser: Tunnels' active/history tables rendered real data (9 connected sessions on Test Corp) and click-to-sort on the Connected column cycled asc/desc correctly; My Feedback's submit-then-appears flow was exercised end-to-end — submitted a real "Phase 2 test entry" bug report via the UI, it appeared in the table immediately with no reload, screenshotted, then deleted via direct SQL (`DELETE FROM "Feedback" WHERE title = ...`) to leave no residue.
**Tests added**: `useTunnels.test.tsx` (2 tests, `renderHook`+`QueryClientProvider` — the id-substitution transform, and the `FetchParams.filters.status`→backend `status` param mapping) and `feedback.keys.test.ts` (2 tests, mirroring `api-key.keys.test.ts`'s array-prefix proof — `feedbackKeys.lists()` invalidates the table's parameterized query; does not invalidate an unrelated detail query).
**Rationale:** Both tables' predicted simplicity held up under investigation — Tunnels because it genuinely has no mutations, My Feedback because its "conversion" was really just discovering and wiring up a hook someone had already written correctly. Neither needed a new invalidation-mechanism proof from scratch (Tunnels has none to prove; My Feedback's `feedbackKeys` factory is the same array-prefix shape already proven for `apiKeyKeys`, though still given its own dedicated test per this initiative's standing discipline of not assuming a proof transfers between factory instances).
**Status:** active. See the following entry for My Organizations, which did *not* turn out to be mechanical.

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 2: My Organizations — accountKeys.all invalidation was a dead target, fixed (the "something novel" this session's brief anticipated)

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Phase 2, fourth session, Part 2)
**Context:** Investigating `MyAccountsTable.tsx` before converting it (backed by `GET /account/me`, confirmed to have zero pagination/sort/search support server-side — same shape as Invitations) surfaced something the brief's own framing explicitly anticipated might happen: not just an unconverted table, but a genuinely separate, previously-undiagnosed bug.
**Investigation findings:** Five mutation hooks across two files — `useCreateOrg`, `useUpdateProfile`, `useRenameOrg` (`useOrg.ts`) and `useTransferOwnership`, `useAcceptInvitation` (`useMembers.ts`) — all called `queryClient.invalidateQueries({queryKey: accountKeys.all})`. `accountKeys` was `createQueryKeys<{userId?: string}>('accounts')` (`account.keys.ts`), so `accountKeys.all` evaluated to `['accounts']`. But grepping for every real `useQuery` reader of "my organizations" data found exactly one: `useMe`/`useMyAccounts`/`useMyProfile` (`useMe.ts`), all sharing one cache entry keyed `meKeys.detail()` = `['me', 'detail']`. `['accounts']` and `['me', 'detail']` share no common prefix — React Query's default invalidation (array-prefix matching against the *actual* key) never matched. `accountKeys` had **zero** real query-backed readers anywhere in the codebase — confirmed by grep — only these five dead invalidation call sites.
**How this had gone unnoticed:** `CreateOrgDialog.tsx` masked it for the one case that would otherwise have been the most visible (creating an org and not seeing it appear) with a `window.location.reload()`, whose own comment even said "can be removed once we have global state management in place" — the real fix (a correct query key) was available the whole time, just not applied. Renaming an org, transferring ownership, and accepting an invitation had no such mask: the sidebar org switcher and "My organizations" table stayed genuinely stale after any of those three actions until a manual browser refresh. A stale comment in `VerticalMenu.tsx` ("Reads from accountKeys.list() cache — populated by useMe") is itself evidence this bug predates this session — the sidebar was refactored onto `meKeys` at some point without updating the mutations that were supposed to keep it fresh, and without updating that comment.
**Options considered:** (1) fix only the mutation invalidation targets and leave `accountKeys` in place as unused dead code; (2) fix the invalidation targets and delete `accountKeys` entirely, since nothing reads through it; (3) also remove `CreateOrgDialog.tsx`'s now-unnecessary reload, or leave it as defensive redundancy.
**Decision:** Option 2 for the dead export (deleted `accountKeys` from `account.keys.ts` — confirmed zero remaining references beyond the five now-fixed call sites and the one stale comment, which was also corrected). Removed `CreateOrgDialog.tsx`'s `window.location.reload()` (not kept as "defensive" — a working invalidation makes a full-page reload strictly worse UX for no safety benefit; keeping a workaround for a bug that's actually fixed just re-hides future regressions instead of letting the test suite catch them). All five call sites now invalidate `meKeys.detail()`.
**Proof, not just reasoning**: `account.keys.test.ts` gained a new `describe` block, two tests — (1) `['accounts']` (the old, literal value of the now-deleted `accountKeys.all` — written as a literal array rather than importing the deleted symbol, so the regression guard survives the cleanup) does NOT invalidate `meKeys.detail()`'s cache entry, proving the bug was real; (2) `meKeys.detail()` (the fix) DOES invalidate the same entry. **Live browser verification**: renamed "Test Corp" → "Test Corp Renamed" via Settings — the sidebar org switcher updated immediately (no reload triggered by the app), then navigated to the Dashboard and confirmed the "My organizations" table also showed the new name immediately, then clicked the Organization column header to confirm click-to-sort re-sorts correctly. Reverted the name back to "Test Corp" afterward; account left in its original state. (The pre-fix stale-sidebar symptom was not separately reproduced-then-reverted before applying the fix — the automated baseline test above is the proof of the pre-fix behavior; the live check was performed post-fix to confirm the fix itself works end-to-end, which is the higher-value check once the bug's mechanism is already proven by an automated test.)
**Conversion**: New `useMyAccountsTableList(params)` (`useMe.ts`) wraps the *same* `useMyAccounts()` query (sharing `meKeys.detail()`'s cache entry with `useMe`/`useMyProfile`) and does search/role-filter/sort/pagination client-side in a `useMemo` — same exact-key-match shape as Invitations' hook, no separate parameterized cache entry. `MyAccountsTable.tsx` converted off the compat shim onto this hook. The `accountId`-accessor "Organization" column was renamed to `accountName` (id and data now match what's actually displayed/sorted, the same class of fix as Members' pilot `'member'`→`'name'` rename) and made sortable, along with Role and Joined; Actions stays non-sortable.
**Rationale:** This is exactly the "something novel" the brief's own framing anticipated and asked to be treated with full rigor rather than rushed — a wrong invalidation target is a different, arguably worse bug than the disjoint-ad-hoc-table-key pattern every other Phase 2 conversion has fixed, because it has nothing to do with the table component at all and would have persisted even after a naive "just convert the table" pass that didn't also check what the *other* consumers of `meKeys.detail()` (the sidebar) were relying on. Finding it required checking not just "does this table have a real query-key-backed hook" but "does a real query-key-backed hook already exist that isn't being used, and do the mutations that are *supposed* to feed it actually reach it" — a slightly different, and in this case more load-bearing, question than the previous three sessions needed to ask.
**Status:** active. All five call sites fixed; `accountKeys` deleted; `CreateOrgDialog.tsx` reload removed; `VerticalMenu.tsx` comment corrected. `pnpm --filter @vhyxvoid/web typecheck`/`build` both pass clean (19 routes, unchanged). `pnpm --filter @vhyxvoid/web test` passes 7 files / 23 tests (up from 5/17 at session start — Tunnels' hook test, `feedback.keys.test.ts`, and 2 tests appended to `account.keys.test.ts`). Root suite unaffected (19 files / 99 tests). `useSelfFetchingServerTable.ts` deleted — after this session, every `GenericServerTable` consumer (Members, API Keys, Tunnels, Invitations, My Organizations, My Feedback) is on the real props-based contract; confirmed zero remaining importers by grep before deleting. Phase 2 of `TABLE_API_ARCHITECTURE_COMPARISON.md`'s recommendation is now fully complete for VhyxVoid (kautilyan-admin's own Phase 2 session remains tracked separately in backlog.md).

### 2026-09-16 — kautilyan-admin/kautilyan-frontend `.claude`-style scaffolding: add-only, not replace

**Decided by:** joint (user answered via AskUserQuestion after investigation)
**Context:** Asked to set up a VhyxVoid-style `.claude/` documentation system (context.md/decision.md/session_update.md/backlog.md/CLAUDE.md bootstrap) in kautilyan-admin and kautilyan-frontend, on the brief's own stated assumption that "most likely" neither repo had any existing project-documentation infrastructure (per `TABLE_API_ARCHITECTURE_COMPARISON.md` not having mentioned one). That assumption was wrong for both repos, confirmed by direct investigation (two research forks, one per repo, full-file reads): kautilyan-admin has a real, 22-session-deep root-level system (`CLAUDE.md` as a conventions doc, `DECISIONS.md`, `SESSION_UPDATES.md`, tracked in git) and kautilyan-frontend has an even closer cousin of VhyxVoid's own system (`CLAUDE.md` as a genuine bootstrap file, `decisions.md` — 77 entries, `session_update.md` — 73 entries, all deliberately gitignored/local-only). Neither matches VhyxVoid's exact shape (different schema, different ordering, no context.md-equivalent in either, no backlog.md-equivalent in either) but both are real, mature, actively-maintained systems, not stubs or abandoned attempts.
**Options considered:** (a) add only what's missing (a new context.md audit + a new backlog.md per repo, leaving each repo's existing decision/session logs and CLAUDE.md completely untouched in format/location/gitignore-status, with only a minimal "read this too" pointer added to each CLAUDE.md); (b) full restructure to VhyxVoid's literal shape (rename/reformat the existing logs into VhyxVoid's schema, move everything under `.claude/`, replace each CLAUDE.md with a pure bootstrap pointer); (c) middle ground — add context.md+backlog.md and relocate (but not reformat) the existing logs into `.claude/` under VhyxVoid's filenames.
**Decision:** (a). Two fork agents each wrote a genuine from-scratch context.md (kautilyan-admin: 173 lines; kautilyan-frontend: 213 lines, both with real Known-Risks findings sourced from direct code reading, not just restating the existing docs) and a backlog.md seeded with real small items, plus a minimal prepend/addition to each repo's CLAUDE.md. New files placed at each repo's root (matching where the existing sibling docs already live, not under `.claude/`, since `.claude/` in both repos currently holds only harness config). kautilyan-admin's new files are git-tracked (matching its existing docs' tracked status); kautilyan-frontend's new files were added to its existing `.gitignore` block (matching its existing docs' deliberate local-only convention). No existing file's content, format, or location was changed beyond the two CLAUDE.md pointer edits — `DECISIONS.md`/`SESSION_UPDATES.md`/`decisions.md`/`session_update.md` were not touched, not even to append a new entry for this session, since the user's chosen option was to leave them "exactly as-is."
**Rationale:** The task brief itself says the goal is to replicate VhyxVoid's *structure, discipline, and bootstrap instructions* — not to copy VhyxVoid-specific content or force VhyxVoid's literal filenames/schema onto a system that already has its own real, working discipline (kautilyan-frontend's is arguably more rigorous in places — verification-heavy prose entries, a distinctive sibling-repo git policy, named quality gates). Restructuring mature, real, gitignored-by-design history into a different shape risked breaking each repo's own established conventions (tooling/session memory that references these files by name) for no functional gain, when the one genuinely missing piece in both — a context.md-equivalent architecture/risk audit — could be added cleanly on its own.
**Status:** active. Notable finding surfaced by this pass, not previously documented anywhere: kautilyan-admin's `views/auth/Login.tsx` logs the plaintext password to the browser console on every login and sets both auth tokens via non-httpOnly cookies — now kautilyan-admin/context.md Known Risks #1, flagged for the user directly (not fixed — this session was documentation-only, no functional code changes made in either repo, per the brief's explicit constraint).

### 2026-09-16 — apps/web committed as a clean baseline, reversing the earlier "stay uncommitted until MUI is fully removed" practice

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed), Claude Code (implementation)
**Context:** Since `apps/web` moved into this monorepo (2026-09-09), the entire working tree had been developed and iterated on without ever being committed — the practical effect of the standing assumption that it should stay that way until the VhyxUI migration's remaining MUI holdouts (blank-layout-pages' illustration/responsive-layout code, `NotificationBell`/`FeedbackButton`, and a few table-view files that still import MUI directly) were fully cleared out, so an eventual archive/cleanup pass wouldn't need to pick through commit history for code that was always meant to be temporary. By this session, that had grown to 625 uncommitted files spanning nine Phase 1/Phase 2 sessions' worth of real, working changes (the VhyxUI migration itself, `@vhyx/api-kit` adoption, and the full `GenericServerTable` props-based conversion) with zero git history to review any of it against.
**Options considered:** (1) keep waiting until MUI is fully gone, as originally intended; (2) commit now as one large single commit; (3) commit now, organized into multiple logical, reviewable slices roughly mirroring the app's own architecture.
**Decision:** Option 3. Before committing anything, verified directly (not assumed) that `apps/web/.gitignore` actually excludes `.env`/`.env.local` (confirmed via `git check-ignore -v` against the real files present on disk) and grepped the whole tree for hardcoded secret-like literals, common credential prefixes, and the specific historical leak pattern this project has hit before (`fetchData.ts`'s stray `console.log` of a real secret, removed 2026-09-15) — found nothing blocking, only one minor unrelated hygiene note (`utils/serverUtility.ts` logs a cookie-token `Promise` object, not an actual secret value). Committed in 9 sequential commits: config/tooling + static assets; shared app foundation (i18n, config values, utils, hooks, contexts); the vendored Vuexy chrome (`@core`/`@layouts`/`@menu`); the api/ data layer (with its co-located tests); shared table infra and UI libs (`libs/`, `components/vhyxui-shims`); auth views; organization views (members/API-keys/billing/tunnels); profile/feedback/notification views; and finally the Next.js route tree + middleware. Test files were kept with their source rather than split into a separate "tests" commit, since a hook and its test are one coherent unit, not two. `.claude/backlog.md` (also gitignored, never previously tracked) was committed separately in the same session, alongside this session's `context.md`/`decision.md` updates — see the immediately preceding commit.
**Rationale:** The original "stay uncommitted" premise made sense while the tree was actively being reshaped screen-by-screen and any given day's state might be mid-migration; it stops making sense once the practical effect is nine sessions of real work with no history to check regressions against, no way to `git blame`/`git log -p` any file, and no baseline for the archiving/MUI-removal work that's coming next to be reviewed against. Committing now — organized, not as one opaque blob — gives that upcoming work a real diff to review instead of a diff against nothing. The remaining MUI usage (the deliberately-deferred spots plus a few table-view files) is now itself a reviewable, blameable piece of the history rather than lost inside a single "add everything" commit, which is exactly what makes it possible to clean up later without losing the ability to see why any given line is there.
**Status:** active. `pnpm --filter @vhyxvoid/web typecheck`/`build` both pass clean at the final committed state (19 routes, unchanged). `git status` shows nothing untracked under `apps/web` — fully committed.

### 2026-09-16 — apps/web/archived/: dead-weight convention set up, genuinely-unused material moved out of the tracked tree

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed), Claude Code (implementation)
**Context:** With `apps/web` now committed as a clean baseline (see the entry directly above), the next step before the real MUI/Vuexy removal work is a safe first pass: move confirmed-dead material out of the tracked tree, as a separate step from actually touching any live component. The brief offered a choice between a root-level `archived/web/` and a package-scoped `apps/web/archived/`, deferring to whatever precedent already existed.
**Precedent check:** This repo already has two `archived/` conventions — a root-level `archived/` (cross-cutting old material: a dead standalone agent, an old audit doc) and a package-scoped `apps/api/archived/` (dead pre-refactor code specific to that one package, gitignored, local-only). Since everything in scope here is apps/web-specific, `apps/web/archived/` was the closer match. Turned out to already exist on disk, with real pre-existing content (`(adminpanel)/`, `(auth)/`, `package.json`, `test.ts` — an even older Next.js route-group structure, predating the current `src/app/[locale]/...` layout, unrelated to and untouched by this session) — confirmed it was never git-tracked and already fully covered by the repo's existing blanket `archived` gitignore rule (bare pattern, matches at any depth, verified via `git check-ignore -v` against the actual `apps/web/archived` path) before adding anything to it. No `.gitignore` edit was needed; Part 1's "add it to .gitignore" was already satisfied by existing infrastructure.
**Investigation methodology, and a real methodology bug caught mid-session:** For `public/images`, an initial basename-only grep pass produced false negatives on generically-numbered files (`avatars/1.png` and `characters/1.png` both being real, live, different files meant a naive `grep -F "1.png"` check incorrectly marked `characters-with-objects/1.png` as "used" — it just happened to share a basename with two different real matches). Redone with full relative-path matching (`illustrations/characters-with-objects/1.png`, not just `1.png`), which is the version actually used to decide what moved. For `@core`/`@menu`, a second methodology gap surfaced: `@menu`'s own components are wired together through barrel re-exports and relative imports (`@menu/vertical-menu` resolves to `vertical-menu/index.tsx`, which re-exports `Menu`/`SubMenu`/`MenuItem`/etc.), so a direct-import-path grep alone produced a long, wrong list of apparent "0 importer" files that are all actually reachable transitively. Caught by spot-checking one (`@menu/vertical-menu/index.tsx`, confirmed live via `VerticalMenu.tsx`'s actual import) before trusting the pattern — **no `@menu` file was moved this session**, per the brief's own exclusion and because the barrel-blind-spot risk was real and demonstrated, not hypothetical. Where `@core` candidates were kept (customizer's SVG icons, the MUI wrapper trio, option-menu, custom-inputs), each was checked two ways — direct import-path grep AND a case-insensitive whole-tree grep for the exported identifier's bare name — specifically to catch a hidden barrel; confirmed no `@core/svg/index.ts` or `@core/components/mui/index.ts` barrel exists, so the direct-path checks for those were safe.
**What moved** (all verified zero-importer before moving, all still available on disk under `apps/web/archived/`, none deleted outright): 180 `public/images` files the Vuexy template ships but this app never references (whole unused demo categories — ecommerce/academy/kanban/logistics product shots, front-pages marketing assets, payment-card icons, misc file-type icons — plus most of the stock avatar/character/logo variants, keeping only the one of each actually used, plus the two unused two-step-verification auth illustrations and the stock create-next-app SVGs); the Vuexy customizer/settings-drawer component and the 8 SVG icons it alone imported (confirmed dead — only a stale doc comment in `themeConfig.ts` still mentions it); 3 unused MUI component wrappers (`Autocomplete`, `Badge`, `TabList` — the first matches context.md's already-documented "zero live Autocomplete usage" finding); the `option-menu` component; the `custom-inputs` Horizontal/Image/Vertical component trio (its `types.ts` stays — still referenced by `libs/theme/types.ts`'s MUI theme-augmentation declarations, which only need the prop *types*, not the components themselves); `libs/table/TableAction.tsx` (`BulkActions`, backlog.md's flagged item — turned out to have zero importers at all, not just unmigrated MUI, so archiving fully resolves it rather than leaving it for a future MUI-migration pass); and the three orphaned `views/org/` member dialogs (`ChangeRoleDialog`/`InviteMemberDialog`/`TransferOwnershipDialog`) that predate and duplicate the live `views/members/` versions `MembersTable.tsx` actually imports.
**What was checked and deliberately left in place**: `@core/theme/overrides/*` — even where the underlying MUI component (e.g. `Rating`, `Fab`, `Timeline`) is never rendered anywhere in this app, these overrides are wired together as one theme config object via `overrides/index.ts`; touching them means editing shared theme-construction code, which is the real removal work this step is explicitly not. All of `@menu` — barrel-export blind spot, see above. `@core/svg/Logo.tsx` — used by `libs/layout/shared/Logo.tsx` and `AcceptInvitationView.tsx`. The four remaining `@core/components/mui/*` wrappers (`Avatar`, `Chip`, `IconButton`, `TextField`) — each has real importers. `custom-inputs/types.ts` — still imported. `views/org/AcceptInvitationView.tsx`, `CreateOrgDialog.tsx`, `InvitationsTab.tsx`, `MyAccountsTable.tsx`, `OrgSettingsView.tsx`, and the `api-keys`/`billing`/`tunnels` subdirectories — all confirmed live (several converted to the props-based table pattern in this same week's Phase 2 sessions).
**Follow-on fix required**: `apps/web/tsconfig.json`'s `include` (`**/*.ts`, `**/*.tsx`) and `.eslintrc.js`'s `ignorePatterns` had no exclusion for `archived/` — once real `.ts`/`.tsx` files landed there, `tsc --noEmit` immediately failed on their now-broken relative/alias imports (e.g. `customizer/index.tsx` importing `@core/svg/SkinDefault`, which no longer resolves from its old location). Added `"archived"` to `tsconfig.json`'s `exclude` and `'archived/**'` to `.eslintrc.js`'s `ignorePatterns`. `vitest.config.ts` needed no change — its `include` is scoped to `src/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}`, and `archived/` sits outside `src/`.
**Rationale:** The whole point of this step being "separate" and "safe" from the real MUI/Vuexy removal work is that it should be provably inert — verified by running the actual gates (typecheck, build, full test suite) after moving, not just after grepping. All three passed clean at 19 routes / 23 tests, unchanged, which is the real evidence nothing load-bearing moved, not just the grep methodology.
**Status:** active. Committed as 3 commits (images; `@core` components/svg; `TableAction.tsx` + `views/org` dialogs) plus this doc update. `backlog.md`'s `TableAction.tsx` item removed (resolved, more completely than originally scoped — it wasn't just unmigrated, it was unused). `apps/web/archived/` is real, on-disk, gitignored via the existing blanket rule, and now holds this session's moved material alongside its pre-existing unrelated content.

### 2026-09-16 — Fresh MUI/Vuexy dependency audit: corrects the previous session's "all four `@core/components/mui/*` wrappers have real importers" claim, and finds a whole orphaned nav-chrome generation the barrel-blind-spot caution had never actually traced

**Decided by:** Claude Code (investigation/planning only — no code removed this session, per the brief)
**Context:** Asked for a from-scratch `@mui/*` import audit (not a re-read of the months-old gap-analysis report), since Steps 1-6 and the Phase 1/2 table refactor have moved the picture since. Grepped all of `apps/web/src` (excluding `archived/`) for `@mui` import lines: 94 files, 209 import lines.
**Methodology:** For every file whose only visible importer was itself or another already-suspect file, traced the import chain outward by path (not by bare identifier name, which produces false positives across same-named sibling files — e.g. `views/members/ChangeRoleDialog.tsx` vs `views/org/ChangeRoleDialog.tsx` from the 2026-09-16 archive session) until it either reached a real route (`app/**/page.tsx`) or terminated in nothing. This is the same rigor the previous session used for images/`@core`, extended to cover the two things that session explicitly declined to trace: the full `libs/layout/`/`@layouts` shell stack, and all of `@menu` (previously excluded wholesale over a demonstrated barrel-export blind spot in a *direct-path-only* grep — the blind spot was real, but the fix is tracing the barrel's own re-exports outward one hop at a time, not avoiding the directory forever).
**Finding 1 — the previous session's "all four `@core/components/mui/*` wrappers have real importers" claim was wrong for 3 of 4.** `Avatar.tsx` and `IconButton.tsx` have zero importers anywhere (only a commented-out reference in `utils/utility.ts`). `Chip.tsx`'s only importer, `libs/components/GenerateMenu.tsx`, is itself dead (see Finding 3) — so `Chip.tsx` is transitively dead too. Only `TextField.tsx` is genuinely live, via `CreateOrgDialog.tsx` and `FeedbackButton.tsx`. That prior entry's claim was a bucket assumption ("the remaining mui wrappers... each has real importers") never checked file-by-file; this session's per-file check is the correction.
**Finding 2 — an entire orphaned dashboard-shell generation, never cleaned up after Step 3.** Step 3 (2026-09-10) rebuilt the dashboard shell on VhyxUI as `libs/layout/vhyxui/{DashboardShell,DashboardSidebar,DashboardTopbar,DashboardFooter,DashboardSidebarNav,ModeDropdown,UserDropdown}.tsx` — confirmed the sole shell actually wired into `app/[locale]/(dashboard)/layout.tsx`. The OLD shell it replaced was never deleted and has zero live importers today: `libs/layout/vertical/{Navbar,NavbarContent,Navigation,VerticalMenu,Footer,FooterContent}.tsx` (only `NavToggle.tsx` in that directory survives, still used by the new `DashboardTopbar`), `libs/layout/shared/{ModeDropdown,UserDropdown}.tsx` (only `Logo.tsx` survives, used by the new `DashboardSidebar`), and the whole `@layouts/VerticalLayout.tsx` subtree it pulled in (`@layouts/components/vertical/{Navbar,Footer,LayoutContent}.tsx`, `@layouts/styles/vertical/{StyledHeader,StyledFooter,StyledContentWrapper}.tsx`, `@layouts/styles/shared/StyledMain.tsx`). `@layouts/LayoutWrapper.tsx` and `@layouts/BlankLayout.tsx` are unaffected — confirmed they only render whatever `ReactElement` is passed to them and never import any of the dead subtree themselves; neither has any `@mui` import at all.
**Finding 3 — the `@menu` vertical-menu *rendering* subsystem is entirely orphaned too; the 2026-09-16 barrel-blind-spot caution was correct to be cautious but the actual answer, traced properly, is "dead."** The new `DashboardSidebarNav.tsx` is a full hand-built VhyxUI replacement (`@vhyxui/react`'s `Badge`, no `@menu` component import at all beyond a comment noting its Vuexy origin). Tracing the `@menu/vertical-menu` barrel's (`@menu/vertical-menu/index.tsx`) actual consumers outward: `types/menuTypes.ts`, `libs/components/GenerateMenu.tsx`, `data/navigation/verticalMenuData.tsx`, and `libs/layout/vertical/{VerticalMenu,Navigation}.tsx` are its only importers, and every one of those is itself unreachable from any route (the last two are Finding 2's dead shell; `menuUtils.tsx`/`verticalMenuData.tsx`/`GenerateMenu.tsx`/`menuTypes.ts` chain into each other and nothing else). This makes the whole `@menu/components/vertical-menu/*` tree, `@menu/vertical-menu/index.tsx`, `@menu/components/RouterLink.tsx`, `@menu/styles/vertical/*`, `@menu/styles/Styled{Backdrop,MenuIcon,MenuLabel,MenuPrefix,MenuSectionLabel,MenuSuffix,SubMenuContent}.tsx`, `@menu/svg/*`, `@menu/utils/{menuUtils,menuClasses}.ts`, and `@core/styles/vertical/{menuItemStyles,menuSectionStyles,navigationCustomStyles}.ts` dead. What's genuinely live in `@menu` — confirmed by DashboardSidebar.tsx's real imports — is only `@menu/hooks/{useVerticalNav,useMediaQuery}.tsx`, `@menu/defaultConfigs.ts`, `@menu/contexts/verticalNavContext.tsx`, and `@menu/types.ts` (a small shared types file both the live hooks and the dead component tree import from — it stays, it's not MUI-coupled and not the blind spot). None of this reverses the "don't touch `@menu`" *action* from the 2026-09-16 session (that session correctly declined to move anything without doing this trace) — it corrects the *belief* that doing this trace safely wasn't tractable in one pass.
**Finding 4 — several live, MUI-rendering view files that `context.md`'s "only explicitly deferred: blank-layout-pages illustration + NotificationBell/FeedbackButton" framing missed.** `views/org/{CreateOrgDialog,MyAccountsTable,billing/BillingView}.tsx`, `views/org/AcceptInvitationView.tsx` (trivial — one `styled('img')` illustration wrapper, everything else already VhyxUI), `views/profile/FeedbackHistoryTab.tsx` + `views/feedback/FeedbackDetailDrawer.tsx` (paired — the drawer is only opened from the history tab), `contexts/FeedbackContext.tsx` (a real, global, app-wide confirm/alert `Dialog` wired into `providers.client.tsx`, consumed via `Confirmation.tsx`'s `useFeedback()` — small but touches every route), and `views/pages/NotFound.tsx` (mostly the same illustration pattern as blank-layout-pages, but its `Button`/`Typography` usage is separable and trivial). These were never flagged as deferred anywhere — they were apparently just missed when Step 5's table-pattern conversion pass moved on.
**Finding 5 — a handful of standalone dead files unrelated to the shell/menu, found by the same per-file check**: `views/members/members.columns.tsx` (superseded by `MembersTable.tsx`'s own local `buildColumns()`, same "orphaned parallel file" shape as the `views/org/` dialog trio archived 2026-09-16), `views/auth/VerifyEmailForm.tsx` (only reachable via a fully commented-out import in `verify-email/page.tsx`), `utils/dropzone.tsx`, `libs/components/{CreatableAutocomplete,Loader}.tsx`, `libs/dialogs/DialogCloseButton.tsx`, `libs/table/TablePageSize.tsx`, `@core/styles/stepper.ts`, `libs/stepper-dot/index.tsx`.
**Illustration-panel re-assessment (blank-layout-pages, deferred since Step 2):** checked whether VhyxUI has grown any breakpoint/media-query/responsive primitive since Step 2 that would make replacing `useTheme()`/`useMediaQuery()`/`theme.breakpoints`/`theme.spacing` more tractable now — grepped `VhyxUI/packages/react/src` directly (the actual sibling repo on disk) for `breakpoint`/`useMediaQuery`/`responsive`: zero hits. No change since Step 2; still requires hand-rolling a CSS-based responsive replacement plus reworking each illustration page's `useTheme()` calls (12 in `Login.tsx` alone). Not more tractable than it looked in Step 2, not less.
**Status:** active — investigation and planning only, nothing removed this session. Full file-by-file findings and the phased removal plan were reported to the user directly; the next removal session should read this entry and the report before re-deriving any of it, and should re-verify with a fresh grep immediately before deleting anything (this audit is a snapshot, not a guarantee against drift between sessions).

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 0 executed: the confirmed-100%-dead nav-shell/@menu/standalone-file inventory from the audit above, deleted

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed), Claude Code (implementation)
**Context:** Phase 0 of the removal plan from the audit above — pure deletion of already-verified-unreferenced files, zero restyling, zero behavior change. Before touching anything, re-verified every item in the audit's file list against the current tree using the same import-path-tracing discipline that caught the barrel-blind-spot in the 2026-09-16 archive session (path-based grep to a real route, not bare-identifier grep). Nothing had shifted since the audit — `apps/web` was clean (`git status` empty) and every file traced to the same conclusion.
**What moved to `apps/web/archived/`** (real component/pattern shape, kept for reference, same convention as the two prior archive commits): the orphaned pre-Step-3 dashboard shell (`libs/layout/vertical/{Navbar,NavbarContent,Navigation,VerticalMenu,Footer,FooterContent}.tsx`, `libs/layout/shared/{ModeDropdown,UserDropdown}.tsx`, `@layouts/VerticalLayout.tsx` and its `components`/`styles` subtree); the entire dead `@menu` vertical-menu rendering subsystem (`@menu/vertical-menu/`, `@menu/components/{vertical-menu/*,RouterLink.tsx}`, `@menu/styles/*`, `@menu/svg/*`, `@menu/utils/*`, `@core/styles/vertical/*`, plus its three dead-adjacent consumers `types/menuTypes.ts`/`libs/components/GenerateMenu.tsx`/`data/navigation/verticalMenuData.tsx`); `views/members/members.columns.tsx`; `libs/components/{CreatableAutocomplete,Loader}.tsx`; `@core/styles/stepper.ts` + `libs/stepper-dot/{index.tsx,styles.module.css}` (a stepper-dot pairing never wired into any live component, not just currently unused); and the three now-fully-dead `@core/components/mui/*` wrappers (`Avatar.tsx`, `Chip.tsx` — its only importer was the also-just-removed `GenerateMenu.tsx` — and `IconButton.tsx`). `TextField.tsx` is the one wrapper that's genuinely live (`CreateOrgDialog.tsx`/`FeedbackButton.tsx`) and was untouched.
**Deleted outright, no archive copy** (judgment call — trivial, fully generic, and either superseded by a still-live equivalent or offering no conceivable future reference value): `views/auth/VerifyEmailForm.tsx` (superseded by the live `VerifyEmailView.tsx` doing the identical job — keeping a second copy of the same pattern adds nothing), `utils/dropzone.tsx` (48 lines of generic `react-dropzone` glue, no design decisions), `libs/dialogs/DialogCloseButton.tsx` (29-line trivial styled one-liner), `libs/table/TablePageSize.tsx` (23-line wrapper, superseded by `GenericServerTable`'s built-in pagination controls). Everything else went to `archived/` on the same "when in doubt, keep the physical copy" default this repo has used consistently since the convention started — these four were the only ones judged to have zero reference value even as an archived curiosity.
**A gitignore-vs-tracked-file gotcha caught mid-session**: the first attempt used `git mv <src> apps/web/archived/...` directly, mirroring how the files were moved conceptually — but `git mv` staged these as tracked renames into `archived/`, ignoring the blanket `archived` gitignore rule entirely. Root cause: gitignore only governs *untracked* paths; a `git mv` of an already-tracked file keeps it tracked regardless of the destination's ignore status. The 2026-09-16 archive session's images/SVGs never hit this because they were plain filesystem copies that were never `git add`ed at the destination — this session's files were already tracked at their source, so the same "just copy the bytes" mental model needed a different mechanical implementation: `cp` to the destination (creates a new, correctly-ignored, untracked file) followed by `git rm` on the original tracked path (stages the deletion, leaves the working-tree copy already sitting safely in `archived/`). Caught by inspecting `git status`/`git ls-files` on the first batch before repeating the mistake across the other ~50 files; the first 16-file batch was un-staged and corrected before any commit.
**What's still live and untouched, re-confirmed**: `libs/layout/vertical/NavToggle.tsx`, `libs/layout/shared/Logo.tsx`, `@layouts/{LayoutWrapper,BlankLayout}.tsx`, `@menu/hooks/{useVerticalNav,useMediaQuery}.tsx` + `defaultConfigs.ts` + `contexts/verticalNavContext.tsx` + `types.ts`, `@core/components/mui/TextField.tsx`.
**Result**: `@mui` import surface dropped from 94 files / 209 import lines (the audit's baseline, re-confirmed unchanged at the start of this session) to **71 files / 147 import lines** — a real, meaningful cut, entirely from code that rendered nothing. `pnpm --filter @vhyxvoid/web typecheck`/`build`/`test` all pass clean: 19 routes (unchanged), 7 test files / 23 tests (unchanged). `eslint` was also run for completeness (not one of the requested gates) — it reports 250 pre-existing problems, all in files untouched by this session (`libs/ui/*.jsx`, `utils/copyToClipboard.ts`, `views/org/billing/BillingView.tsx`'s unused vars, etc.) and unrelated to anything removed here; not this session's regression, not fixed here since it's out of scope.
**Rationale**: Same standard as every archive/deletion pass in this repo — verified by running the actual gates after moving, not just after grepping, because that's the only real evidence nothing load-bearing moved.
**Status:** active. Committed as 3 commits (the dashboard-shell subtree; the `@menu` subsystem + its dead-adjacent consumers; the standalone files — archived and deleted-outright together, since git tracks both the same way, only their destination on disk differs). `backlog.md`'s Phase-0 pointer item removed (resolved). Phase 0 of the removal plan is complete; Phases 1-4 (small live-component restyles, org/profile view migrations, NotificationBell/FeedbackButton rebuild, the illustration-panel problem) remain open, unchanged from the audit's plan.

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 1 executed: NotFound/AuthGuard/AcceptInvitationView/ScrollToTop migrated, and a real Turbopack SSR build break caught and fixed

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed), Claude Code (implementation)
**Context:** Phase 1 of the removal plan — the four standalone, no-theme-dependency restyles identified in the audit. Re-verified each file's actual current MUI usage fresh (not trusting the audit's description) before touching anything, per the same discipline as every phase so far.
**Re-verification findings, each confirmed exactly as the audit predicted**: `NotFound.tsx` — `Button`/`Typography` are genuinely separable; its `MaskImg` illustration (`useTheme()`/`useMediaQuery()`/`theme.breakpoints`/`theme.direction`) is real theme coupling, same shape as blank-layout-pages, correctly left alone. `AuthGuard.tsx` — exactly one `CircularProgress`, no other MUI. `AcceptInvitationView.tsx` — exactly one MUI touchpoint, `styled('img')` for its illustration wrapper, and — checked directly, not assumed — that `styled()` call has zero theme coupling (only fixed pixel values: `maxWidth`, `width`, `marginBottom`), unlike `NotFound`'s `MaskImg` (which reads `theme.spacing()`/breakpoints/direction). This is the actual distinction the audit was pointing at: not every `styled('img')` in this app is the "illustration-panel problem" — some are just a leftover MUI utility call with no real theme dependency at all, and this one was safe to convert to a plain `<img>` with Tailwind classes and drop the MUI import entirely.
**What changed**: `Button`/`Typography` → `@vhyxui/react`'s `Button` + the `@/components/vhyxui-shims` `Typography` shim in `NotFound.tsx` (matching `VerifyEmailSentView.tsx`'s established `<Button asChild><Link>...</Link></Button>` pattern for the "Back To Home" link-as-button). `CircularProgress` → `<Spinner size='lg' />` in `AuthGuard.tsx` (`lg` chosen as the closer visual match to MUI's default 40px `CircularProgress`, vs. the `md`/32px used for inline spinners elsewhere — this is a full-viewport loading moment, not an inline one). `AcceptInvitationView.tsx`'s `Illustration = styled('img')(...)` removed entirely, replaced with a plain `<img className='max-is-[280px] is-full mbe-6' />` using this app's existing logical-property Tailwind convention. Dashboard layout's inline `<Button variant='contained' className='is-10 bs-10 rounded-full p-0 ...'>` → `<Button iconOnly aria-label='Scroll to top' icon={...} className='rounded-full' />`, using the established `iconOnly` pattern from `Register.tsx`/`Login.tsx`'s social-login buttons — confirmed VhyxUI's `--vhyx-size-md` token (the `iconOnly` default) is exactly 2.5rem/40px, an exact match for the original's `is-10 bs-10`, so no explicit `size` prop was needed.
**A real bug caught mid-session, not a pre-existing one**: after the edits, `pnpm build` failed — `Error: Failed to collect configuration for /[locale]/profile`, `TypeError: (0, i.createContext) is not a function`, reproducible from a clean `.next` (not a stale-cache artifact). Root cause, isolated by checking which files in the app import from `@vhyxui/react` without their own `'use client'` directive: `libs/table/RowAction.tsx` and `libs/components/TablePaginationComponent.tsx` also do this and are fine, because they're only ever reached from inside an already-`'use client'` subtree (their nearest ancestor establishes the boundary). `app/[locale]/(dashboard)/layout.tsx` is different — it's a genuine Server Component with no `'use client'` of its own, and it's the actual Server/Client boundary-crossing point for this whole route segment (it does `await getSystemMode()`, which is why it can't just get a blanket `'use client'` added). Importing `@vhyxui/react`'s `Button` directly at that file's top level broke Turbopack's RSC/SSR bundling in a way that MUI's `Button` (the thing it replaced, which worked fine in the exact same spot) did not. **Fix**: extracted the button into its own small, explicit client component — `@core/components/scroll-to-top/ScrollToTopButton.tsx`, with its own `'use client'` — and import *that* into the layout instead of importing `@vhyxui/react` directly. This is the standard, unambiguous Next.js pattern for this exact situation (an explicit client-component file, not an implicit boundary inferred from import position), and it fixed the build immediately, confirmed on a from-scratch `.next` rebuild.
**Functional check**: no Chrome browser extension available in this environment, so no live browser walkthrough. Instead: started the real local dev backend (`LOCAL_DEV_BACKEND.md`'s standard invocation, port 9000) and `apps/web` on port 4010 (4000 was occupied by an unrelated project's dev server on this machine), and curled the rendered HTML directly. `/this-route-does-not-exist` → 200, "Page Not Found"/"Back To Home" present with `vhyx-button` class (confirms `NotFound.tsx`'s VhyxUI Button actually rendered, not just compiled). `/invitations/accept` (no token) → 200, "Invalid invitation link" renders inside a real `vhyx-alert`, and the illustration `<img>` still renders correctly after the `styled()`→plain-`<img>` conversion. `/dashboard` (unauthenticated) → 307 to `/login?redirectTo=%2Fdashboard` (this app's server-side auth-redirect middleware, unrelated to `AuthGuard`'s own client-side check, confirmed unaffected). No error/exception text leaked into any of the rendered HTML. `AuthGuard`'s own `Spinner` render during the brief client-side bootstrap window specifically wasn't directly observed (that state is client-store-driven and momentary, not reachable via a static curl of SSR output) — noted here rather than silently claimed; risk is low since it's the same `<Spinner size='...' />` call already proven working in `VerifyEmailView.tsx`/`AcceptInvitationView.tsx`.
**Also ran `eslint`** (not one of the requested gates, but checked anyway): caught one real `import/order` violation in the new `NotFound.tsx` import block (introduced by this session's edit, not pre-existing) and fixed it to match the established grouping convention (react/next → external incl. `@mui`/`classnames`/`@vhyxui/react` → blank line → internal `@core`/`@/` incl. the `vhyxui-shims` Typography). Re-ran lint on all four touched files after the fix: clean. The 250 pre-existing problems from Phase 0's session are untouched and still out of scope.
**Result**: `@mui` import surface: 71 files/147 lines (Phase 0's end state) → **68 files/142 lines**. `typecheck`/`build`/`test` all pass clean: 19 routes (unchanged), 7 test files/23 tests (unchanged).
**Status:** active. Committed as a single commit (`cb7db61`) — all four targets are small and interdependent only through the shared "no theme coupling" property the audit already established, not worth splitting further. `backlog.md`'s Phase 1 items removed from the open list. Phases 2-4 (org/profile view migrations, NotificationBell/FeedbackButton rebuild, the illustration-panel problem) remain open, unchanged from the audit's plan.

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 2 vs Phase 3 scope discrepancy resolved: FeedbackHistoryTab/FeedbackDetailDrawer are independent of FeedbackButton, included in Phase 2

**Decided by:** Claude Code (Phase 2 session)
**Context:** The audit entry above's Finding 4 listed `views/profile/FeedbackHistoryTab.tsx` + `views/feedback/FeedbackDetailDrawer.tsx` alongside `contexts/FeedbackContext.tsx` and the `views/org/` view files without assigning explicit phase numbers to each; its own Status/Phase-0/Phase-1 summary lines then grouped "org/profile view migrations" as one phase-2 bucket and "the NotificationBell/FeedbackButton rebuild" as a separate later phase, without saying which bucket the history-tab/drawer pair belonged to. This session's brief flagged the resulting discrepancy directly: `backlog.md`'s own wording (written by an earlier session) had already folded FeedbackHistoryTab+Drawer into the Phase 2 "org/profile view migrations" list, while a plausible reading of "paired with the FeedbackButton rebuild" would put them in Phase 3 instead.
**Investigation:** Grepped both files' imports and grepped the whole app for `FeedbackButton` — zero references in either direction. `FeedbackHistoryTab.tsx` imports `FeedbackDetailDrawer.tsx` (the drawer is only opened from the history tab's row-click), `GenericServerTable`, `useMyFeedbackTableList`, and `utils/feedback.util.ts` — never `FeedbackButton.tsx`. `FeedbackButton.tsx` is a separate submission-form dialog wired directly into the dashboard layout (`app/[locale]/(dashboard)/layout.tsx`), sharing only the same feedback API domain (`useFeedback.ts` hooks) with the history tab/drawer, not any code coupling. They are genuinely independent: one is a read-only list+detail view over already-submitted feedback, the other is the submission form itself.
**Decision:** Independent — included FeedbackHistoryTab.tsx and FeedbackDetailDrawer.tsx in this Phase 2 session, matching `backlog.md`'s existing framing rather than deferring them to Phase 3. Phase 3 is now scoped as NotificationBell + FeedbackButton only (the two components that actually remain MUI-coupled and untouched).
**Rationale:** The audit's own file-inventory grouping (Finding 4) was an artifact of both being "Feedback"-domain files discovered in the same investigation pass, not evidence of code coupling — the actual test the brief asked for (import/dependency check) resolves it cleanly in favor of independence.
**Status:** resolved, not open. See the "Phase 2 executed" entry below for the actual migration.

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 2 executed: CreateOrgDialog, MyAccountsTable, BillingView, FeedbackContext, FeedbackHistoryTab, FeedbackDetailDrawer migrated off MUI

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed), Claude Code (implementation)
**Context:** Phase 2 of the removal plan from the 2026-09-16 audit — the org/profile view migrations, plus resolving the Phase 2/3 scope question for FeedbackHistoryTab/FeedbackDetailDrawer (see the entry directly above). Re-verified each file's current MUI usage fresh before touching anything, per the standing discipline for every phase so far.
**What changed, per file:**
- `views/org/CreateOrgDialog.tsx` (93→~90 lines) — `Dialog`/`DialogTitle`/`DialogContent`/`DialogActions`/`Button`/`LoadingButton`/`Typography`/`CustomTextField` → VhyxUI `Dialog` (Portal+Overlay+Content+Footer)/`Form`/`TextField`/`Button`, `vhyxui-shims` `Typography`. Same `untypedForm`/`void form.formState.errors` template pattern as `CreateApiKeyDialog.tsx` (decision.md, 2026-09-10).
- `views/org/MyAccountsTable.tsx` — `Chip`/`Typography`/`Button`/`Box` → `Badge`/`vhyxui-shims Typography`/`Button`/plain `div`. New local `roleBadgeVariant()`, identical mapping to `MembersTable.tsx`'s own helper (`roleLevelColor()`'s `'error'`→`'danger'`, everything else passes through).
- `views/org/billing/BillingView.tsx` (447 lines, the largest single file in this session) — full MUI surface (`Box`/`Card`/`CardContent`/`Typography`/`Chip`/`Divider`/`Button`/`LoadingButton`/`Skeleton`/`Alert`/`Dialog`+subcomponents) replaced across `UpgradeDialog`, `InvoiceRow`, `SubscriptionCard`, and `InvoiceSection`. New local `billingBadgeVariant()` (same mapping shape as `feedbackBadgeVariant`/`roleBadgeVariant`). Invoice PDF/hosted-invoice links use `Button`'s `asChild` pattern to render as real `<a href>` elements (`ButtonProps` extends `ButtonHTMLAttributes`, no native `href`). The `PAST_DUE` alert's inline "Update payment" button uses `variant='ghost'` with an inline `color: var(--vhyx-color-danger)` override — `Button` has no per-instance color prop, and `variant='link'` was tried first but rejected: it renders in the accent (indigo) color, which read as visually wrong inside a danger-red alert. **Real behavior improvement as a side effect**: the success banner (`justUpgraded`) previously called MUI Alert's `onClose`, which only fired the callback (URL cleanup) and never actually hid the alert — a latent, pre-existing bug, since `justUpgraded` is computed once from `window.location.search` at render time and MUI's `onClose` doesn't unmount anything itself. VhyxUI's `Alert` manages its own dismissal state internally (`dismissible`/`onDismiss`), so the same conversion now correctly hides the banner on click, not just cleans the URL.
- `contexts/FeedbackContext.tsx` — grepped every consumer of `useFeedback()` first, per the brief's explicit instruction: `libs/components/Confirmation.tsx` is the only one, reading only `showFeedback`. The context/hook's full external API shape (`feedbackOpen`/`feedbackMessage`/`feedbackType`/`customIcon`/`showFeedback`/`handleClose`, from `useFeedbackDialog.ts`) is completely unchanged — only the internal `Dialog`/`Button`/`Typography` JSX moved to VhyxUI. `Dialog.Footer`'s default CSS-module `justify-content: flex-end` is overridden with an inline `style={{ justifyContent: 'center' }}` rather than a `justify-center` className, since a CSS-module class and a Tailwind utility class have equal specificity and the winner would otherwise depend on unpredictable stylesheet load order — only an inline style is guaranteed to win.
- `views/profile/FeedbackHistoryTab.tsx` + `views/feedback/FeedbackDetailDrawer.tsx` — `Chip`/`Typography`/`Box` → `Badge`/`vhyxui-shims Typography`/plain `div`; MUI `Drawer` → VhyxUI `Drawer` (identical compound-component API to `Dialog`, confirmed already used once in this app for the mobile nav drawer, `DashboardSidebar.tsx`). `feedbackBadgeVariant()` extracted into `utils/feedback.util.ts` (alongside the existing `typeColor`/`statusColor`/`priorityColor`) since both files needed the identical MUI-color→Badge-variant mapping — shared rather than duplicated a third time. The drawer's header is no longer sticky while the body scrolls underneath it (VhyxUI's `Drawer.Content` has no separate scrolling region for header vs. body, unlike the original hand-rolled MUI `sx={{ position: 'sticky' }}` header) — a minor, accepted cosmetic regression, not worth hand-rolling a custom sticky layout for.
**Server/Client boundary check**: all six files already carried their own `'use client'` before this session (unchanged) and are only ever consumed by other client components or by `page.tsx` files that just import the already-client component — never a bare `@vhyxui/react` import inside a genuine Server Component, which is the specific shape that broke Phase 1's `(dashboard)/layout.tsx`. No boundary issue found or introduced.
**Functional check**: no Chrome browser extension available this session either (same as Phase 1) — no live browser walkthrough. Instead: killed the two orphaned `ts-node-dev` `apps/api` supervisors found already running (per `LOCAL_DEV_BACKEND.md`'s documented cleanup procedure) and started a fresh one against the local dev DB; started `apps/web` on port 4177 (port 4000 was, again, occupied by an unrelated project's Next.js v15.1.9 dev server on this machine — left untouched). Verified real data end-to-end at the API layer for every hook these six files call: logged in as `test@example.com`, `GET /account/me` returned the real 2-org seed data `MyAccountsTable` renders; `GET .../billing/subscription` and `.../billing/invoices` for Test Corp returned a real active PRO subscription and 7 real paid Stripe invoices (confirming `BillingView`'s exact endpoint wiring); `POST /account/organizations` created a real "Phase 2 QA Org" that immediately appeared in a follow-up `/account/me` call (proving `CreateOrgDialog`'s mutation → `MyAccountsTable`'s data source is wired correctly end-to-end), then deleted via direct SQL (`DELETE FROM "Account"`, cascades) to leave no residue; `DELETE .../invitations/<bogus-uuid>` returned a real `404 {"message":"Invitation not found"}`, confirming the exact error shape `Confirmation.tsx`'s `catch` block feeds into `showFeedback()` (the one path that reaches the rewritten `FeedbackContext` Dialog). All six SSR routes (`billing`, `dashboard`, `profile`) rendered 200 with no server-side exceptions under an `is_authenticated=1` cookie. **What could not be directly observed**: the actual post-hydration, client-rendered DOM (real Badge colors, Dialog open/close, the FeedbackContext success/error banner actually appearing) — these are all client-side-fetched (`'use client'` + TanStack Query), so a curl of the SSR HTML shows only the pre-hydration shell, never the live-data render. Flagged here rather than assumed fine, same standard as Phase 1's AuthGuard spinner gap. Both dev processes were stopped at the end of the session, per `LOCAL_DEV_BACKEND.md`'s standing convention.
**Result**: `@mui` import surface: 69 files/142 lines (re-confirmed via `git stash` against the pre-session tree — corrects Phase 1's own "68/142" end-state note as an off-by-one, not a regression) → **63 files/105 lines**. `typecheck`/`build`/`test` all pass clean: 19 routes (unchanged), `apps/web` 7 test files/23 tests (unchanged), root suite 19 files/99 tests (unchanged). `eslint` (not a requested gate, checked anyway): 249 problems, effectively unchanged from Phase 0's ~250 baseline — the only new item is one `padding-line-between-statements` warning in `CreateOrgDialog.tsx` that exactly mirrors an identical, already-accepted instance in `CreateApiKeyDialog.tsx`'s own `void form.formState.errors` line (the reused template pattern itself, not a new problem class).
**Status:** active. Committed as 4 commits (CreateOrgDialog+MyAccountsTable; BillingView; FeedbackContext; FeedbackHistoryTab+FeedbackDetailDrawer+feedback.util.ts). `backlog.md`'s Phase 2 item resolved and removed from the open bucket, replaced with a Phase 3-4-only item. Phase 3 (NotificationBell/FeedbackButton rebuild) and Phase 4 (the blank-layout-pages illustration-panel problem) remain open, unchanged from the audit's plan.
