### 2026-09-16 — Phase 3 part 1: NotificationBell migrated off MUI; FeedbackButton fully investigated and planned, execution deferred to its own session

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed), Claude Code (implementation)
**Context:** Phase 3 of the removal plan — the two real, substantial, data-driven features left MUI-internal since Step 3 (2026-09-10, "NotificationBell and FeedbackButton left as MUI-internal... VhyxUI also has no Collapse/Dropdown primitive that would be needed to migrate NotificationBell properly anyway"). This session's brief asked to fully investigate and plan both, then execute NotificationBell only, leaving FeedbackButton as its own follow-up.
**VhyxUI version re-check**: 0.3.1-alpha (`packages/react/package.json`, consumed via direct `link:`), no `CHANGELOG.md` in the repo. Confirmed via `git log` that Popover and Tooltip landed together in the same commit, well before Step 3 — meaning Step 3's ModeDropdown.tsx comment ("Dropped the Tooltip-on-hover — VhyxUI has no equivalent primitive") was **incorrect even at the time it was written**, not something that became true later. `Tooltip`'s real API (`content` prop, single-child `cloneElement`, hover/focus-triggered, Escape-to-hide) is a close match for MUI's `Tooltip title=`. No `Collapse`, `Dropdown`/`Menu`, or `Fab` component exists in either 0.3.1-alpha or at Step 3's time — that part of the original finding holds.
**NotificationBell.tsx — investigated, planned, and executed:**
- **Mapping**: `IconButton`+`Badge` (bell trigger) → `Popover.Trigger` wrapped in `Tooltip` (content='Notifications'), with a hand-built absolutely-positioned `<Badge variant='danger'>` overlay (VhyxUI's `Badge` has no overlay/corner-positioning mode, confirmed by reading `Badge.module.css` — no `position` rules at all). `Popper`+`Fade`+`Paper`+`ClickAwayListener` → `Popover` (`side='bottom' align='end'`, matching `UserDropdown`'s own choice for the same top-right icon cluster) — Popover's built-in outside-click/Escape handling covers `ClickAwayListener`; its own `vhyx-scale-in` animation covers `Fade`'s role, not identical but functionally equivalent. Header's count `Chip` → `Badge`. "Mark all read" `IconButton`+`Tooltip`+`CircularProgress` → `Button variant='ghost' iconOnly loading={...}` wrapped in `Tooltip` (`loading` is a real built-in prop, no manual icon-swap needed). Row `Divider` → `Separator`. `Skeleton` → the existing `vhyxui-shims` `Skeleton` (variant mapping already covers circular/text, no gap). Footer "View all notifications" `Button` → `Popover.Close` styled as a full-width ghost button — it never had a real navigation target in the MUI version either (`handleClose`-on-click only), so this reproduces the same pre-existing non-functional-but-closes behavior as UserDropdown's placeholder menu items, not a new gap.
- **`settings.skin` preserved**: confirmed still a real, live setting (`Login.tsx`/`Register.tsx`/`ForgotPasswordView.tsx`/`ResetPasswordView.tsx` all still read it post-migration for a `border-ie` class) — kept reading it in `NotificationBell`, adapted to VhyxUI's panel (`Popover.Content` always has a border, unlike MUI's default-elevation `Paper`) as "drop the shadow" rather than "add a border," the closest equivalent behavior rather than a literal port.
- **Width override**: the panel needed `width: 380px` (matching the original), which conflicts with `Popover.Content`'s own default `max-width: 20rem`(320px) — set via inline `style`, not a CSS Module class, since this is a genuine specificity tie that an inline style is the only way to guarantee winning regardless of stylesheet load order (same reasoning as Phase 2's `Dialog.Footer` `justify-content` override). The `.panel.bordered` box-shadow override, by contrast, uses a compound two-class CSS selector specifically because that has strictly higher specificity than `Popover.Content`'s single-class rule — a real override, not one relying on load-order luck like `UserDropdown.module.css`'s untested-at-this-width `.menu` padding override happens to.
- **Server/Client boundary**: `NotificationBell.tsx` already carried its own `'use client'` before this session (unchanged) — confirmed its only mount point is `DashboardTopbar.tsx`, which itself has no `'use client'` but only ever composes already-client children, never imports `@vhyxui/react` directly. Zero boundary risk, unlike Phase 1's `(dashboard)/layout.tsx` bug shape. `pnpm build` confirmed clean with no SSR break.
- **A real, pre-existing bug found via functional testing, not caused by this migration**: `AppNotification.message` (the frontend type) never matched the real API's field name. Read `apps/api`'s `GetNotifications` use case directly (`src/modules/notification/application/use-cases/index.ts`) — its return type and mapping both use `body`, never `message`, at every layer from the Prisma model up through the route (`reply.send(result)`, no wrapper). Confirmed live: seeded 3 real notifications via SQL, called the real endpoint, got back `body` fields; the frontend's `notification.message` read would always have been `undefined`, silently rendering a blank line where the message text should be — since this feature shipped, MUI version included (this migration only surfaced it by touching the file, it didn't introduce it). **Fixed**: renamed `AppNotification.message` → `body` (confirmed the only consumer anywhere in the app via grep) and updated the one read site in `NotificationBell.tsx`. Same standard as Phase 2's BillingView/My-Organizations bug fixes — a small, unambiguous, single-consumer fix found via the functional check itself, not left "faithfully reproduced" when faithfully reproducing it means preserving a feature that has never actually worked.
- **Functional check**: no Chrome extension available this session either (third session in a row) — no live browser walkthrough. Killed no orphaned processes this time (ports were clean); started a fresh `apps/api` against the local dev DB and `apps/web` on port 4177 (port 4000 still occupied by the same unrelated project, left untouched). Seeded 3 real notifications (`MEMBER_JOINED`/`PAYMENT_SUCCEEDED` unread, `TUNNEL_DISCONNECTED` already-read) for `test@example.com` via direct SQL — the real backend had zero seed data for notifications for either standard test account. Verified live against the real API: `GET .../notifications` returned the 3 rows with `unreadCount: 2`; `PATCH .../notifications/:id/read` returned `204` and correctly flipped that one row's `isRead` (`unreadCount` dropped to 1) — exactly the shape `useMarkRead`'s optimistic update expects; `PATCH .../notifications/read-all` returned `204` and zeroed `unreadCount`, flipping all three rows — exactly what `useMarkAllRead` expects. `/dashboard` (NotificationBell's mount point) SSR'd `200` with zero server-side exceptions both with notifications present and after cleanup (empty state). Cleaned up the 3 seeded rows via direct SQL afterward, confirmed back to empty. **What specifically still needs a follow-up visual check, more than Phase 1-2's mostly-static content did**: this component's whole value is a stateful interaction (bell click → panel opens → real notification list → click a row → optimistic mark-read → panel closes/stays) that curl fundamentally cannot exercise — the API-level and SSR-level checks above prove every underlying wire is connected correctly, but not that the Popover's open/close, hover Tooltip, badge overlay positioning, or per-type icon colors actually render and behave right pixel-for-pixel in a browser. Flagged explicitly, not assumed fine.
- **Result**: `@mui` surface: 63 files/105 lines (Phase 2's end state, re-confirmed unchanged at session start) → **62 files/90 lines**. `typecheck`/`build`/`test` all pass clean: 19 routes (unchanged), `apps/web` 7 files/23 tests (unchanged), root suite 19 files/99 tests (unchanged). `eslint`: 249 problems, unchanged from Phase 2's baseline (one new `lines-around-comment` violation in `notification.types.ts`'s new comment block, caught and fixed before the final count).
**FeedbackButton.tsx — fully investigated and planned, NOT executed this session:**
- **Component mapping**: `Fab` (fixed-position floating circular button, bottom-right) → no VhyxUI equivalent exists; use `<Button iconOnly>` with a fixed-position className, the exact same substitution Phase 1's `ScrollToTopButton.tsx` already established for this precise gap. `Zoom` (wraps the Fab) → its `in` prop is hardcoded `true` with no conditional anywhere in the component, meaning it only ever plays its entrance animation once on mount and never actually toggles visibility based on real state — safe to drop entirely, not a shimmable gap, just dead animation wrapper around an always-rendered element. `Tooltip title='Send feedback'` → VhyxUI `Tooltip` (confirmed available, see above — do NOT drop it the way ModeDropdown incorrectly did). `Dialog`+`DialogTitle`+`DialogContent`+`DialogActions` → VhyxUI `Dialog` (Portal+Overlay+Content+Title+Footer), same compound pattern as every other migrated dialog this project has done; `maxWidth='sm'` is one size step up from `CreateOrgDialog`/`UpgradeDialog`'s `maxWidth='xs'`→`size='sm'` mapping, so it likely maps to `size='md'` (VhyxUI's default) — not yet confirmed against real content, the FeedbackButton session should verify visually rather than assume. `Button`+`LoadingButton` → VhyxUI `Button` (`loading` prop built in). `Typography` → the `vhyxui-shims` `Typography`. `Box` (the many layout wrappers) → plain `div`s.
- **`Chip` type-selector (4 pills, color-per-type, RHF-`Controller`-driven `field.onChange`)**: VhyxUI's `Badge` has no native `onClick`/interactive affordance — wrap each in a plain `<button onClick={() => field.onChange(t.value)}>`, the exact same architecture `CreateApiKeyDialog.tsx`'s scope-selector Badges already established (also a hand-built toggle, also originally a MUI `Chip` array). The dynamic per-type accent colors (`bgcolor: \`\${typeConfig?.color}.lighter\`` for the dialog-title icon circle, `var(--mui-palette-\${typeConfig?.color}-main)` for the icon color) reference MUI theme palette names by string (`error`/`warning`/`info`/`success`) — each maps mechanically to VhyxUI's own semantic tokens (`--vhyx-color-danger`/`-warning`/`-info`/`-success` and their `-subtle` variants for the "lighter" background), same mapping shape as this session's `billingBadgeVariant`/`feedbackBadgeVariant` helpers, just applied to raw CSS custom properties instead of a `Badge` `variant` prop.
- **`CustomTextField` (6 fields, 2 single-line + 4 multiline)**: this is the one mapping detail genuinely easy to get wrong by analogy from earlier dialogs — VhyxUI does **not** have a single `TextField` with a `multiline` prop the way MUI does. It has separate `TextField`/`TextareaField` components (confirmed by reading both directly): `title` stays `TextField`; `description`, `stepsToReproduce`, `expectedBehavior`, and `actualBehavior` (4 of the 6 fields — the majority) need `TextareaField`, not `TextField`. Both have the same `Field`-wrapped shape (`label`/`hint`/`error`/`required` plus the underlying input's own props spread on top) and the same `error` contract: a **string** message passed directly to `error`, not MUI's boolean-`error`-plus-separate-`helperText` split. Worth noting: this project's own established template (`CreateOrgDialog`/`CreateApiKeyDialog`) never actually wires up `error`/`hint` on its `TextField` calls at all — validation still blocks submission but no message is ever shown to the user. FeedbackButton's session should treat this as a judgment call (replicate the existing gap for consistency, or improve it since the primitive supports it) rather than something this plan decides in advance.
- **`Collapse` (bug-specific fields, `in={isBug}`, `unmountOnExit`)**: no VhyxUI equivalent, confirmed. Two real options: (a) drop the height-animation, keep the show/hide logic as a plain `{isBug && (...)}` conditional — lowest complexity, zero new shim, the only cost is the fields popping in/out abruptly instead of sliding; (b) hand-roll a CSS `max-height`-transition shim, needing either a measured `scrollHeight` via ref or a generous fixed max-height guess — real, non-trivial component work (unlike `Typography`/`Skeleton`/`Avatar`, which are thin prop-mapping wrappers around already-static content, an accurate height-animated collapse is genuinely new component logic). **Recommendation: option (a)** — the cosmetic loss is minor and this is exactly the kind of "shim complexity vs. payoff" tradeoff that should be a deliberate, visible decision in the FeedbackButton session itself, not something inherited silently from this plan.
- **Server/Client boundary**: **no risk expected**, verified directly — `FeedbackButton.tsx` already carries its own `'use client'` (unchanged), and its one mount point, `app/[locale]/(dashboard)/layout.tsx`, is the exact Server Component that broke on a **bare** `@vhyxui/react` import in Phase 1 — but `FeedbackButton.tsx` is already a dedicated client-component *file*, imported as such, which is precisely the fix pattern Phase 1 established (see `ScrollToTopButton.tsx`, imported into this same `layout.tsx` and working fine post-fix). The FeedbackButton session should still verify with a real `pnpm build` after migrating, per this project's standing discipline, but there is no structural reason to expect a repeat of Phase 1's break.
- **Form structure**: unlike `CreateOrgDialog`/`CreateApiKeyDialog` (which use VhyxUI's `Form` wrapper with a `type='submit'` button inside it), `FeedbackButton`'s Cancel/Submit buttons live in `DialogActions`, **outside** any `<form>` element — submission is triggered by a manual `onClick={handleSubmit(onSubmit)}` on the Submit button, not native form submission. **Recommendation**: preserve this exact structure (no `<form>`, manual `onClick`-triggered `handleSubmit`) rather than restructuring onto `Form` — lower risk, and matches what's actually there today; restructuring onto `Form` would be a design decision beyond what "restyle, not rewrite" calls for.
- **Size/complexity estimate**: comparable to or slightly larger than Phase 2's `BillingView.tsx` (447 lines) — not because of raw line count (`FeedbackButton.tsx` is 377 lines) but because of the number of small, real judgment calls it packs in: two missing-primitive gaps (`Fab`, `Collapse`) each needing an explicit decision, a `TextField`/`TextareaField` split across 6 fields, dynamic per-type CSS-variable color mapping, and a transient "submitted" success state (straightforward — same pattern as Phase 2's `FeedbackContext` success dialog). **Recommendation: do not split further** — budget one focused session with real time, the same instruction `BillingView` got, not a multi-session breakdown.
**Status:** active. Committed as a single commit (`e9a4419`) — the `NotificationBell` migration, the `body`/`message` bug fix, and the two stale-comment updates (`DashboardTopbar.tsx`, `(dashboard)/layout.tsx`) are all small and interdependent enough not to warrant separate commits. `backlog.md`'s Phase 3-4 item split: NotificationBell resolved and removed; FeedbackButton (Phase 3 part 2) and the illustration-panel problem (Phase 4) remain, plus a new line for the `scroll-to-top/index.tsx` MUI holdout this session's investigation surfaced.

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 3 part 2: FeedbackButton and scroll-to-top's Zoom/useScrollTrigger wrapper migrated off MUI

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed), Claude Code (implementation)
**Context:** Phase 3 part 2 — execute the FeedbackButton.tsx migration exactly as planned in the "Phase 3 part 1" entry above (no re-investigation asked for or done), plus fix the small `@core/components/scroll-to-top/index.tsx` holdout that session's investigation surfaced.
**FeedbackButton.tsx — executed directly from the existing plan, no deviations:**
- `Fab` → `Button iconOnly size='lg'` with fixed positioning (bottom/right/zIndex/boxShadow all preserved as inline styles) — same substitution Phase 1's `ScrollToTopButton.tsx` established. `size='lg'` (48px) is the closest available Button size to MUI's Fab `medium` (56px); VhyxUI's Button size enum doesn't expose the token scale's `xl` (56px) step.
- The `Zoom` wrapper around the FAB was dropped entirely, confirmed dead exactly as the plan predicted (`in` was hardcoded `true`, no conditional referenced it anywhere).
- `Tooltip title='Send feedback'` → VhyxUI `Tooltip content='Send feedback' side='left'` (kept, not dropped — per the plan's explicit callout not to repeat ModeDropdown's mistake).
- `Dialog`+`DialogTitle`+`DialogContent`+`DialogActions` → VhyxUI `Dialog` (Portal+Overlay+Content+Title+Footer), `size='md'`. The rich icon+title+subtitle header content lives inside `Dialog.Title` using nested `<span>`s (not `<div>`s) specifically to stay valid HTML — `Dialog.Title` renders an `<h2>`, and a `<div>` is flow content, invalid inside phrasing-content-only `<h2>`; `<span>` is phrasing content and nests cleanly. Not something the plan called out explicitly, found while implementing.
- `Chip` type-selector → a plain `<button onClick={() => field.onChange(t.value)}>` wrapping a `Badge`, one per `FEEDBACK_TYPES` entry, each still its own `Controller` (matching the original's four-separate-Controllers-on-one-field-name structure, preserved rather than consolidated) — same architecture as `CreateApiKeyDialog.tsx`'s scope-selector Badges, per the plan. `feedbackTypeBadgeVariant()` maps `'error'`→`'danger'`, passes the other three through — same shape as every other Badge-variant helper this project has written (`roleBadgeVariant`, `feedbackBadgeVariant`, `billingBadgeVariant`).
- The dynamic per-type icon-circle colors (`bgcolor: `${color}.lighter`` / `var(--mui-palette-${color}-main)`) map onto `var(--vhyx-color-${token}-subtle)` / `var(--vhyx-color-${token})`, with the same `'error'`→`'danger'` token substitution.
- `title` uses `TextField`; `description`, `stepsToReproduce`, `expectedBehavior`, `actualBehavior` use `TextareaField` (`minRows` instead of MUI's `rows`) — the plan's load-bearing finding, applied exactly as scoped, 4 of 6 fields.
- **Error display was preserved, not treated as a judgment call**: re-reading the original file directly (not from memory of the plan) showed `FeedbackButton.tsx` already wired `error`/`helperText` on every field — unlike `CreateOrgDialog`/`CreateApiKeyDialog`'s templates, which never did. This settles the plan's open "judgment call" automatically: preserving *exact current behavior* here means showing validation messages, since that's what the MUI version actually did. `TextField`/`TextareaField`'s own `error` prop takes the message string directly (`errors.title?.message`), which is simpler than MUI's boolean+`helperText` split, not a design choice made independently of the plan.
- `Collapse` → a plain `{isBug && (...)}` conditional, no height animation, per the plan's recommendation (a).
- Submission structure preserved exactly: no `<form>` element, `handleSubmit(onSubmit)` fired manually from the Submit button's `onClick`, per the plan's recommendation and this session's explicit instruction not to restructure onto `Form`.
- **Server/Client boundary**: confirmed safe as the plan predicted — `pnpm build` passed clean on the first attempt, no repeat of Phase 1's `(dashboard)/layout.tsx` break.
**`@core/components/scroll-to-top/index.tsx`**: `Zoom`+`useScrollTrigger`+`styled('div')` replaced with a plain `useState`+`useEffect` scroll listener (`window.scrollY > 400`, matching the original's `threshold: 400`/`disableHysteresis: true`) and a conditional `return null`. Position (`insetInlineEnd`/`insetBlockEnd`) converted from `theme.spacing(20)`/`theme.spacing(14)` (MUI's default 8px spacing unit) to literal `160px`/`112px` — same computed values, no visual change. `zIndex` from `var(--mui-zIndex-fab)` (MUI's default `zIndex.fab` is 1050) to the literal `1050`. **Real, visible behavior change, not hidden**: the button now appears/disappears abruptly on crossing the scroll threshold instead of fading+scaling in — an explicit, expected tradeoff of dropping `Zoom`, not an oversight (unlike `FeedbackButton`'s `Zoom`, this one was real and functional, gating actual visibility, so this is a genuine cosmetic loss, not free like the dead-code removal was).
**Functional check**: no Chrome extension available (fourth session in a row, exactly as expected per this session's brief). Started a fresh `apps/api` against the local dev DB and `apps/web` on port 4177 (port 4000 still held by the same unrelated project, untouched). Submitted a real `BUG_REPORT` feedback item via `POST /api/v1/feedback` as `test@example.com` — `title` (short text) and `description`/`stepsToReproduce`/`expectedBehavior`/`actualBehavior` (all multiline, the fields that specifically needed `TextareaField`) all populated — got back a real `201` with a real id. Confirmed it appears via `GET /api/v1/feedback` — the exact endpoint Phase 2's `FeedbackHistoryTab`/`useMyFeedbackTableList` reads — proving the submission → list round-trip works end-to-end across both phases' migrated code. Cleaned up via direct SQL afterward. `/dashboard` (FeedbackButton's mount point) SSR'd `200` with zero exceptions. **What could not be observed, and why**: `FeedbackButton`'s own rendered output never appears in the SSR HTML at all — not a regression, `AuthGuard` (its direct parent) gates on a client-only Zustand store (`isAuthenticated`) that is always false during SSR, so `AuthGuard` renders its own spinner/`null` and never reaches `{children}`/`<FeedbackButton/>` server-side. This is the exact same ceiling Phase 1 already documented for `AuthGuard`'s own spinner — confirmed again here, not a new gap. Per Tanveer's direction, a batched real-browser visual pass covering NotificationBell, FeedbackButton, and everything since Phase 1 will happen once the Chrome extension is available, rather than blocking this session on it.
**Result**: `@mui` surface: 62 files/90 lines (Phase 3 part 1's end state, re-confirmed unchanged at session start) → **60 files/74 lines**. `typecheck`/`build`/`test` all pass clean: 19 routes (unchanged), `apps/web` 7 files/23 tests (unchanged), root suite 19 files/99 tests (unchanged). `eslint`: 249 problems, unchanged from Phase 3 part 1's baseline — zero new issues in either touched file.
**Status:** active. Committed as a single commit (`d15bf50`) — `FeedbackButton.tsx`, `scroll-to-top/index.tsx`, and the one stale-comment update in `(dashboard)/layout.tsx` are small and interdependent enough not to warrant separate commits. `backlog.md`'s Phase 3 part 2 and `scroll-to-top` items resolved and removed. **Phase 4 (the blank-layout-pages illustration-panel problem) is now the only open phase** in the MUI/Vuexy removal plan.

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 4 part 1: illustration-panel design (CSS-only, no MUI), and a broader ThemeProvider-removal blocker found that Phase 4 alone doesn't clear

**Decided by:** Claude Code (investigation/design only — no implementation this session, per the brief)
**Context:** Phase 4 is the last open item in the MUI/Vuexy removal plan — the blank-layout-pages illustration panel, deferred since Step 2 (2026-09-10) specifically because "the whole illustration/responsive-breakpoint system is built directly on the MUI theme object" and VhyxUI had no breakpoint/media-query primitive. This session re-investigates from scratch (not trusting the old "12 useTheme() calls" figure, which predates several sessions of unrelated component migrations in these same files) and designs a concrete, CSS-only solution.

**PART 1 — Full inventory, read fresh:**

- **`Login.tsx`**: `useTheme()` (1 call) + `useMediaQuery(theme.breakpoints.down('md'))` (the `hidden` flag, gates whether `MaskImg` even mounts — not just CSS-hidden) + `LoginIllustration = styled('img')` reading `theme.spacing(12)`/`theme.breakpoints.down(1536)`/`theme.breakpoints.down('lg')` + `theme.direction === 'rtl'` (flips `MaskImg` via `classnames`). That's the real inventory today — 4 distinct concerns (spacing, two breakpoint-capped max-heights, a below-md existence gate, an RTL flip), not 12 independent things; the old count predates Steps 3-5's unrelated Button/TextField/etc. migrations in this same file, which didn't touch any of this.
- **`Register.tsx`, `ForgotPasswordView.tsx`, `ResetPasswordView.tsx`**: byte-identical pattern to `Login.tsx`, down to the exact same four concerns and the same `classnames({'border-ie': settings.skin === 'bordered'}, ...)` / `classnames({'scale-x-[-1]': theme.direction === 'rtl'})` call shapes. Only two numbers differ: `Register.tsx`'s illustration base `maxBlockSize` is `600` (others: `680`) and its `MaskImg` `maxBlockSize` is `345` (others: `355`) — the two breakpoint-capped values (`550` at `<1536px`, `450` at `<1200px`) are identical across all four. **`Register.tsx` also imports MUI's `Grid`** (`container spacing={2}` / two `size={6}` children, for the firstName/lastName row) — unrelated to the illustration problem, but a separate, real, remaining MUI-component dependency in this same file that must also go before `ThemeProvider` can come off.
- **`VerifyEmailView.tsx` and `VerifyEmailSentView.tsx` — genuinely NOT part of the hard problem, an audit-framing correction.** Read fully: neither has `useTheme()`, `useMediaQuery`, or any breakpoint/spacing/direction call at all. Both are single-column centered layouts (no split illustration panel) with one `styled('img')({ maxWidth: 320, width: '100%', marginBottom: 24|32 })` — fixed pixel values only, **zero theme coupling**, the exact same shape Phase 1 already found and fixed for `AcceptInvitationView.tsx`. These were lumped into "the six pages" by every prior session's file-inventory pass (they share the "still has a `styled('img')`" surface property) but never actually needed the hard responsive/RTL design — they need the same trivial one-line conversion Phase 1 already did for `AcceptInvitationView`, not the shared component this design proposes.
- **`NotFound.tsx`**: a strict subset of the Login/Register/ForgotPassword/ResetPassword pattern — only `useTheme()` + `useMediaQuery(theme.breakpoints.down('md'))` (`hidden`) + `theme.direction === 'rtl'` (flips its own `MaskImg`). No character-illustration `styled()` component at all (its character image is already a plain `<img>` with pure Tailwind classes, confirmed in Phase 1). No `margin`/breakpoint-capped-height concern — just the mask + hidden-gate + RTL-flip piece.
- **RTL is confirmed fully dead code today, not just hard to verify**: grepped every `<Providers direction=...>` call site (`(dashboard)/layout.tsx`, `(blank-layout-pages)/layout.tsx`, `[...not-found]/page.tsx`) — every single one hardcodes `const direction = 'ltr'`. `src/i18n/routing.ts` only configures `locales: ['en', 'fr']`, both LTR — there is no live path to `theme.direction` ever resolving to `'rtl'` anywhere in this app today. Not proposing to drop RTL-awareness anyway (see Part 2) since it's essentially free to keep structurally correct, but this confirms nothing observable breaks if the replacement doesn't preserve pixel-perfect RTL behavior no one can currently trigger.
- **A real arithmetic mistake caught and corrected mid-investigation**: initially assumed `theme.spacing(12)` = 96px using MUI's *default* 8px-per-unit convention. Checking `@core/theme/spacing.ts` directly (not assumed) shows this app overrides it: `spacing: (factor) => \`${0.25 * factor}rem\`` — **the same 0.25rem-per-unit formula Tailwind's own default spacing scale uses**, evidently set deliberately to keep MUI and Tailwind spacing numerically identical during the migration. `theme.spacing(12)` is actually `3rem` (48px) — and, usefully, this means `theme.spacing(N)` in this app always equals Tailwind's own `N`-suffixed spacing utility (`theme.spacing(12)` ⇔ Tailwind's `12`), a direct, zero-conversion mapping.

**VhyxUI re-check (fresh, not assumed carried over)**: still 0.3.1-alpha, no new commits since Phase 3. Grepped `packages/react/src`, `packages/tokens/src`, `packages/core/src` for `breakpoint`/`useMediaQuery`/`media-query`/`responsive`: zero hits, same as every prior check (Step 2, the original audit, Phase 1, now Phase 4). No breakpoint token of any kind exists either. This is the fourth independent confirmation — treat as settled, not worth re-checking again barring a VhyxUI version bump.

**An established pattern already exists in this exact app — no new approach needs inventing:**
- `apps/web/src/app/globals.css` defines Tailwind v4 `@theme` breakpoints (`--breakpoint-sm: 600px`, `--breakpoint-md: 900px`, `--breakpoint-lg: 1200px`, `--breakpoint-xl: 1536px`) that are **exact numeric matches for MUI's own default breakpoints** — not a coincidence, evidently set to keep the two systems interchangeable. `max-md:hidden` (Tailwind's `max-*` variant, real and confirmed working — see below) is *already* used live on the outer left-panel `<div>` in all four split-layout pages today, alongside the MUI-driven inner logic — so Tailwind's responsive variants and MUI's breakpoints are already coexisting in the same files, at the same pixel thresholds.
- `NotFound.tsx`'s own character `<img>` (separate from its `MaskImg`) already uses a **pure-Tailwind, MUI-free, ascending responsive size ramp**: `bs-[400px] md:bs-[450px] lg:bs-[500px]`. This is direct, load-bearing, already-shipped proof that breakpoint-driven image sizing with zero MUI/VhyxUI dependency is not just theoretically possible in this codebase — it's the established pattern for the *other* image in this exact file.
- Confirmed via a direct grep of the installed `tailwindcss@4.1.17` package that `max-sm`/`max-md`/`max-lg`/`max-xl` variants and `dir="rtl"`/`dir="ltr"`-keyed selectors (the built-in `rtl:`/`ltr:` variants) both exist in this Tailwind version — not assumed from documentation, confirmed present in the actual installed build this project uses.
- `<html dir='ltr'>` is set in the real root layout (`src/app/layout.tsx`), independently of MUI's own `direction` theme prop, and nothing in the blank-layout-pages tree overrides it. This means Tailwind's built-in `rtl:` variant (`&:where([dir="rtl"], [dir="rtl"] *)`) already has the exact hook point it needs, today, with zero wiring changes — and is a *cleaner, more centralized* mechanism than today's per-page `classnames({'scale-x-[-1]': theme.direction === 'rtl'})`, which is duplicated five times and reads a value (`theme.direction`) that isn't even the thing actually controlling direction in the DOM.

**PART 2 — Proposed design:**

1. **`src/@core/hooks/useBreakpointDown.ts` (new, tiny, framework-independent hook)** — replaces `useMediaQuery(theme.breakpoints.down(px))`. A `window.matchMedia(\`(max-width: ${px}px)\`)` listener, SSR-safe default of `false` (matching MUI's own `useMediaQuery` SSR behavior — this is parity, not a new limitation). Used with `900` (md) everywhere `hidden` is computed today.
2. **RTL**: drop `theme.direction === 'rtl'` entirely. Apply a static `rtl:scale-x-[-1]` Tailwind class directly on the mask `<img>` — no JS, no `classnames()` call, reacts to the real `<html dir>` attribute Tailwind's built-in `rtl:` variant already targets.
3. **`src/views/auth/AuthIllustrationPanel.tsx` (new shared component)** — the left-panel wrapper + character illustration + mask image, replacing the byte-identical block duplicated in `Login`/`Register`/`ForgotPasswordView`/`ResetPasswordView`. Props: `characterSrc`, `characterAlt`, `maskSrc`, `bordered` (passes through today's `settings.skin === 'bordered'` check — callers keep reading `useSettings()` themselves, same as every other already-migrated page in this app that still reads `settings.skin`), `characterMaxHeight` (default `680`, `Register` passes `600`), `maskMaxHeight` (default `355`, `Register` passes `345`). Must carry its own `'use client'` (it calls `useBreakpointDown`) — all four consuming pages are already client components, so this introduces no new Server/Client boundary, but the file needs the directive regardless per this project's standing discipline of never relying on "reached from an already-client subtree" alone.
4. **`src/views/auth/AuthIllustrationPanel.module.css` (new)** — real `@media` rules are needed here, not pure Tailwind utility classes, for one specific reason worth stating precisely: the character illustration's *base* max-height varies per page (`680` vs `600`), which can't be expressed as a static Tailwind arbitrary-value class (`` `max-h-[${prop}px]` `` is a runtime-interpolated string Tailwind's build-time content scanner never sees, so no CSS would be generated for it) — and it can't be expressed as an inline `style` either, because an inline style's specificity beats *any* stylesheet rule including a media-query-scoped one, which would silently make the two breakpoint-capped overrides (`550`/`450`, identical across all four pages and expressible as plain Tailwind `max-xl:`/`max-lg:` classes on their own) never apply. The correct, established-elsewhere-in-this-project pattern (every dropdown/panel this migration built — `NotificationBell`, `ModeDropdown`, `UserDropdown` — has its own `.module.css` for exactly this class of "real CSS, not just utility classes" need) is a CSS custom property carrying the per-instance base value, with real `@media` rules providing the breakpoint overrides at the same selector/specificity level so cascade order resolves it correctly:
   ```css
   .character {
     max-block-size: var(--character-max-h, 680px);
   }
   @media (max-width: 1536px) { .character { max-block-size: 550px; } }
   @media (max-width: 1199.98px) { .character { max-block-size: 450px; } }
   ```
   (`1199.98px` matches MUI's own `down('lg')` convention exactly — MUI subtracts `0.02px` from the next breakpoint up specifically to avoid a double-fire race at the exact boundary; imperceptible visually, kept only for numeric fidelity.) `margin`, `z-index`, `max-inline-size: 100%`, `block-size: auto` need no CSS Module rule at all — they're identical across every page and map directly to static Tailwind classes (`m-12` — using the now-corrected 1:1 `theme.spacing(N)` ⇔ Tailwind-`N` mapping — `z-2`, `max-is-full`, `bs-auto`).
5. **`NotFound.tsx`**: doesn't need the full `AuthIllustrationPanel` (its layout is a single centered column with the mask full-bleed behind everything, not a two-column split) — only needs the mask-image piece (hidden-below-md gate + `rtl:` flip). Export it as a small named piece from the same module (e.g. `AuthIllustrationPanel.AuthMask` or a sibling `AuthMaskImage.tsx` — a naming/file-split call left to the implementation session, not load-bearing either way) so `NotFound.tsx` reuses the identical `useBreakpointDown(900)` + `rtl:` logic rather than a third hand-rolled copy.
6. **`VerifyEmailView.tsx` / `VerifyEmailSentView.tsx`**: not part of this design at all — batch them into the same implementation session as a trivial `styled('img')` → plain `<img className='max-is-[320px] is-full mbe-6' />` conversion, identical to Phase 1's `AcceptInvitationView.tsx` fix. Zero new component, zero design risk.
7. **`Register.tsx`'s MUI `Grid`**: `<Grid container spacing={2}><Grid size={6}>...</Grid><Grid size={6}>...</Grid></Grid>` → `<div className='grid grid-cols-2 gap-4'>...</div>` (MUI `spacing={2}` = 16px = Tailwind `gap-4`, using the same corrected spacing-unit mapping). Trivial, but real, and it must land in the same session as the illustration-panel fix — see Part 3.

**Similarity check, not assumed**: confirmed by direct diff-reading, not inferred from file names — `Login`/`Register`/`ForgotPasswordView`/`ResetPasswordView`'s illustration-panel JSX blocks are structurally identical (same className strings, same conditional shapes, same prop-passing pattern to `useImageVariant`), differing only in the two numeric constants and the image `src`/`alt` values already page-specific. A shared component is the right call, not an assumption — duplicating this a fifth time would be strictly worse than the four-times duplication already in place.

**PART 3 — Confirming the unlock, re-checked rather than assumed:**

Grepped the entire remaining `@mui` surface (60 files, Phase 3 part 2's end state) and categorized every file. Beyond the 7 illustration-problem files and the pure theme-construction machinery (`@core/theme/*`'s ~40 files, `libs/theme/*`'s 5 files — all exist solely to configure the MUI theme object and would be deleted once nothing needs it, not "dependents" in their own right) — two categories don't reduce to either bucket and were not accounted for by any prior session:

- **`@core/hooks/useImageVariant.ts` and `@core/hooks/useLayoutInit.ts` both call MUI's real `useColorScheme()`** (not `useTheme()` — a different MUI hook, for resolved light/dark mode specifically) — genuine runtime dependencies, not comments or dead imports. `useImageVariant.ts` is used *only* by 5 of these exact 7 files (`Login`/`Register`/`ForgotPasswordView`/`ResetPasswordView`/`NotFound`) — fully in-scope for this same implementation pass, not a separate concern. **`useLayoutInit.ts` is not** — it's called from `@layouts/LayoutWrapper.tsx` (the **dashboard** route group's layout) *and* `@layouts/BlankLayout.tsx` (blank-layout-pages' layout), confirmed via both files' actual call sites, not assumed from the name. This means **`ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline` cannot come off the dashboard route group either**, purely because of this one hook — a dependency entirely outside the illustration-panel problem's scope, on a route group Phase 1-3 already fully migrated off MUI otherwise.
- **`libs/theme/ModeChanger.tsx` also calls `useColorScheme()`/`setMode()`** for the same reason `useLayoutInit.ts`'s own comment states outright: "We need to change the mode in settings context to apply the mode change to MUI components." Once no MUI components render anywhere (after this design's changes + `Register`'s `Grid` + the dead-file cleanup below), that sentence becomes false, and the call becomes dead weight — a deletion, not a redesign. `ModeChanger.tsx` already computes everything it needs (`resolvedMode`) from `settings.mode` + `useMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` (from `react-use`, not MUI) *before* ever touching `useColorScheme()` — the MUI call is genuinely severable, not entangled with the real logic.
- **Recommended fix, not yet implemented**: extract a tiny `useResolvedMode()` hook (`settings.mode` + the same `useMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` fallback `ModeChanger.tsx` already does — not new logic, just de-duplicated) for `useImageVariant.ts` to call instead of `useColorScheme()`. `useLayoutInit.ts` and `ModeChanger.tsx` each keep their non-MUI responsibilities (cookie write, `data-theme` attribute write) and simply drop the `useColorScheme()` import and `setMode()` call — both become pure deletions once verified nothing else needs `setMode()`'s effect, which requires the *last* MUI component gone first (this design's changes, `Register`'s `Grid`, and the dead-file cleanup below) — so this is correctly the *final* step of the removal, not something to do mid-way.
- **`@core/components/mui/TextField.tsx` is now fully dead — zero importers anywhere.** Phase 0 (2026-09-16) confirmed it as the one live wrapper of the four `@core/components/mui/*` files, via its two then-real consumers, `CreateOrgDialog.tsx` and `FeedbackButton.tsx`. Both were migrated off it since (Phase 2 and Phase 3 part 2 respectively) — grepped fresh, confirmed zero references anywhere in the app today. Should be deleted outright (not archived — it's a 1:1 superseded wrapper with a live VhyxUI replacement already used everywhere, same judgment as Phase 0's other outright-deleted trivial files), independently of the rest of this design; added to `backlog.md` as its own small item since it needs no design work, just deletion + a gate re-run.
- **Everything else in the 60-file list is accounted for**: `@core/components/custom-inputs/types.ts` (type-only, tied to `libs/theme/types.ts`'s MUI theme-augmentation declarations — dies naturally when the theme tree does), `libs/components/Confirmation.tsx` and `app/[locale]/(blank-layout-pages)/verify-email/page.tsx` (both matched on a comment mentioning "@mui/material" in prose, not a real import — false positives in the grep, verified by reading each directly), `libs/components/VhyxUIToastRegion.tsx` (matched on the word "ThemeProvider" inside a comment about `CustomThemeProvider`, also a false positive).

**Answer to Part 3's question**: No — solving the illustration-panel problem alone does not fully unlock `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline` removal. Three more things are needed, all now identified and scoped: `Register.tsx`'s `Grid` (trivial, same session), `useImageVariant.ts`'s `useColorScheme()` call (the `useResolvedMode()` extraction, same session, in-scope since its only callers are these same 7 files), and `useLayoutInit.ts`/`ModeChanger.tsx`'s `useColorScheme()`/`setMode()` calls (only removable *after* every other MUI usage — including the dashboard route group's, already clear — is gone; this is correctly the last step, not a blocker to starting). The dead `TextField.tsx` wrapper is a fourth, unrelated, independently-actionable deletion.

**Recommended sequencing for the implementation session**: (1) the trivial `VerifyEmailView`/`VerifyEmailSentView` conversions and the dead `TextField.tsx` deletion — zero design risk, do first; (2) `Register.tsx`'s `Grid` → plain grid; (3) the new `useBreakpointDown` hook + `AuthIllustrationPanel`/mask-image component + CSS Module, applied to `Login`/`Register`/`ForgotPasswordView`/`ResetPasswordView`/`NotFound`; (4) verify zero remaining `@mui` component imports (not just in these 7 files — a fresh full-tree grep) before touching step 5; (5) only then, `useImageVariant.ts`'s `useResolvedMode()` swap and `useLayoutInit.ts`/`ModeChanger.tsx`'s `useColorScheme()`/`setMode()` removal, followed by actually deleting `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline`/`Providers.tsx`'s MUI wiring and the entire `@core/theme`/`libs/theme` construction tree. Functional check should specifically re-verify dark/light mode switching still works correctly end-to-end (via `data-theme`) after step 5, since that's the one step touching genuinely shared, always-active runtime logic rather than a single route group's static markup.
**Status:** active — investigation and design only, nothing implemented this session. This entry is the reference the implementation session should execute from directly, same role Phase 3 part 1's plan played for part 2. `backlog.md`'s Phase 4 item updated to point here; a new item added for the dead `TextField.tsx` wrapper.

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 4 part 2a: the auth illustration panel migrated off MUI's theme, executed directly from the Phase 4 part 1 design

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed), Claude Code (implementation)
**Context:** Phase 4 part 2a — execute the illustration-panel design from the "Phase 4 part 1" entry above directly, with no re-investigation. Explicitly scoped to exclude `Register.tsx`'s MUI `Grid` and any `ThemeProvider`/`useColorScheme()`/`useLayoutInit.ts`/`ModeChanger.tsx`/`useImageVariant.ts` work — both are separate, already-identified blockers with their own follow-up, not part of "the illustration panel."
**Built exactly per the design, no deviations**:
- `src/@core/hooks/useBreakpointDown.ts` — `window.matchMedia`, SSR-safe `false` default, re-evaluates on `change` events.
- `src/views/auth/AuthMaskImage.tsx` — the mask-image piece (hidden-below-900px gate via the new hook, `rtl:scale-x-[-1]`), reused by both `AuthIllustrationPanel` and `NotFound.tsx` directly (the design left the naming/file-split as a non-load-bearing call — a sibling file was the simpler choice, no reason to nest it under `AuthIllustrationPanel`'s own namespace).
- `src/views/auth/AuthIllustrationPanel.tsx` + `.module.css` — the shared left-panel wrapper, with `characterMaxHeight`/`maskMaxHeight` props (defaulting to `680`/`355`, `Register.tsx` passing `600`/`345`) and a CSS custom property (`--character-max-h`) feeding the module's `@media`-scoped overrides, exactly as designed.
- `Login.tsx`, `Register.tsx`, `ForgotPasswordView.tsx`, `ResetPasswordView.tsx` — `useTheme()`/`useMediaQuery`/the two `styled('img')` components all removed, replaced with `<AuthIllustrationPanel characterSrc=... characterAlt=... maskSrc=... bordered={settings.skin === 'bordered'} />` (`Register.tsx` additionally passing `characterMaxHeight={600} maskMaxHeight={345}`). `classnames` import dropped from all four where it had no other use (only `Login.tsx`/`Register.tsx`/`ForgotPasswordView.tsx`/`ResetPasswordView.tsx` — none had a second consumer of `classnames` after the illustration block moved out).
- `NotFound.tsx` — `useTheme()`/`useMediaQuery`/its `MaskImg` `styled()` component removed, replaced with `<AuthMaskImage src={miscBackground} />` (defaults match exactly — its original `MaskImg` was always `maxBlockSize: 355`, the same as `AuthMaskImage`'s default, no override needed).
- `VerifyEmailView.tsx` / `VerifyEmailSentView.tsx` — the trivial fix, exactly as the design called for: `styled('img')({maxWidth: 320, width: '100%', marginBottom: 24|32})` → plain `<img className='max-is-[320px] is-full mbe-6|mbe-8' />` (`theme.spacing`'s now-confirmed 1:1 mapping to Tailwind's own scale: `24px` = `mbe-6`, `32px` = `mbe-8`), identical in shape to Phase 1's `AcceptInvitationView.tsx` fix.
- `Register.tsx`'s MUI `Grid` — confirmed untouched; only its import comment was updated to point at this entry instead of claiming it's "out of scope for component migration" for the illustration reason (which no longer applies — the real reason now is "separate blocker, its own follow-up").
**Server/Client boundary**: no new risk — all seven files already carried their own `'use client'` before this session, `AuthIllustrationPanel.tsx`/`AuthMaskImage.tsx` both declare their own `'use client'` too (required since they call `useBreakpointDown`), and neither is ever imported from a Server Component directly (`NotFound.tsx`'s own `'use client'` status confirmed unchanged). `pnpm build` passed clean on the first attempt.
**Functional check**: no Chrome extension available (fifth session in a row) — real-backend + curl verification, with the flag the brief specifically asked for made explicit here rather than glossed over: **the actual responsive behavior at different viewport widths is inherently unverifiable via curl** — a curl request has no viewport, so nothing in this session's verification can confirm the character illustration actually shrinks at 1536px/1200px or that the mask image actually stops rendering below 900px in a real browser. What curl *can* and did confirm: started a fresh `apps/api` (local dev DB) and `apps/web` on port 4177 (port 4000 still held by the same unrelated project, untouched); every one of the 7 converted pages (`login`, `register`, `forgot-password`, `reset-password?token=...`, the 404 route, `verify-email`, `verify-email-sent`) returned a clean `200` with zero server-side exceptions and zero Turbopack compile warnings across the whole dev server log. Inspected the raw rendered HTML directly (not just the status code) for each split-layout page and confirmed the exact expected markup: `<img class="AuthIllustrationPanel-module__STjr7q__character z-[2] bs-auto max-is-full m-12" style="--character-max-h:680px">` for `Login`/`ForgotPasswordView`/`ResetPasswordView`, the same with `--character-max-h:600px` for `Register.tsx` (proving the prop override actually reaches the DOM, not just compiles), and `<img alt="mask" class="bs-auto is-full absolute block-end-0 z-[-1] rtl:scale-x-[-1]" style="max-block-size:355|345px">` on every page including `NotFound` — confirming the CSS Module class, the Tailwind utility classes, the inline custom property, and the static `rtl:` class are all present exactly as designed. Exercised three real mutations against the actual backend: `POST /auth/login` with real credentials (`200`, real tokens returned — the same call `Login.tsx`'s `onSubmit` makes, unchanged by this session), `POST /auth/forgot-password` (`200`, real anti-enumeration response), and `POST /auth/register` with a real throwaway account (`201`, created and then deleted via direct SQL to leave no residue) — confirming none of the untouched form/submission logic broke as a side effect of the illustration-panel restyle. `reset-password?token=...` was checked for the token-present code path (renders the form, not the "Invalid reset link" alert) rather than a full real-token redemption, since `passwordService.resetPassword`'s own logic is unchanged code, out of this session's scope.
**Result**: `@mui` surface: 60 files/74 lines (Phase 3 part 2's end state, re-confirmed unchanged at session start) → **54 files/62 lines**. `views/auth/` and `views/pages/NotFound.tsx` now import `@mui` only via `Register.tsx`'s `Grid` — the sole remaining touchpoint in the whole illustration-panel problem's original file set. `typecheck`/`build`/`test` all pass clean: 19 routes (unchanged), `apps/web` 7 files/23 tests (unchanged), root suite 19 files/99 tests (unchanged). `eslint`: 249 problems, unchanged from Phase 3 part 2's baseline — zero new issues in any of the 9 touched/new files.
**Status:** active. Committed as 2 commits (the hook + shared components + CSS Module + the 5 illustration-panel conversions; the 2 trivial `VerifyEmailView`/`VerifyEmailSentView` fixes, kept separate since they're a genuinely different kind of change). **Not done, deliberately**: `Register.tsx`'s `Grid`, and `useImageVariant.ts`/`useLayoutInit.ts`/`ModeChanger.tsx`'s `useColorScheme()`/`setMode()` calls — both explicitly out of scope this session, both still required before `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline` can actually be removed (see the "Phase 4 part 1" entry's Part 3 finding, unchanged). `backlog.md`'s Phase 4 item split: the illustration-panel design is now fully executed and removed from the open list; a new item added recording the `Grid`/`useColorScheme()` blockers explicitly, not folded into "Phase 4 complete" since Phase 4 as originally scoped (the illustration panel) is done but the broader `ThemeProvider` removal is not.

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 4 part 2b: design for the two remaining ThemeProvider blockers, plus two genuinely new dependencies found on the exhaustive final check

**Decided by:** Claude Code (investigation/design only — no implementation this session, per the brief)
**Context:** Phase 4 part 2b — investigate and design fixes for the two blockers Phase 4 part 1 identified (`Register.tsx`'s MUI `Grid`, and `useImageVariant.ts`/`useLayoutInit.ts`/`ModeChanger.tsx`'s real `useColorScheme()` calls), then do one final, genuinely exhaustive grep of the whole `apps/web/src` tree — not trusting the running file/import-count tally from Phases 0-4a — for anything else still tying the app to `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline`/`useTheme()`/`useColorScheme()`. Found two real, previously-unlisted items on that final check.

**PART 1 — `Register.tsx`'s `Grid`:**

Read fresh: `<Grid container spacing={2}><Grid size={6}><TextField label='First name' autoFocus .../></Grid><Grid size={6}><TextField label='Last name' .../></Grid></Grid>` — two fields, a flat 50/50 split via MUI's new (v7-unified) `Grid`'s `size={6}` prop. **No responsive breakpoint behavior at all** — `size={6}` is a bare number applied at every viewport width, not a responsive object (`size={{xs: 12, sm: 6}}`) — so this was never actually doing anything MUI's plain CSS Grid utilities couldn't already do identically.

**An identical, already-shipped precedent exists in this exact app**: `views/profile/ProfileView.tsx` (already migrated) has the *same* firstName/lastName two-field row, already converted to `<div className='grid grid-cols-2 gap-4'><TextField label='First name' .../><TextField label='Last name' .../></div>` — no MUI `Grid` wrapper per field needed at all, `TextField`s go directly inside a plain CSS Grid div. **Recommended fix**: the identical pattern for `Register.tsx`, preserving `autoFocus` on the first field:
```tsx
<div className='grid grid-cols-2 gap-4'>
  <TextField label='First name' autoFocus {...form.register('firstName')} />
  <TextField label='Last name' {...form.register('lastName')} />
</div>
```
**One real, worth-flagging discrepancy, not silently resolved**: `theme.spacing(N)` in this app maps 1:1 to Tailwind's own `N`-suffixed utility (established in Phase 4 part 1) — `spacing={2}` would literally convert to `gap-2` (8px), not `gap-4` (16px). `ProfileView.tsx`'s already-shipped version uses `gap-4`; its original pre-migration MUI source is not recoverable from git history to confirm what spacing value it actually used (the whole `apps/web` tree was committed post-migration, Phase-0-era). **Recommendation: match `ProfileView.tsx`'s shipped `gap-4`** for visual consistency with the one other place in this app that has the exact same "two related fields side by side" UI idiom, rather than a literal, isolated `gap-2` conversion of `Register.tsx`'s own original value — but this is a genuine judgment call, not a fact, and the execution session should feel free to override it if there's a reason to prefer literal fidelity instead.

**PART 2 — the `useColorScheme()` dependency, investigated fully, not assumed:**

Read `useImageVariant.ts`, `useLayoutInit.ts`, and `libs/theme/ModeChanger.tsx` fully, plus (new this session) `libs/theme/index.tsx` (`CustomThemeProvider`, the actual `ThemeProvider`+`CssBaseline`+`AppRouterCacheProvider` wiring site) to understand the complete picture, not just the three flagged files in isolation.

- **`useImageVariant.ts`**: `const { mode: muiMode, systemMode: muiSystemMode } = useColorScheme()`, then `currentMode = muiMode === 'system' ? muiSystemMode : muiMode || mode` — MUI's `systemMode` is itself reactive to *live* OS-preference changes (MUI's CssVarsProvider sets up its own internal `matchMedia` listener for this — confirmed, that's the actual point of MUI's "system" mode support), so this hook currently *does* pick up a live OS toggle correctly, not just an explicit in-app settings change. Any replacement must preserve that reactivity, not just read `settings.mode` once.
- **`useLayoutInit.ts`**: confirmed the prior description is accurate — this drives the `colorPref` **cookie** (an SSR hint for the *next* page load) via a live `useMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` subscription, and separately calls MUI's `setMode()` "to apply the mode change to MUI components" (the file's own comment) whenever `settings.mode === 'system'`. The cookie write is **unconditional** (happens regardless of `settings.mode`), only the `setMode()` call is gated. Called from *both* `@layouts/LayoutWrapper.tsx` (dashboard) and `@layouts/BlankLayout.tsx` (blank-layout-pages) — re-confirmed via both files' actual call sites, exactly as Phase 4 part 1 found, not different this time.
- **`ModeChanger.tsx`**: confirmed the prior description's distinction holds — this reacts to *explicit* `settings.mode` changes (its `useEffect`'s dependency array is `[settings.mode]` only), computing `resolvedMode` and writing it to both MUI (`setMode()`) and VhyxUI (`document.documentElement.setAttribute('data-theme', resolvedMode)`). Mounted once, inside `libs/theme/index.tsx`'s `CustomThemeProvider`.

**A real, previously-undiscovered gap found by tracing the dependency arrays precisely, not just re-reading the comments**: `ModeChanger.tsx`'s `data-theme` write is gated on `[settings.mode]` only — it does **not** re-run when the OS preference changes live while `settings.mode === 'system'`, even though `isDark` (from its own `useMedia` call) *does* update reactively and *does* cause `ModeChanger` to re-render — the `useEffect`'s body just never re-executes on that re-render, since its dependency array didn't change. Grepped every `data-theme` write site in the app (`document.documentElement.setAttribute` and the initial SSR value) to be certain nothing else covers this: only two exist — the SSR-rendered `data-theme={systemMode}` in `app/layout.tsx` (a one-time initial value) and this one gated `ModeChanger.tsx` effect. **This means: a live OS dark-mode toggle while `settings.mode === 'system'` has never updated VhyxUI's `data-theme` attribute** — only MUI's `setMode()` (via `useLayoutInit.ts`'s *separate* effect) has ever caught that case, for MUI components only. This is a real, currently-live bug, independent of the MUI-removal work itself — it just happens to be fixed as a natural byproduct of this design, since `ModeChanger.tsx` needs touching anyway to drop its `useColorScheme()` call.

**Does `data-theme` alone (without `useColorScheme()`) give everything needed? Yes, once the dependency-array gap above is also closed.** The one thing MUI's `useColorScheme()` provided that a bare `data-theme` attribute read does not is the *live-reactivity* to OS-preference changes — but that reactivity was never coming from `useColorScheme()`'s own internals being special; it was piggybacking on `useMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')`, a plain, already-used, non-MUI `react-use` hook. **Reuse, don't reinvent**: per the brief's own question, `useBreakpointDown`'s *technique* (hand-rolled `window.matchMedia`) is not the right thing to adapt here — it was only hand-rolled because no existing hook covered arbitrary pixel breakpoints. `react-use`'s `useMedia` already fully covers the `prefers-color-scheme` query and is the app's own already-established, three-times-used pattern for exactly this (`useLayoutInit.ts`, `ModeChanger.tsx`, and `libs/theme/index.tsx`'s `CustomThemeProvider` all already call it identically) — the fix should wrap `useMedia`, not `window.matchMedia` directly.

**Proposed design — a new `useResolvedMode()` hook**, `src/@core/hooks/useResolvedMode.ts`:
```ts
import { useMedia } from 'react-use'

import type { SystemMode } from '@core/types'
import { useSettings } from './useSettings'

export function useResolvedMode(fallback: SystemMode = 'light'): SystemMode {
  const { settings } = useSettings()
  const isDark = useMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)', fallback === 'dark')

  if (settings.mode === 'system') return isDark ? 'dark' : 'light'

  return settings.mode
}
```
- **`useImageVariant.ts`**: drop `useColorScheme()`, call `useResolvedMode(mode === 'dark' ? 'dark' : 'light')` instead (the `mode: Mode` param it already receives is always pre-resolved to `SystemMode` by every real caller — all seven view files type it `{ mode: SystemMode }` — so it's the correct SSR-safe fallback, same role `systemMode`/`colorSchemeFallback` already play in `useLayoutInit.ts`/`ModeChanger.tsx`/`CustomThemeProvider`). Keep the same narrow `useMemo` dependency array + `eslint-disable` shape the original used (`[resolvedMode]` replacing `[mode, muiMode, muiSystemMode]`) — a faithful restyle, not a rewrite of the memoization strategy.
- **`useLayoutInit.ts`**: drop the `useColorScheme()` import and the `if (settings.mode === 'system') setMode(appMode)` block entirely. **Once that block is gone, `useSettings()`/`settings` becomes fully unused in this file** — the cookie write was already unconditional, never gated on `settings.mode` — so the `useSettings` import should be removed too, not just the MUI one. The hook's whole remaining job becomes "write the OS-preference-derived `colorPref` cookie on live change," nothing else.
- **`ModeChanger.tsx`**: drop `useColorScheme()`/`setMode()`, keep the `data-theme` write, and **fix the dependency array to `[settings.mode, isDark]`** (closing the gap found above) — this single change makes `ModeChanger.tsx` alone sufficient to catch both trigger types (explicit settings change *and* live OS-preference change) that today require both `ModeChanger.tsx` and `useLayoutInit.ts` (for MUI only) to cover between them.
**Not addressed, deliberately out of scope**: `useLayoutInit.ts`'s cookie write being unconditional (not gated on `settings.mode === 'system'`) looks like it *might* let a live OS-preference change overwrite an explicitly-chosen light/dark cookie value ahead of the next SSR pass — but this is a pre-existing characteristic entirely unrelated to `useColorScheme()`, not something this design should silently change while "just" removing an MUI import. Noted here, not fixed, not filed as a certainty (would need to be confirmed as an actual bug via a real test before touching it) — worth a look in a dedicated session if anyone notices dark-mode-preference "flickering" back to system behavior.
**Where `ModeChanger` gets mounted from once `libs/theme/index.tsx`'s `CustomThemeProvider` is itself deleted** is explicitly left as an open call for the execution session — it depends on exactly how `Providers.tsx` gets restructured once it stops rendering `<ThemeProvider>`, which is real implementation work outside "design the two blockers," the scope this session was given.

**PART 3 — the exhaustive final check, not trusting the running tally:**

Grepped `apps/web/src` (excluding `archived/`) fresh, separately, for `ThemeProvider`, `CssBaseline`, `useTheme(`, and `useColorScheme(` as literal strings — not just re-deriving from the `@mui`-file-count tally every prior phase tracked.

- **`ThemeProvider`**: 4 files. `(dashboard)/layout.tsx` (comment only, re-verified), `libs/components/VhyxUIToastRegion.tsx` (comment only, re-verified — mentions "CustomThemeProvider" in prose), `libs/theme/index.tsx` (the real thing — `CustomThemeProvider` wraps MUI's actual `ThemeProvider`), `libs/components/Providers.tsx` (the real consumer — `import ThemeProvider from '@/libs/theme'` then `<ThemeProvider direction={...} systemMode={...}>{children}</ThemeProvider>`, i.e. `libs/theme/index.tsx`'s default export renamed on import). **Only two real files** (`libs/theme/index.tsx`, `Providers.tsx`) — both already known, both are the actual wiring that a final removal session deletes/rewrites, not new dependents.
- **`CssBaseline`**: 4 files, all previously-known and re-verified — `app/layout.tsx` (comment), `(public-pages)/layout.tsx` (comment), `(dashboard)/layout.tsx` (comment), `libs/theme/index.tsx` (real, `<CssBaseline />` rendered inside `CustomThemeProvider`).
- **`useTheme(`**: 1 file — `views/auth/AuthIllustrationPanel.tsx`, and it's a **comment** referencing the old, now-removed pattern ("each of those four pages duplicated on its own MUI `styled()`/`useTheme()`") — not a real call. Zero real `useTheme()` calls remain anywhere in the app after Phase 4 part 2a.
- **`useColorScheme(`**: exactly the 3 files Part 2 above already covers — `useImageVariant.ts`, `useLayoutInit.ts`, `ModeChanger.tsx`. No fourth caller found.

**Two genuinely new, previously-unlisted findings from broadening the check beyond these four exact strings**:

1. **`app/layout.tsx` — a real, live `InitColorSchemeScript` from `@mui/material`, not previously called out by name in any prior phase's file-by-file breakdown.** `import InitColorSchemeScript from '@mui/material/InitColorSchemeScript'`, rendered as `<InitColorSchemeScript attribute='data' defaultMode={systemMode} />` inside the **root** layout — mounted on every single route in the app, MUI or not. This is MUI's CssVarsProvider flash-of-wrong-theme prevention script (an inline `<script>` that sets a `data-*` attribute before hydration, matching `libs/theme/index.tsx`'s own `cssVariables: { colorSchemeSelector: 'data' }` config). **Confirmed unnecessary once `ThemeProvider` goes**: `data-theme={systemMode}` (VhyxUI's own attribute) is *already* set directly in the same SSR-rendered `<html>` tag, one line above — a script isn't needed for VhyxUI's flash-prevention the way MUI's architecture needed one, since this app has full control over the root HTML output already. **Fix: delete the import and the JSX line entirely, no replacement needed** — but only in the same final step that deletes `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline`/`libs/theme/index.tsx`, not before (this session doesn't implement it, just records it as a required part of that final step so it isn't missed).
2. **`libs/layout/shared/Logo.tsx` uses `@emotion/styled` directly — found by broadening the check to `@emotion` (Emotion is MUI's underlying styling engine, a real transitive dependency, but importable directly and invisible to every prior `@mui`-string-only grep).** `Logo.tsx` is rendered on every one of the seven auth pages plus the dashboard sidebar — about as widely-used as a component gets in this app. **Checked directly, not assumed from the surface pattern (the same discipline Phase 1 used for `styled('img')` calls)**: `LogoText`'s styled-template-literal callback destructures only `transitionDuration`/`isHovered`/`isCollapsed`/`isBreakpointReached` — all plain component props sourced from `useVerticalNav()` (a `@menu` hook), never `theme`. **Zero theme-context coupling — confirmed harmless, not a `ThemeProvider` blocker.** `@emotion/styled`'s `styled()` works completely standalone without any Provider (MUI's or a raw Emotion one) when the callback never reads an injected `theme` prop, which this one never does. A second, unrelated `@emotion/styled` hit (`@menu/types.ts`, `import type { CSSObject }`) is a type-only import with zero runtime footprint, same non-issue. **Not a blocker, not fixed, noted for completeness**: whether to eventually replace `Logo.tsx`'s `@emotion/styled` usage (e.g. with inline styles or a CSS Module using data attributes for the conditional collapse/hover states) is a separate, lower-priority dependency-hygiene question — about fully retiring the `@emotion`/`@mui` *packages* from `package.json`, not about removing `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline`, which don't need this touched at all.

**Answer to Part 3's question, exhaustively**: no other file anywhere in `apps/web/src` imports `ThemeProvider`, `CssBaseline`, or calls `useTheme()`/`useColorScheme()` beyond what's already accounted for across Phases 0-4 and this session's Part 2. The **complete, final list of everything standing between the current state and `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline` actually coming off** is now: (1) `Register.tsx`'s `Grid` (Part 1, designed above); (2) `useImageVariant.ts`/`useLayoutInit.ts`/`ModeChanger.tsx`'s `useColorScheme()`/`setMode()` calls (Part 2, designed above, including the `data-theme` live-sync bug fix); (3) `app/layout.tsx`'s `InitColorSchemeScript` (newly found this session, delete-only, same final step); (4) the actual deletion of `libs/theme/index.tsx`'s `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline`/`AppRouterCacheProvider` wiring and `Providers.tsx`'s import of it, plus the entire `@core/theme`/`libs/theme` construction tree (~44 files) that only exists to feed it; (5) the already-flagged, fully-dead `@core/components/mui/TextField.tsx` (independently deletable any time, not gated on the rest). `Logo.tsx`'s `@emotion/styled` usage and `@core/components/custom-inputs/types.ts`'s type-only MUI import are both confirmed non-blockers, noted for completeness, not on this list.
**Status:** active — investigation and design only, nothing implemented this session. This entry is the reference the next (execution) session should implement directly, same role Phase 4 part 1's entry played for part 2a. `backlog.md`'s combined `Grid`/`useColorScheme()` item updated to point here with the concrete designs; new items added for `InitColorSchemeScript` and the confirmed-harmless `Logo.tsx`/`@emotion` finding (informational, not an action item).
