### 2026-09-16 — Phase 4 part 3 (final): ThemeProvider/CssBaseline removed entirely — the MUI/Vuexy removal plan is complete

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed), Claude Code (implementation)
**Context:** Phase 4 part 3 — execute the complete design from "Phase 4 part 2b" directly (no re-investigation), then remove `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline` entirely once every blocker is cleared. This is the final session of the whole MUI/Vuexy removal plan that began with the original gap-analysis audit.

**Executed exactly per the design, with one small necessary correction found while compiling:**
- **`Register.tsx`'s `Grid`** → `<div className='grid grid-cols-2 gap-4'>`, matching `ProfileView.tsx`'s shipped precedent exactly (confirmed fresh: `ProfileView.tsx` line 104 is literally `grid grid-cols-2 gap-4` today). `autoFocus` preserved on the first field.
- **`useResolvedMode.ts`** built exactly as designed, with one real fix the design's own code sample needed: `settings.mode`'s actual type is `Mode | undefined` (`useSettings.tsx`'s context declares `mode?: Mode`), not the plain `Mode` the design assumed — `tsc` caught this immediately (`Type '"light" | "dark" | undefined' is not assignable to type 'SystemMode'`). Fixed by adding the `!settings.mode ||` guard, matching `ModeChanger.tsx`'s own existing `if (settings.mode)` pattern for the same optionality. A small, expected "verify the design once you actually compile it" catch, not a design flaw in the reasoning — just an untested code sample.
- **`useImageVariant.ts`/`useLayoutInit.ts`/`ModeChanger.tsx`** fixed exactly as designed: `useImageVariant.ts` calls `useResolvedMode(mode === 'dark' ? 'dark' : 'light')` instead of `useColorScheme()`, keeping the same narrow `useMemo` deps + `eslint-disable` shape. `useLayoutInit.ts` drops the MUI block *and* its now-fully-unused `useSettings` import (confirmed: the cookie write was already unconditional, so nothing else in the file needed `settings`). `ModeChanger.tsx` drops `useColorScheme()`/`setMode()`, keeps the `data-theme` write, and fixes the dependency array to `[settings.mode, isDark]` — closing the live-OS-preference-change `data-theme` sync gap found during the design session, as a direct byproduct of the same edit. `ModeChanger.tsx` also gained its own `'use client'` directive, needed because it's now rendered directly from `Providers.tsx` (a Server Component) rather than from within the `libs/theme/index.tsx` wrapper being deleted.
- **`app/layout.tsx`**: deleted the `InitColorSchemeScript` import and its JSX line entirely, no replacement — `data-theme={systemMode}` on the same `<html>` tag already covers VhyxUI's own SSR flash-prevention.
- **`Logo.tsx`**: confirmed fresh, no change needed — still only reads `theme`-free component props in its `@emotion/styled` callback.
- **Re-ran the exhaustive `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline`/`useTheme(`/`useColorScheme(`/`InitColorSchemeScript` grep against the tree after steps 1-4, before deleting anything**, exactly as instructed: `ThemeProvider` matched 5 files (4 already-known comments + `libs/theme/index.tsx`/`Providers.tsx`'s real wiring, plus a harmless new self-referential comment in `useResolvedMode.ts` describing what it replaces — not a real dependency); `CssBaseline` matched the same 4 known files, all comments except `libs/theme/index.tsx`; `useTheme(` matched only the known `AuthIllustrationPanel.tsx` comment; `useColorScheme(` matched only `useResolvedMode.ts`'s own explanatory comment (all three *real* calls were confirmed gone). `InitColorSchemeScript`: zero matches, confirmed deleted. **Nothing new, nothing missed** — safe to proceed.
- **Deleted `Providers.tsx`'s `ThemeProvider` import**, replacing it with a direct `<ModeChanger systemMode={systemMode} />` render alongside `<ClientProviders>{children}</ClientProviders>`. `direction` stays in the `Props` type (its three call sites all still pass it) but is no longer destructured/used internally — it only ever fed the now-deleted `AppRouterCacheProvider`'s RTL stylis plugin; touching the three call sites themselves was judged out of scope (they're not broken, just now pass an inert prop).
- **Deleted the entire `@core/theme/` directory (43 files)** and `libs/theme/index.tsx`/`mergedTheme.ts`/`types.ts`/`userTheme.ts` (`ModeChanger.tsx` is the one file that stays in `libs/theme/`, now the sole occupant). Re-confirmed before deleting that nothing outside this tree imports any of it (only `libs/theme/index.tsx`/`mergedTheme.ts` did, both also going).
- **A new, small orphaning found and handled while deleting**: `@core/components/custom-inputs/types.ts` had exactly one importer, `libs/theme/types.ts` — now gone. Archived to `apps/web/archived/src/@core/components/custom-inputs/types.ts`, joining the `Horizontal.tsx`/`Image.tsx`/`Vertical.tsx` trio already archived there since Phase 0 (same convention, same directory).
- **`@core/components/mui/TextField.tsx`** deleted per the brief, also archived to `apps/web/archived/src/@core/components/mui/TextField.tsx` — matching the same directory where `Avatar.tsx`/`Chip.tsx`/`IconButton.tsx`/etc. already live from Phase 0's archiving pass, not a fresh judgment call.

**A real gap in prior sessions' "zero importers" checks, found via the lint run (see below), corrected in understanding but not in scope for this session**: every prior "confirmed zero importers" grep for `CustomTextField`/`components/mui/TextField` (Phase 0, Phase 3 part 1, this design entry) was scoped to `--include="*.tsx" --include="*.ts"`, which silently excluded `.jsx` files. Four already-known-dead `.jsx` files under `libs/components/` (`CustomCheckboxAutocomplete.jsx`, `SoloCheckboxCreateOption.jsx`, `CustomDatePicker.jsx`, `CustomControlledAutoComplete.jsx`) do reference `CustomTextField`/`components/mui/TextField` — but all four were **already** completely dead and broken before this session (each also has multiple *other* unresolved imports pre-dating this work, e.g. `@/utils/fetchData`, deleted back in a 2026-09-15 session — confirmed via a fresh grep that none of these four files has any real importer anywhere). Deleting `TextField.tsx` did not break anything that was ever working; it just added one more already-expected `import/no-unresolved` error to files that were already unreachable and already broken. Not fixed this session (these four files are outside "remove ThemeProvider/CssBaseline" — they're part of the same large, un-triaged dead-`.jsx`-file pile flagged since Phase 0/1, deliberately not promoted to a discrete backlog item then, and not now either, for the same reason).

**Functional check**: no Chrome extension available (sixth session in a row) — stated plainly, per the brief's own instruction, rather than working around it. Started a fresh `apps/api` (local dev DB) and `apps/web` on port 4177 (port 4000 still held by the same unrelated project, untouched). Curled every route across both route groups after the full removal: `login`, `register`, `forgot-password`, `reset-password?token=...`, the 404 route, `verify-email`, `verify-email-sent`, `pricing`, `docs`, `support` (public-pages group), `dashboard`, `profile`, `invitations/accept` (dashboard group, `is_authenticated=1` cookie) — all `200`, zero server exceptions, zero Turbopack warnings in the dev log. Confirmed `Register.tsx`'s new grid renders correctly in the raw HTML (`<div class="grid grid-cols-2 gap-4">` with the `firstName` input carrying `autofocus`). **Confirmed `Logo.tsx`'s Emotion styling survives the `AppRouterCacheProvider` removal correctly, empirically, not just reasoned**: the SSR-rendered `login` page HTML contains both the Emotion-generated class (`css-1wk9fr7`) on the `LogoText` element *and* the matching `<style data-emotion="css 1wk9fr7">` tag in the same response — Emotion's default cache is injecting styles correctly server-side without the MUI-specific Next.js cache provider. **Confirmed the runtime MUI theme output is actually gone, not just the imports**: the same page HTML that used to contain thousands of `--mui-palette-*`/`--mui-shape-*`/`--mui-shadows-*` CSS custom-property declarations (from `createTheme()`'s inline style block) now contains zero. Real mutations exercised against the actual backend: `POST /auth/login` with real credentials (`200`) and a real `POST /auth/register` (`201`, cleaned up via SQL afterward) — both confirming the untouched form/submission logic in `Login.tsx`/`Register.tsx` still works correctly after their surrounding illustration/grid restyles.
**What could not be verified, stated plainly rather than worked around**: the live OS-dark-mode-preference-change reactivity fix (`ModeChanger.tsx`'s `[settings.mode, isDark]` dependency-array fix) is the one thing in this whole phase that's genuinely about live, dynamic browser behavior — toggling the OS's `prefers-color-scheme` while the app is open on `settings.mode === 'system'` — and no browser tool of any kind was available this session (not just the Claude-in-Chrome extension; no other browser automation was accessible either) to simulate a live `prefers-color-scheme` change via devtools' override or otherwise. Relied entirely on direct code-level verification: the dependency-array change is syntactically and logically sound (confirmed by re-reading it against the exact bug description from the design session), and `isDark`'s reactivity to live `matchMedia` changes is `react-use`'s own well-established, already-proven-working behavior in this exact file (the *same* `useMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` call already existed here before this session's edit — only the effect's dependency array changed, not the reactivity mechanism itself). This is a real, disclosed verification gap, not silently assumed fine — worth a real browser check (with devtools' rendering-emulation `prefers-color-scheme` override) once the Chrome extension is available, as part of whatever batched visual pass eventually covers this whole migration.

**Result, corrected after a fuller final `@mui` grep than the one used mid-session**: **zero real `@mui` imports remain in any live, reachable file in `apps/web/src`** — every route, hook, and component actually rendered by the app is fully off MUI. But the true full-tree count is **not** "two comments" as first drafted here; a wider grep across `.jsx`/`.js` (not just `.tsx`/`.ts` — the same blind spot already caught once this session for the `TextField.tsx` importer check, and evidently not generalized to this final count before first writing this entry) turns up **37 more files**, all `.jsx`/`.js`, all under `libs/ui/`, `libs/card-statistics/`, `libs/styles/`, and `libs/components/*.jsx`, that still contain real, uncommented `@mui`/`@mui/lab` imports. Every one of these 37 was checked for importers (by both filename and named-export grep, including dynamic `import()` and the app's only two path aliases, `@/*`/`@core/*`) and **all 37 are unreachable dead code** — no barrel/index file re-exports any of these directories, and their only cross-references are to each other within the same dead pile (e.g. `FileUploadDialog.jsx` → `DragAndDropComponent.jsx`, `VerticalTabsPanel.jsx` → `TabsPanel.jsx`). This is the same category of pre-existing dead-`.jsx` pile flagged earlier this session (the 4 files referencing `TextField.tsx`) and in earlier phases (Phase 0/1) — just a materially larger slice of it than any prior session had actually enumerated with an `@mui` grep. Not a regression from this session's work (none of these 37 files were touched, and none were ever reachable), but the earlier draft of this entry's "zero real imports, two comments" framing was inaccurate and is corrected here rather than left standing. Genuinely comment-only, confirmed by direct inspection: `(blank-layout-pages)/verify-email/page.tsx` and `libs/components/Confirmation.tsx`. `typecheck`/`build`/`test` all pass clean: 19 routes (unchanged), `apps/web` 7 files/23 tests (unchanged), root suite 19 files/99 tests (unchanged). `eslint`: 253 problems, **up 4 from the 249 baseline** — precisely explained above (the four already-dead `.jsx` files' newly-unresolvable `TextField` import), not a regression in anything that was ever live or working, and consistent with this project's standing "large pre-existing `.jsx` pile, out of scope" framing since Phase 0. **A new backlog item was added** for a future session to actually enumerate and delete/archive this 37-file dead `.jsx`/`.js` pile (`libs/ui/`, `libs/card-statistics/`, `libs/styles/`, most of `libs/components/*.jsx`) — it was out of scope for "remove ThemeProvider/CssBaseline" (none of it depends on or blocks that removal), but it's now the single largest remaining concentration of raw `@mui` source text in the tree and worth a dedicated cleanup pass.
**Not done, deliberately, flagged for a future session**: removing the now-fully-unused `@mui/lab`/`@mui/material`/`@mui/material-nextjs`/`@mui/utils` packages from `package.json` (keeping `@emotion/styled`/`@emotion/react`/`@emotion/cache`, since `Logo.tsx` genuinely still needs `@emotion/styled`). This wasn't one of the six explicit execution steps and touches `package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml` — a meaningfully different risk profile than a pure source-code change (affects the whole pnpm workspace's lockfile, not just `apps/web`) — so it's recorded as the one remaining, low-risk, independently-actionable cleanup step rather than folded into this session without being asked.
**Status:** active — **the entire MUI/Vuexy removal plan (Phases 0-4) is now complete** for `apps/web`'s live component/theme surface. Committed as 3 commits (`Register.tsx`'s Grid; the `useResolvedMode()` hook + three-file fix; the actual `ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline` removal + tree deletion). `backlog.md`'s combined `ThemeProvider`/`Grid`/`TextField.tsx` items all resolved and removed; a new item added for the `@mui`/`@emotion` `package.json` cleanup, and another for the gap-2-vs-gap-4 spacing-unit-math discrepancy this session matched-not-resolved (per the brief's own instruction) as a small design-system consistency question for a later pass.

### 2026-09-16 — Phase 4 part 4: dead-.jsx-pile archiving + @mui package.json cleanup, mode-management investigation, chrome-visual.md checklist created

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed), Claude Code (implementation for archiving/package.json; investigation-only for mode-management)
**Context:** Three-part follow-up session after Phase 4 part 3 closed the MUI/Vuexy removal plan: (1) archive the 37-file dead `.jsx`/`.js` pile found during Phase 4 part 3 and remove now-unused `@mui/*` packages from `package.json`; (2) investigate (not yet implement) whether the mode/theme-sync code (`ModeChanger.tsx`, `useLayoutInit.ts`, `useImageVariant.ts`, `useResolvedMode.ts`, `useSettings`) has accumulated redundant logic now that `ThemeProvider` is gone; (3) create `.claude/chrome-visual.md`, a standing checklist of every visual-verification gap flagged since Phase 1.

**PART 1 — archiving, re-verified via precise import-path tracing, not bare grep:**

Bare-basename grep is unreliable for common words — `libs/ui/form.jsx` and `libs/ui/label.jsx` both showed dozens of "hits" via `grep -rl "form"` / `grep -rl "label"` that were entirely false positives (`form.register(...)`, `<label>` JSX, CSS class names, etc., in completely unrelated live files). Redid the check with precise import-statement matching (`from ['"][^'"]*/name['"]`, `require(...)`, `import(...)`) restricted to the specific relative/aliased path, which found **zero** real references to either file — confirming the bare-grep "lesson" the brief referenced was real and would have produced a false "still referenced" result. Ran the same precise check across all 37 files: found real importers only *within* the pile itself (`FileUploadDialog.jsx`→`DragAndDropComponent.jsx`, `VerticalTabsPanel.jsx`→`TabsPanel.jsx`, `StatsWithAreaChart.jsx`→`AppReactApexCharts.jsx` via `dynamic()`, `CustomDatePicker.jsx`→`AppReactDatepicker.jsx`, `DragAndDropComponent.jsx`/`ProjectDropZone.jsx`→`AppReactDropzone.js`) — a fully self-contained, disconnected subgraph with zero edges into any live file. Also confirmed no barrel/index file exists anywhere under `libs/` (would have been the classic blind spot) and no dynamic `import()` or path-alias reference from outside the pile. One important cross-check this surfaced: `libs/components/Confirmation.tsx` (flagged in Phase 4 part 3 as one of the two harmless-comment-only `@mui` matches) is genuinely **live** — `libs/table/type.ts` and `libs/table/RowAction.tsx` both really import it — correctly excluded from the archive list, not a contradiction, just confirms the precise-tracing method correctly distinguishes live from dead.

**What moved to `apps/web/archived/src/`** (35 files, directory structure preserved, matching the established convention — safe default is to keep the physical copy): the `libs/card-statistics/*` (5 files), `libs/styles/App*` (8 files, chart/calendar/datepicker/dropzone/rich-text/toastify vendor-glue), `libs/ui/*` (5 files — a part-shadcn/ui, part-MUI form-primitives kit; `form.jsx` in particular has an entire alternate implementation fully commented out at the top of the file, then a second, live-but-unreachable MUI-based implementation below it), and 22 of `libs/components/*.jsx` (custom autocompletes/date-pickers/dialogs/etc.). None of these are trivial wrappers — sizes range 29-487 lines, real Vuexy-template component logic, matching the "substantial, keep a copy" precedent rather than the "trivial one-liner, delete outright" precedent.

**Deleted outright, no archive copy** (judgment call, matching the Phase 0 precedent for trivial/fully-generic files): `libs/components/UtilityComponent.jsx` (8 lines, a bare `getAvatar()` MUI `Avatar` wrapper) and `libs/components/AddHeader.jsx` (14 lines, a bare `Typography` wrapper) — both too trivial to carry any future reference value even as an archived curiosity, same bar Phase 0 used for `DialogCloseButton.tsx`/`TablePageSize.tsx`.

**Backlog sweep**: grepped `backlog.md` and `decision.md` for any other "found dead, not yet archived" items from across the whole MUI removal arc — found none beyond what's handled here (the `TextField.tsx`/`custom-inputs/types.ts` items were already resolved in Phase 4 part 3).

**`@mui/*` package.json removal**: confirmed zero remaining `@mui` imports anywhere in `apps/web/src` (post-archiving — only the same two pre-existing comment-only files, `verify-email/page.tsx` and `Confirmation.tsx`, unchanged) before touching `package.json`. Removed `@mui/lab`/`@mui/material`/`@mui/material-nextjs`/`@mui/utils` (kept `@emotion/*` — still genuinely used by `Logo.tsx`).

**A real regression caught and fixed before considering this done**: running `pnpm install` after the `package.json` edit broke `pnpm typecheck` — `Type '...QueryClient' ... refers to a different member that cannot be accessed`, a duplicate-package-instance TS error. Root cause, traced fully: `@vhyx/api-kit` (consumed via `link:../../../vhyx-api-kit`, a separate sibling repo with its own independent `pnpm-lock.yaml`/node_modules) declares `@tanstack/react-query` as a peerDependency but also has its own `devDependency` on it, independently locked to `5.102.8` in its own repo. `apps/web`'s own specifier was `^5.90.19`, unlocked in the root lockfile until this session's `pnpm install` — which resolved it to `5.103.1`, diverging from `vhyx-api-kit`'s own `5.102.8` and producing two physically-distinct copies of the same package family, which TypeScript's private-class-field structural typing correctly rejects as incompatible. Fixed by pinning `apps/web`'s `@tanstack/react-query` to the exact version `5.102.8` (no caret) to force parity. Re-ran `pnpm install`, `typecheck` passed clean. This is a fragile fix (recorded in `backlog.md`) — the real fix belongs in how `vhyx-api-kit` resolves its own peer dependency, out of this session's scope.

**A scope question surfaced and resolved with the user's input, not silently decided**: the `pnpm install` needed for the `@mui` removal produced a ~5,700-line `pnpm-lock.yaml` diff — investigated before assuming this was acceptable. Traced the cause: `apps/web` had **no importer entry in the root `pnpm-lock.yaml` at all** before this session (confirmed via `git show HEAD:pnpm-lock.yaml | grep apps/web` — zero matches), a pre-existing gap predating this session entirely (apps/web was moved into the monorepo 2026-09-09; its dependency tree was apparently never locked into the shared root lockfile since). Any full `pnpm install` was always going to write apps/web's entire ~400-package transitive tree into the lockfile for the first time, regardless of the `@mui` removal — not scope creep from this session's own pending, unrelated `package.json` edits to root/`packages/agent`/`packages/sdk` (verified via an isolated test: stashing those files and re-running install still produced a near-identical-sized diff). Asked the user how to handle committing this given root/agent/sdk's pre-existing unrelated pending edits were also sitting in the same file; user chose to commit `apps/web/package.json` only and leave `pnpm-lock.yaml` (and the three unrelated pending `package.json` files) uncommitted, reviewed separately later. `pnpm-lock.yaml` on disk is fully up to date (both installs were actually run, `typecheck`/`build`/`test` all verified against the real, current `node_modules`) — only the *commit* is scoped narrowly, not the underlying dependency resolution. Recorded as its own `backlog.md` item so this isn't lost.

**Verification**: `typecheck` clean, `build` clean (19 routes, unchanged), `apps/web` test suite 7/23 unchanged, root suite 19/99 unchanged, `eslint` dropped from 253 → **36 problems** (the entire dead-`.jsx`-pile's lint noise, including the +4 delta from Phase 4 part 3's `TextField.tsx` deletion surfacing in 4 already-broken files, is now gone along with the archived files themselves — `archived/` is excluded from ESLint's `ignorePatterns`). Final `@mui` count: zero in `package.json`, zero real imports in `src/` (same two pre-existing harmless comments as before).

**Committed as 2 commits**: the 39-file archive/delete; the `package.json` `@mui` removal + `@tanstack/react-query` pin (with `pnpm-lock.yaml` deliberately excluded per the decision above).

**PART 2 — mode-management investigation (investigation + proposal only, NOT implemented, awaiting review):**

Read every file end to end: `useResolvedMode.ts`, `ModeChanger.tsx`, `useLayoutInit.ts`, `useImageVariant.ts`, `useSettings.tsx`/`settingsContext.tsx`, `serverHelpers.ts` (`getMode`/`getSystemMode`/`getServerMode`/`getSettingsFromCookie`), `Providers.tsx`, `app/layout.tsx`, `@layouts/BlankLayout.tsx`/`LayoutWrapper.tsx`, both route-group `layout.tsx` files, `ModeDropdown.tsx`, and every page calling a `serverHelpers` mode function.

**Finding 1 — `ModeChanger.tsx` duplicates `useResolvedMode()`'s exact logic instead of calling it.** Both compute the identical resolution (`!settings.mode || settings.mode === 'system' ? (isDark ? 'dark' : 'light') : settings.mode`), but `ModeChanger.tsx` still runs its own independent `useSettings()` + `useMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)', ...)` call rather than delegating to the hook built specifically to replace this pattern in Phase 4 part 3. Net effect: three independent, functionally-overlapping `matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` listeners are mounted simultaneously on every page (one inside `ModeChanger`, one inside whichever `useImageVariant`/`useResolvedMode` caller is active, one inside `useLayoutInit`). **Proposed fix**: `ModeChanger.tsx` calls `useResolvedMode(systemMode)` and writes whatever it returns to `data-theme`, deleting its own duplicate `useSettings()`/`useMedia()`/ternary. One disclosed behavior difference: the current `if (settings.mode) {...}` guard skips the `data-theme` write entirely when `settings.mode` is falsy (leaves a stale value); `useResolvedMode`'s existing `!settings.mode ||` branch instead always resolves a sane value from `isDark`. This is very likely a strict improvement (no stale value left behind in an edge case that may never actually occur in practice, since `SettingsProvider` always initializes `mode` to a real value), but it is a real, disclosed behavior change, not something to silently fold in.

**Finding 2 — `getServerMode()` and `getSystemMode()` in `serverHelpers.ts` are mathematically identical functions**, proven by tracing both branches (for `mode==='system'`, both return the `colorPref` cookie; otherwise both return `mode` directly) and confirmed concretely: `app/[locale]/[...not-found]/page.tsx` calls **both** in the same function body, computing the same value twice under two different names. `login/page.tsx` and `register/page.tsx` use `getServerMode()`; every other equivalent page (`forgot-password`, `reset-password`, `verify-email`, `verify-email-sent`, `invitations/accept`, both route-group layouts) uses `getSystemMode()` for the identical purpose, feeding the identical `mode`-shaped prop into their view components. **Proposed fix**: delete `getServerMode()` entirely, switch its 3 callers to `getSystemMode()`. Zero behavior change — proven identical, not just "probably" — pure dead-duplication removal.

**Finding 3 — a stale comment in `app/[locale]/(dashboard)/layout.tsx`** (its multi-line comment above the `Layout` component) describes pre-Phase-4-part-3 reality that's no longer true: it says `useLayoutInit` "live-syncs MUI's color mode" (MUI is fully gone), claims `ModeChanger.tsx` "only reacts to explicit `settings.mode` changes, not live OS-preference changes" (the exact bug Phase 4 part 3 fixed — `ModeChanger` now also reacts to live OS-preference changes), and states "CssBaseline/ThemeProvider (inside Providers) stays active" (removed entirely in Phase 4 part 3). Needs a factual rewrite regardless of whether any of the above logic changes — leaving it as-is actively misleads the next reader about why `useLayoutInit` and `ModeChanger` both still exist. (They do still serve a genuinely different purpose even after Finding 1's fix: `useLayoutInit`'s `colorPref` cookie is an SSR fallback consulted only on the *next* page load; `ModeChanger`'s `data-theme` write is the *live* client DOM attribute for the *current* page. That structural distinction is real and worth preserving in the corrected comment.)

**Considered and flagged as unconfirmed, deliberately not touched**: `useLayoutInit`'s `colorPref` cookie write is unconditional (fires on every `isDark` change regardless of `settings.mode`), even though `getSystemMode()` only ever reads that cookie when `mode==='system'` — meaning the write is simply inert/ignored, not wrong, when the user has picked an explicit light/dark mode. This was already flagged and deliberately deferred in the Phase 4 part 2b entry ("worth a look only if anyone notices dark-mode flickering") — recommend continuing to defer it; it's a separate, still-unconfirmed question, not "redundant logic that could now be one clean implementation," and folding it into this cleanup would expand scope beyond what was asked.

**Considered and recommended AGAINST**: fully unifying `useLayoutInit`'s cookie write and `ModeChanger`'s `data-theme` write into one hook/component to eliminate even the 2nd/3rd `matchMedia` listener. This would require either lifting `isDark` into a shared context/`useSyncExternalStore`-backed singleton (new architecture, more moving parts than exist today) or restructuring where `Providers`/`BlankLayout`/`LayoutWrapper` compose (they're mounted from genuinely different places in the tree today). A couple of cheap `matchMedia` listeners is not a real performance problem, and `react-use`'s `useMedia` is already this app's own established, proven-cheap pattern for this exact query — trading that for a shared-state architecture's own new coordination complexity is not clearly a win. **Recommendation: apply Finding 1 and Finding 2 only** (both zero-risk, provably-equivalent deletions of real duplication), fix Finding 3's stale comment, and leave `useLayoutInit` as its own separate concern.

**Status: awaiting review, nothing implemented.** Per the brief's explicit instruction, this investigation stops at a proposed design — Findings 1-3 should be implemented only after review/go-ahead in a follow-up session.

**PART 3 — `.claude/chrome-visual.md` created:**

Compiled by grepping `decision.md` (not reconstructed from memory) for every "Chrome extension unavailable"/"could not be verified"/"not visually confirmed" passage from Phase 1 onward (six consecutive sessions, zero browser-automation access in any of them) and extracting the specific, concrete visual/interactive claim each one left unverified. Organized as one checklist grouped by page/component, each item citing its originating decision.md entry. Chose **check-off-in-place over delete-when-resolved** (unlike `backlog.md`): this file is a verification audit trail for one large, now-complete migration, not an ambient list of ongoing dev annoyances — knowing *when* and *that* an item was actually checked has lasting value, and deleting the line on completion would erase that record. Covers: `AuthGuard`'s bootstrap spinner, `NotFound`/`AcceptInvitationView`/dashboard-redirect rendering, `ScrollToTop`'s button sizing (Phase 1); real Badge colors, Dialog/Drawer open-close, the `FeedbackContext` banner (Phase 2, six files); `NotificationBell`'s Popover/Tooltip/badge-overlay/per-type-icon-colors/full interaction loop (Phase 3 part 1); `FeedbackButton`'s FAB/Dialog/Chip-selector/per-type-colors/validation display/Collapse-replacement (Phase 3 part 2); scroll-to-top's abrupt show/hide (Phase 3 part 2); the illustration panel's actual responsive shrink/hide behavior at real viewport widths (Phase 4 part 2a, the one thing curl structurally cannot ever confirm); the OS-dark-mode live-sync fix (Phase 4 part 3, the specific bug this whole investigation arc chased); and `Register.tsx`'s new grid layout (Phase 4 part 3).

**Status:** active. `backlog.md`'s two resolved Phase 4 part 3 items (the dead-`.jsx`-pile, the `@mui` package.json removal) removed; two new items added (the pre-existing, unrelated `pnpm-lock.yaml`/root-`package.json` drift left uncommitted; the fragile `@tanstack/react-query` exact-version pin). `.claude/chrome-visual.md` is a new standing file, not yet referenced from `context.md` — worth a one-line pointer there in a future session if it becomes a live habit to consult.

### 2026-09-17 — Phase 4 part 5: mode-management proposal (Phase 4 part 4's Findings 1-3) implemented, nothing beyond

**Decided by:** Tanveer (directed — explicit go-ahead on exactly the three approved fixes, explicit instruction not to pursue the deeper unification that was recommended against), Claude Code (implementation)
**Context:** Phase 4 part 4's investigation proposed three zero-risk fixes and one thing to explicitly avoid. This session implements the three approved fixes only.

**Fix 1 — `ModeChanger.tsx` now calls `useResolvedMode(systemMode)`** instead of its own independent `useSettings()` + `useMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)', ...)` + ternary. The `useEffect` now depends on `[resolvedMode]` (a single derived string) instead of `[settings.mode, isDark]`, and no longer needs an `eslint-disable` comment for `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` — the new dependency array is already complete and honest. Confirmed the one disclosed behavior difference flagged in the investigation is exactly what landed: the old `if (settings.mode) {...}` guard skipped the `data-theme` write entirely when `settings.mode` was falsy; `useResolvedMode`'s existing `!settings.mode ||` branch now always resolves a value from `isDark` instead. No other behavior change.

**Re-verified the fix against Phase 4 part 3's exact original bug description before considering this done, as instructed**, since this touches the precise code that bug lived in: traced `useMedia`'s actual implementation (`node_modules/.pnpm/react-use@17.6.0/.../useMedia.js`) to confirm `window.matchMedia(query)` is called once per mount (inside `useEffect`, not in `getInitialState`, since `useResolvedMode` always passes a `defaultState`) and that its `change` listener drives a `setState` call — meaning `useResolvedMode`'s `isDark` is genuinely live-reactive, not a one-time read. Hand-traced all four scenarios: (1) `settings.mode==='system'`, OS starts light, OS toggles live to dark → `resolvedMode` recomputes from the new `isDark`, `data-theme` updates. (2) `settings.mode==='dark'` (explicit), OS toggles live → `resolvedMode` stays `'dark'` regardless (matches old behavior, which also ignored `isDark` for explicit modes). (3)/(4) mirror cases. No SSR/hydration risk from the `!settings.mode` branch's changed behavior: `SettingsProvider` always initializes `_settingsState.mode` to a real value before first client render, and the write only happens inside a post-mount `useEffect`, never affecting SSR HTML.

**Added `apps/web/src/libs/theme/ModeChanger.test.tsx`** (no prior test covered this code) — 5 tests: explicit-mode resolution ignoring OS preference; `'system'`-mode resolution from the initial OS preference on mount; **the critical regression test** — a live, simulated `matchMedia` `'change'` event while `settings.mode==='system'` updates `data-theme` (this is the exact Phase 4 part 3 bug, now exercised directly rather than only reasoned about); a live OS change while `settings.mode` is explicit does *not* affect `data-theme`; and a `window.matchMedia` call-count check confirming `ModeChanger` only ever creates one `(prefers-color-scheme: dark)` subscription (guards against a botched merge accidentally leaving two mechanisms active). jsdom has no real `matchMedia`, so the test stubs a controllable fake `MediaQueryList` (`vi.stubGlobal('matchMedia', ...)`) with a working `addEventListener`/`change`-listener registry, letting `fireChange()` simulate a live OS toggle. All 5 pass. One real lint catch during this: an unused `beforeEach` import and an unused typed-but-unused mock parameter (`_query`, needed only for `mock.calls`' TS element type) — fixed (removed the import; added a narrowly-scoped `eslint-disable-next-line` for the parameter, matching the same pattern `useLayoutInit.ts` already uses for an analogous unused-destructure case).

**Fix 2 — deleted `getServerMode()` from `serverHelpers.ts`**, switched its three callers (`login/page.tsx`, `register/page.tsx`, `[...not-found]/page.tsx`) to `getSystemMode()`. `[...not-found]/page.tsx` previously called *both* functions and used their (identical) results for two different props — now calls `getSystemMode()` once and reuses the single result for both `BlankLayout`'s `systemMode` and `NotFound`'s `mode`, a small additional cleanup beyond the mechanical rename since the two variables were never anything but the same value under two names. Confirmed zero remaining references to `getServerMode` anywhere in `apps/web/src` after the edit.

**Fix 3 — rewrote `(dashboard)/layout.tsx`'s stale comment.** Corrected: `useLayoutInit` no longer "live-syncs MUI's color mode" (MUI is gone; it now only maintains the `colorPref` SSR cookie); the comment's claimed distinction from `ModeChanger` ("only reacts to explicit settings.mode changes, not live OS-preference changes") was itself the *exact* bug Phase 4 part 3 fixed — restated the real, still-true distinction instead: `useLayoutInit`'s cookie is an SSR fallback for the *next* page load, `ModeChanger`'s `data-theme` write is the live DOM attribute for the *current* page, both now reacting to the same two trigger types; removed the "CssBaseline/ThemeProvider stays active" claim entirely (both were removed in Phase 4 part 3) and replaced it with what `Providers` actually wires today.

**Did not touch**: `useLayoutInit.ts`'s own separate `useMedia` subscription, any shared-context/`useSyncExternalStore` unification, or `useResolvedMode.ts`'s own comment (which still references the now-deleted `libs/theme/index.tsx`'s `CustomThemeProvider` as a historical justification for choosing `useMedia` — a small, separate staleness noticed but out of scope for "these three fixes," not folded in; worth a `backlog.md` line if it keeps nagging).

**Verification**: `typecheck` clean, `build` clean (19 routes, unchanged), `apps/web` test suite **8 files/28 tests** (7/23 baseline + the new file's 5 tests), root suite 19/99 unchanged, `eslint` **36 problems — unchanged from the Phase 4 part 4 baseline** (a transient +2 from the new test file's own unused-import/unused-param mistakes was caught and fixed before considering this done, not left in).

**Status:** active. Committed as a single commit — all three fixes plus the regression test are small and interdependent enough (the test specifically covers Fix 1) not to warrant separate commits. `backlog.md`: no items resolved or added by this session (the `useResolvedMode.ts` comment staleness noted above wasn't promoted to a backlog line — deliberately, matching this project's own standard for "small enough to just mention here" vs. "worth tracking").
